# Implementation Notes — Version 1

These notes add the technical decisions that the concept ([vscode-dev-environments.md](vscode-dev-environments.md)) leaves open. The concept is the source of the requirements. If a note and the concept differ, the concept wins, and the note must be corrected.

## 1. Scope

- Phase 1 and phase 2 of the concept (section 12), including Docker Compose configurations (`dockerComposeFile` and `service`, section 15).
- Not in scope: phase 3 (profile template, backup of unpushed work, `build.cacheFrom`, Podman).
- The verification tasks V-1 to V-12 are not done yet. Code that depends on an unverified assumption has a comment `// Assumption (V-n): …`, so that a later check can find it.

## 2. Technology

| Item | Decision |
|---|---|
| Language | TypeScript, `strict: true`, target ES2022. The type check resolves imports like the bundler (`module: preserve`, `moduleResolution: bundler`) and fails on an unused import, local, or private member (`noUnusedLocals`, plan step 11I, decision U9 of 2026-10-08; unused parameters stay allowed); esbuild writes CommonJS bundles for Node.js 20. |
| Bundler | esbuild (`esbuild.mjs`). Three bundles: `dist/extension.js` (external: `vscode`), `dist/groupsPreviewWorker.js` (the worker thread of the repository groups editor, no `vscode`), and `dist/helperChannel.js` (the script of the worker and of its batch helpers, section 17). The worker's bundle carries two scripts as text (`scripts/workerScripts.mjs`), each the minified bundle of its entry: the Session Monitor container of each Docker engine (`src/remoteMonitor/main.ts`, the module `devenv:monitor-script`, plan step 11D2) and the thread of the host access analysis (`src/core/helper/configurationAnalysisWorker.ts`, the module `devenv:analysis-script`, plan step 11E2; section 7). The worker's bundle is always minified and has no source map: the extension sends it over SSH through the pipe loader (section 18), so less data goes over the connection. Gone: `dist/sessionMonitor.js` of the removed Session Monitor process (plan step 8, PR C), `dist/remoteMonitor.js` (plan step 11D2: its script is in the worker's bundle), and `dist/configurationAnalysisWorker.js` (plan step 11F1: the extension runs no analysis; a build removes one of an earlier build). |
| Runtime dependencies | None. Use the Node.js modules `https`, `child_process`, `fs`, `crypto`, `os`, `path`. The Dev Container CLI is not a dependency of the extension: it is installed in the workspace helper image. `package.json` lists `@devcontainers/cli` as an exact devDependency only to pin this version: the build passes it to the code as `__DEVCONTAINER_CLI_VERSION__`, and the package is neither bundled nor shipped. So Dependabot proposes each new CLI version in a pull request of its own, and the contract tests (the options and the lifecycle error text, against the package) and `npm run test:docker` check it before a release. |
| Tests | vitest. Unit tests run without VS Code and without Docker. `npm run test:docker` (folder `test/docker/`, not part of `npm test`) runs the core modules against the real Docker engine: the clone of a public repository, the open pipeline on a seeded environment (first open, update, no connection, a removed container, a container of an older version, delete), the container-only Git (variables, label, the settings of the Dev Containers extension in the label, token file, Git configuration, `~/.gitconfig`, credential helper, the helpers of `credentials.gitconfig`, a removed token file, and Git 2.30 of an Alpine 3.13 image), the host access policy (refused configurations, among them a label of Dev Environments, a variable of container-only Git, and a volume of a Docker Compose project; ports on 127.0.0.1; a service on the computer reached through `host.docker.internal`; `--platform`, `--cap-drop ALL`, `--rm`, and `-it`; two GitHub accounts with one repository: two volumes and containers with the identity of each owner, both restored after a lost registry), and the refresh and cleanup of the helper image. It uses a Docker configuration without credentials and credential helpers (its only `auths` entry, for a registry that does not exist, turns off the default credential helper of the platform), so no credentials of the user, and fails when it leaves or removes a Docker object other than the helper image and pulled base images. Unit 7 (`test/docker/remoteHost.test.ts`): an SSH server in a container (`test/docker/sshd`: Alpine, openssh-server, Docker CLI, key authentication only, the socket of the engine of the runner mounted), a test SSH config used through a wrapper `ssh` first on `PATH` (the SSH config of the user is not touched), and a Docker configuration of its own with the context of the host (named after it, for example `htldvm`): the test of a host and its failures (unknown host key, login failed, unreachable), the switch of the context and the remote mode, and the open pipeline of a seeded environment through the context (every Docker call through SSH, counted in the log of the SSH server; the container and the volume on the engine reached through SSH; the token in the tmpfs; `dockerHost` in the registry; a stop refused on another host). Not covered: a genuinely separate machine, a rootless remote engine, the attach of the Dev Containers extension, port forwarding (section 16, live checks). Plan step 3 (section 18): `src/core/loader/pipeLoader.test.ts` (the constants, the one-line program, `loaderCommand`, `encodeBundle`, `bundleHash`) and `src/core/loader/pipeLoader.e2e.test.ts` (the real `node -e` loader in a temporary folder: the entry gets the rest of the input and the paused input; another hash, an input that ends early, a line that is no JSON string or too long, a missing entry and invalid arguments exit 3 and store nothing; the resume from a stored file without reading the input; a stored file with another hash is replaced; no temporary file stays; a bundle of 2 MB); `test/docker/remoteMonitor.test.ts` checks the loader command of the monitor, the resume after `docker restart`, and a changed stored script (exit 3 after a restart, then `ensure` creates the container again). |
| VS Code API | `engines.vscode` `^1.90.0`, `@types/vscode` of the same version |
| Package | `@vscode/vsce` creates the VSIX file (decision D-6: private VSIX) |

**Layering rule.** Code in `src/core/` never imports `vscode`. It receives everything it needs through interfaces (process runner, file system paths, logger, clock). Code in `src/vscode/` adapts VS Code to these interfaces. The bundles of the containers (`src/remoteMonitor/`, `src/helperChannel/`) use Node.js built-ins and pure modules of `src/core/` only. So most of the logic can be tested without VS Code.

## 3. Extension manifest

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `name` / `displayName` / `publisher` | `vscode-dev-environments` / `Dev Environments` / `nimblescape` |
| `extensionKind` | `["ui"]` |
| `extensionDependencies` | `["ms-vscode-remote.remote-containers"]` — **required**. The extension must declare its dependency on the Dev Containers extension explicitly. It uses this extension to connect a window to a container (concept 7.8). |
| `activationEvents` | `onStartupFinished`, `onResolveRemoteAuthority:attached-container` |
| View container | Activity bar, id `devEnvironments`, title "Dev Environments", one view `devEnvironments.repositories` (concept 6.2) |
| Settings | Exactly the settings of concept section 8, prefix `devEnvLauncher` (working name, decision D-1) |
| Command category | "Dev Environments" |
| Commands | `devEnvironments.start`, `.stop`, `.delete`, `.selectConfiguration`, `.rebuild`, `.showOnGitHub`, `.switchEnvironment` (keybinding `ctrl+alt+e`, on macOS `cmd+alt+e`), `.refresh`, `.search`, `.showLog`, `.signIn`, `.selectOwners` ("Select Organizations…", icon `$(filter)`), and `.selectOwnersFiltered` (the same command with the icon `$(filter-filled)`, hidden in the Command Palette). The former `.installDocker` ("Install Docker…", which opened the walkthrough) was removed with the walkthrough (user decision 2026-09-27); the action **Install Docker…** of the error `dockerNotInstalled` runs `.dockerSetup.show` (hidden in the Command Palette: looks for the Docker CLI again, then runs `devEnvironments.repositories.focus`, the view with the Docker setup); the action **Start Docker** of the error `dockerEngineNotRunning` runs `.dockerSetup.start`. `BootstrapDocker` (the Docker CLI of the extension; before plan step 11I2, the CLI adapter `ContainerAdapter`) reports a CLI that it found before and that cannot be started anymore (ENOENT) with `onCliLost`, and the Docker setup shows the setup (`DockerSetup.reportCliLost`, without a lookup, so that the next call of BootstrapDocker looks the CLI up again; on Windows it then checks `wsl --status`, review round 3, W3-1). The options of BootstrapDocker (`findDocker`, `onDaemonStatus`, `onCliLost`) come from `dockerAdapterOptions` in src/vscode/dockerAdapterOptions.ts, which has its own unit test (review round 3, W3-2) Unit 7: `devEnvironments.useRemoteDockerHost` ("Use a Remote Docker Host…") and `.useLocalDocker` ("Use the Local Docker"), in the Command Palette (section 16). `.chooseDockerHost` ("Choose the Docker Host…", hidden in the Command Palette): the Quick Pick of "Use a Remote Docker Host…" with the current host marked (a current host that is no alias of the SSH config, one entered as an SSH address, is added as the first entry, marked; review round 3, G2), and "Use the Local Docker" last, marked when the local Docker is current (user request 2026-09-28, "it shall show the config list again"); the command of the first row of the list. User request 2026-09-28 ("the icon can then go away"): the view title bar has no icon of the Docker host anymore (it had `.useRemoteDockerHost` and `.chooseDockerHost` with the context key `devEnvironments.remoteDockerHost`, both removed). User decision 2026-09-28: each Docker host question (`switchToRemote`, `switchToLocal`, `switchBack`; `RemoteDockerCommands.confirm`) has a second modal button "<button>, Don't Ask Again", remembered per question (not per host; the buttons of `switchToRemote` and `switchBack` read "…, Don't Ask Again for Any Host") in `dontAsk` of remote-docker.json (`RemoteDockerState`); a question answered so is skipped (logged). `.askAgainDockerHost` ("Ask Again Before Changing the Docker Host", Command Palette) clears them. `DockerHostIndicator` (src/vscode/dockerHostIndicator.ts) sets the first row of the list (`DockerHostRow` in treeView.ts, with the choice of the host as its command; only above other rows, so an empty view keeps its welcome content; user report 2026-09-28: the merged header of the single view did not show the title reliably; user request 2026-09-28, "the headline shall be shown also in local mode": "Local Docker" with the switched-off monitor `devenv-monitor-off`, "Remote Docker host: <host>" with `devenv-monitor-remote`, the switched-off monitor with the connection sign (user requests: "use the remote monitor icon"; 2026-09-28, "align the header icon: local unfilled, remote unfilled with connect icon"; the icon font of `contributes.icons`), "Docker endpoint not supported: <endpoint>" with `devenv-monitor-warning`, the switched-off monitor with the warning sign), the view's title ("<host> (remote)", "Local Docker", "<endpoint> (not supported)"; while no Docker CLI is found, where the target reads as the local Docker, "Dev Environments" and no host row: review round 3, G1) and its description ("Remote: <host>") from every target that `DockerTargets.resolve` reads (`onDidResolve`); activation reads the target once. Unit 7, PR 2: `.closeAndKeepRunning` ("Close and Keep Running"), in the Command Palette only while the context key `devEnvironments.connected` is set (the controller sets it when this window has an environment), and in the menus of the row of that environment (flag `canCloseAndKeepRunning`, section 12). `.rowActivated` (hidden in the Command Palette, in no menu; user request 2026-09-27): the `command` of each repository row (`TreeItem.command` in treeView.ts, with the row as its argument). `Controller.rowActivated` runs Start with the row on a double-click: `RowActivationTracker` (src/vscode/rowActivation.ts, no `vscode` import) counts two activations of the same row within `DOUBLE_CLICK_INTERVAL_MS` (500 ms, the clock port) as one and then starts over (a triple-click starts once); with `workbench.list.openMode` = `doubleClick` (`readListOpenMode` in settings.ts, read at each activation) every activation counts. Nothing happens where the row has no `canStart`, or when its environment is connected in this window (checked live, in case the row is older than the connection). Enter and Space arrive as one activation and only select. |
| View title bar | `navigation@0` Select Organizations… (`.selectOwners` when `!devEnvironments.ownersFiltered`, `.selectOwnersFiltered` when `devEnvironments.ownersFiltered`; the extension sets this context key from the setting `owners` at activation and at each change), `navigation@1` Search, `navigation@2` Refresh (user request 2026-09-28: no icon of the Docker host; the first row of the list chooses it, and the header shows "Dev Environments: <title>" with the Docker host as the title); Collapse All comes from the tree view |
| Docker setup commands | `devEnvironments.dockerSetup.install`, `.dockerSetup.installWsl`: the buttons of the Docker setup in the welcome view; `.dockerSetup.start`: the action **Start Docker** of the notification "Docker is installed. Start it now?" after an installation, and of the error "Docker is not running." (`dockerEngineNotRunning`; offered only in a local window, `vscode.env.remoteName === undefined`). All hidden in the Command Palette (`menus.commandPalette`, `when: false`); each refuses in a remote window with "Open a local window to install Docker." (`.dockerSetup.start`: "Open a local window to start Docker.") |
| Welcome views | First the Docker setup while `devEnvironments.dockerSetupRequired` (user decision 2026-09-26: "when no remote docker is configured and local docker is not available, the repositories shall not be shown, instead, the side view shall show the install docker wizard"): the intro "Dev Environments needs Docker, which is not installed on this computer." (user decision 2026-09-27: shorter texts, numbers only where there are two steps, on Windows); `isWindows && !devEnvironments.wslReady` "1. Install WSL 2…" with **Install WSL 2** (`.dockerSetup.installWsl`), `isWindows && devEnvironments.wslReady` "✓ WSL 2 is installed." instead; "Install Docker…" per platform (`isWindows` numbered 2., `isMac` and `isLinux` without a number) with **Install Docker** (`.dockerSetup.install`; macOS and Windows also name the license of Docker Desktop, the Docker Subscription Service Agreement); "Your repositories appear here once Docker is installed…" (no button; `!isLinux` "Dev Environments starts Docker when needed.", `isLinux` "Dev Environments asks to start Docker Engine when needed."). Then the sign-in entry, unchanged; then the entries of the list, each with `&& !devEnvironments.dockerSetupRequired`. While the setup is required, `Sidebar` gives the tree an empty model (so VS Code shows the welcome view) but keeps the full model for `model()` (the switcher); the discovery keeps running. The former row **Install Docker…** above a list (`InstallDockerRow`) was removed: it could no longer appear |
| Context keys of the Docker setup | `devEnvironments.dockerMissing` (no CLI found; looked up every 10 seconds while missing), `devEnvironments.dockerSetupRequired` (`dockerSetupRequired(dockerMissing, remoteDockerHostConfigured)` in src/core/docker/dockerSetup.ts: no CLI and no remote Docker host; the remote host: the last read Docker context points to `ssh://` (unit 7, `DockerTargets.last`); without a CLI no context is read, so the setup shows as before), `devEnvironments.dockerInstalled` (the opposite of `dockerMissing`), `devEnvironments.dockerReady` (the last `docker info` that ran anyway succeeded; the extension never asks Docker for it in the background, because of the Resource Saver of Docker Desktop), `devEnvironments.wslReady` (Windows: `wsl --status` exit code 0, checked at activation while Docker is missing, when a CLI that was found is lost, and after `wsl --install`) |

## 4. Local storage

All files are in the global storage folder of the extension (`context.globalStorageUri.fsPath`), on the local computer.

| File | Content | Writers |
|---|---|---|
| `registry.json` | Environment Registry (concept 7.5) | windows |
| `repositories-<account ID>.json` | Stored result of the discovery of one GitHub account (concept 6.2, 7.4); the account ID is the numeric GitHub user ID of the session. It also records the scan scope it was built with (`scope`, lower-case logins, sorted; empty: all repositories) and the repositories without configuration with their `pushedAt` and default branch (`withoutConfiguration`, for the incremental detection). A list without either field is invalid and built again. | windows |
| `sessions/<window-id>.json` | Window status file (concept 7.9). Another window deletes it when its process ended and it was not updated for 60 s plus the waiting time (plan step 8, PR C, section 12). | one window |
| `pending/<environment-id>.json` | Pending connection file (concept 7.9). Ignored after 2 minutes; the sweep of a window deletes one whose `createdAt` is more than an hour from now (monitor cleanup, user decision 2026-09-29; plan step 8, PR C: the windows sweep, section 12). | windows |
| `operations/<environment-id>.json` | Pending operation, for example `rebuild` (concept 7.14) | windows |
| `disconnect/<environment-id>.json` | Request from a window to the window that is connected to the environment: close the connection first, then hand off the stop, rebuild, or delete (concept 6.2, 7.14). Dropped after 60 seconds; the sweep of a window deletes one whose `requestedAt` is more than 10 minutes from now, and Delete deletes the one of its environment (monitor cleanup, user decision 2026-09-29). | windows |
| `reopen.json` | Reopen record (concept 7.10) | windows |
| `computer.id` | Unit 7, PR 2: the id of this installation, 32 random hex digits (128 bits), the source of the heartbeats to the Session Monitor container of each engine (section 16). Created with `wx` by the first reader (EEXIST: read it); invalid content is replaced. No secret. | windows |
| ~~`monitor.json`, `monitor.lock`, `monitor.version`, `monitor.exit`, `monitor.log`~~ | Plan step 8, PR C: the files of the removed Session Monitor process (its settings, lock, protocol version, exit request, log). Nothing writes or reads them any more; files left by an earlier version stay (no migration, user decision Q7 of 2026-10-02). | — |
| `helper.json` | State of the workspace helper images (section 7): per helper tag the base image and its digest at the build, `builtAt`, the ID of the image that this installation built (`imageId`), the helper generation of that build (`generation`), `checkedAt`, `lastUsedAt`, a rebuild that a check asked for, and the marks of the cleanup for foreign and removed tags; the time of the last cleanup. `imageId` and `generation` are for diagnosis; no code reads them. User decision 2026-09-29: no previous helper image | windows |

Rules:
- Every JSON write is atomic: write a temporary file in the same folder, then rename it. The temporary file is `.<name>.<pid>.<8 hex digits>.tmp` (`atomicJson.ts`); one that a killed write left behind is deleted by the sweep of a window when its modification time is more than an hour from now (section 12; a leftover of the cut of `monitor.log` of the removed Session Monitor process has the same form and is deleted too).
- Read-modify-write of `registry.json` happens under a lock (a lock folder created with `mkdir`, removed after the write; a lock older than 10 seconds counts as stale).
- `deactivate()` uses only synchronous file writes.
- In the workspace volume (not in the global storage), the folder `/workspaces/.devenv+` holds the configuration of the container: `gitconfig`, `credentials.gitconfig`, `docker/`, and `gh/` (only gh's own `config.yml`, no secret) (concept section 9, section 7 "Container-only Git" below). Unit 15: the token is not in the volume. The token and the sign-in of the GitHub CLI are in the tmpfs `/run/devenv` of the running dev container (`TOKEN_FOLDER`).

## 5. Names and labels

- Environment ID: `crypto.randomUUID()`, random, shown in no name: the full ID is only in the labels (user decisions 2026-10-03). Its **pair** `<adjective>-<scientist>` (`namePair` in `src/core/namePairs.ts`: the first 4 bytes of the SHA-256 of the ID pick one of about 100 adjectives, the next 4 one of about 140 scientists; the lists are part of the names and never reordered) ends every name of the environment. An ID is not used when the pair is in use on the Docker engine (a registry entry, or a volume with `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id`, has an ID with that pair) or when its volume or container exists (at most 20 tries, `unusedEnvironmentId`): two environments of one repository (of two accounts) get different names.
- One name for everything of an environment (`resourceName`, user decisions 2026-10-03): `devenv-<owner>-<repository>-<adjective>-<scientist>`, for example `devenv-majikmate-module-ts-curious-hopper`, lower case, every run of other characters than `[a-z0-9]` as one `-`, at most 63 characters (a long `<owner>-<repository>` is shortened, the pair is kept). It names the workspace volume, the dev container, the repository of the environment image, and the Docker Compose project (so also `<project>-<service>`, `<project>_<key>`, and `<project>_default`). `isEnvironmentResourceName` recognises the shape (`devenv-…-<adjective>-<scientist>` of the lists).
- Environment image: `<name>:<build number>` (`environmentImageName`). After each build the pipeline gives it the labels `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id`, `nimblescape.devenv.repository`, `nimblescape.devenv.owner-id`, and `nimblescape.devenv.build-record` (the build record as JSON, at most 8 KiB, without its pinned ID, else empty: the label is always set, so that no such label of the base image stays; `imageRecordLabels`; review round 1 of PR #88, A-R1-3): `EngineDocker.labelImage` gives them without a build (plan step 11B3a; decision of 2026-10-03, no extra containers where the API suffices; before, a metadata build with only `FROM <image>`): `DockerEngine.labelImage` creates a container from the image (never started), commits it under the same name with `LABEL` changes, and removes the container (no new layer, no network; the Dev Container CLI's `--label` reaches neither an image-only configuration without Features, which it only tags, nor Docker Compose); then the previous image of the tag is removed when nothing names it. The registry's build record pins the ID of the labelled image (`BuildRecord.imageId`, option 1 of 2026-10-03): an open uses the image only with that ID (`pinnedImagePresent`), so an image swapped under the name is built again, never started. A container that must be created again without its build record (the configuration cannot be read, the recreate offer, the restore after a failed `up`) is created from the image it was created from (`containerImage`: its name only while that names the container's image ID, else that image by its ID; review round 1 of PR #88, A-R1-1). The policy allows the label of the build record only on the environment image (A-R1-3); the image of a side service of Docker Compose may carry the labels of another environment of the same account (a registry entry; A-R1-7, as the user decision of 2026-09-28 allows the images of the account's own environments). Delete and a rebuild remove the images of `BuildRecord.compose.images` only when they carry the environment's ID (A-R1-2), and the first open checks the labels of its workspace volume right after the create of the volume (which, as `docker volume create`, takes an existing volume as it is) and clones into no volume of another environment (`requireOwnVolume`, review round 2, A-R2-2); the cleanup of a failed first open removes nothing of its name (container, images, Compose project, volume) when the volume carries another environment's ID or its labels cannot be read (then the entry stays) (A-R1-4, A-R2-2). A volume that cannot be removed keeps the entry the same way, with its create mark ended, so the next open completes the clone (`resumeInterruptedClone`) or Delete removes it (PR #78 review rounds 1 and 2, A-R1-1, A-R2-1; `removeFailedFirstOpen`). Review round 3 (A-R3-1): an open of an existing environment, and Delete, read whose the workspace volume is first (`workspaceVolumeOwnership`): a volume of its name with another environment's ID (or without the label) is never opened, and Delete then removes only what carries the environment's ID and the entry, nothing of its name; when Docker cannot say, Delete stops without removing anything. Review round 4 (A-R4-1, A-R4-2): Clone again checks its new volume like the first open (and removes it after a failure only while it is its own; review round 5, A-R5-1: when whose it is cannot be read or the removal fails, the `create` mark stays as ended, so the next open completes the clone; review round 6, A-R6-1: also a `docker volume create` that reports a failure after the daemon created the volume), and the listing of Select configuration reads only its own volume. An entry without a build record (another computer, or a lost registry) takes it over from the newest image of its name (`adoptImageRecord`, `imageBuildRecord`: the environment ID, repository, and owner labels must be the entry's, and the record must name exactly that image and build number) and pins that image's ID: trust on first use; an image of no fitting labels is built again.
- Labels on the volume: `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id`, `nimblescape.devenv.repository`, and `nimblescape.devenv.owner-id` (the GitHub user ID of the owner account; `reconcileFromVolumes` restores the owner from it, and its login at the next open). Additional volumes: before `up` creates a container, the pipeline creates each named volume that it mounts (configuration `mounts`, the `runArgs` that Docker gets, and the image metadata; `mountedVolumeNames`) and that does not exist yet, with the same three labels and `nimblescape.devenv.volume=additional` (`LABEL_VOLUME`, `VOLUME_KIND_ADDITIONAL`, `additionalVolumeLabels`). Only these labels make a volume an environment's own (`isOwnVolume`: `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id` is its ID, and `nimblescape.devenv.owner-id` is its owner): the pipeline records such volumes as additional volumes, and Delete removes only them. It records also the existing additional volumes of other environments of the same owner that the environment mounts (`recordedVolumes`, `isSameOwnerAdditionalVolume`), only to protect them (host access policy below). After a lost registry, `reconcileFromVolumes` restores these own volumes (`nimblescape.devenv.volume` with the environment's labels), the additional volumes of other environments of the same owner that the surviving container mounts (`isSameOwnerAdditionalVolume`), and, to protect them (`protectedMountedVolumes`), the named volumes without labels of Dev Environments that the surviving container of the environment mounts (`listEnvironmentContainers`, label `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id`), for example volumes that Docker created at `up` without labels: not the workspace volume, not a name that `foreignVolumeName` gives to something else, not an existing volume with labels of another program or environment (`volumeLabelOwner`), and not a volume that does not exist. As recorded volumes, the policy refuses them to the environments of other accounts; Delete keeps them (`removableVolumes`: not the environment's own) and records them in `keptVolumes` with the owner. A name with `${devcontainerId}` is not created: Dev Container CLI 0.89.0 substitutes `${devcontainerId}` in `read-configuration` only when a container with the id labels exists (`dA&&(q=yn(q,EA=>tg(ht(nA),EA)))` in `devContainersSpecCLI.js`), so before the first `up` the name is unknown; Docker creates that volume at `up` without labels, and it is never the environment's (Delete keeps it). The container gets `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id` through the CLI option `--id-label`, and `nimblescape.devenv.container-version=1` through `runArgs` (`CONTAINER_VERSION` in `src/core/names.ts`, the version of the container setup; raise it when a change of the setup needs existing containers to be created again, for example a change of the settings of the Dev Containers extension for the container, which reach it only through the label of a new container). A label of `runArgs` whose key starts with `nimblescape.devenv.` or `devcontainer.` is refused (section 7, host access policy), except these two labels of the override configuration with their exact values (`CONTAINER_VERSION_LABEL`, `CONTAINER_CONFIG_UNKNOWN_LABEL`). A container without this label, or with an older value, is created again from its environment image (`up --remove-existing-container`, no build, the volume is kept), and the progress shows that the files outside the volume are removed; without the workspace helper, nothing is started (no `docker start` fallback, user decision 2026-09-29). A container created while the configuration could not be read gets `nimblescape.devenv.container-config=unknown` (and no `runArgs` or `appPort` of the repository); it is created again in the same way at the first open that can read the configuration (`containerIsCurrent`).
- Label prefix (user report 2026-09-27): every label key of the extension starts with `nimblescape.devenv.` (`LABEL_PREFIX`; the keys are in `EXTENSION_LABEL_KEYS` in src/core/names.ts, and `names.test.ts` fails for a label of the prefix in the code that is not in the set, and for any `devenv.…` label in the code). Other tools use the prefix `devenv.` (an in-house image tool named devenv puts `devenv.fingerprint` and `devenv.inputs` on its images), so the extension no longer uses it: the host access policy refuses every key with `nimblescape.devenv.` (and `devcontainer.…` except `devcontainer.metadata`, `isReservedLabel` in src/core/policy/rules.ts) in `runArgs`, in the labels of a Compose service, its build, its volumes and networks, and on the images, and allows every other key, also `devenv.…`. No migration: containers, volumes, and images with the old `devenv.…` labels are not found; the environments are created again.
- Registry entry: `owner: { id, login }`, required: an entry without a valid owner is invalid, left out when the file is read, and the next write keeps a copy of the file first, as for any invalid entry. The rule `isAvailableTo` (src/core/ownership.ts) applies to the sidebar, the pickers, all commands, the reopen, restored windows, pending operations, and disconnect requests; the environment service checks it again in each operation, and the controller once more right before it connects the window. One environment per repository and account (D-3): the registry refuses a second entry with the same repository (case-insensitive) and owner, and `findForAccount(repository, accountId)` gives the environment of the signed-in account. `EnvironmentService.open` reads the session first and uses its account: its own environment, else a new environment. The controller looks up only the environment of the signed-in account for a repository (for Start, Search, the switcher, and Select configuration…, the account of a session with a working token: while GitHub rejects the token, the new sign-in comes before the environment is chosen, so an account change at that sign-in chooses the environment of the new account, also for Try again of a Start; Stop and Delete ask for no new sign-in); A restored window checks the account again right after it takes its environment, and after a failed open pipeline (an account change during its checks, which found no environment of the window yet), and leaves an environment that the account may not use, with its token file. `otherAccount` applies only to an environment that a command names by its ID (a row or the status bar item from before an account change, a restored window, and an account change during an open). Try again of a first open of Select configuration… runs the step of the command after its pick again, with the same configuration, for the account signed in then: an environment that the account has then is rebuilt with the configuration (nothing more when it has that configuration already), as the command does. `reconcileFromVolumes` restores one environment per repository and owner (label `nimblescape.devenv.owner-id`; a volume without a valid owner label is skipped), and only volumes whose name is `resourceName(repository, id)` of their labels; after each `up` (also a failed or cancelled one) and at each open of a running container, its mounted named volumes whose labels make them its own (`isOwnVolume`) join the additional volumes too (a failed first open removes the environment, and its additional volumes stay with its account, a known limit); the additional volumes of a restored entry are the volumes with `nimblescape.devenv.volume` whose labels make them its own. Every other volume (anonymous volumes, volumes of other programs or environments, volumes without the owner label, and `${devcontainerId}` volumes) is never recorded, restored, or removed. Delete removes only the additional volumes that the question listed and the user confirmed (`additionalVolumesToRemove`, also in pending operations and disconnect requests), and only when their labels make them the environment's own and no other environment records them and no Delete of another account kept them (`removableVolumes`); each other one is kept with a log line that names why (for example a volume without the labels of the environment: the user removes it). The question lists only the volumes that Delete would remove (`EnvironmentService.removableAdditionalVolumes`; none when Docker does not answer), so it never offers a volume that it keeps. The additional volumes that a Delete keeps (not confirmed, not removable, or all of them at the Delete of `filesMissing`) are recorded in the same registry change that removes the entry (`EnvironmentRegistry.remove(id, { kept, removed })`, `RegistryFile.keptVolumes`: name, owner, time; one record per name and owner; a removed volume drops all records of its name); `hostAccessInput` adds the records of other owners whose volume exists to `foreignVolumes`, and drops (`forgetKeptVolumes`) the records of a mounted name whose volume no longer exists. Only kept volumes that exist are recorded; a failed first open records the additional volumes that it leaves behind the same way. Invalid records are left out when the file is read, and the next write keeps a copy of the file first (`droppedRecords`). The records cannot be restored after a loss of the registry (container restrictions, section 10).

## 6. Docker

- **Finding the CLI.** The extension host on macOS often has a short `PATH`. Search `PATH`, then `/usr/local/bin`, `/opt/homebrew/bin`, `/Applications/Docker.app/Contents/Resources/bin`, and with `HOME` `~/.docker/bin` (Docker Desktop's per-user CLI setting) and `~/Applications/Docker.app/Contents/Resources/bin` (macOS; user decision 2026-09-27), and `C:\Program Files\Docker\Docker\resources\bin` (Windows). While the CLI is missing (or cannot be started), the extension searches again, at most every 10 seconds, so a Docker installed while VS Code runs is found without a reload.
- **Docker start** as in concept 7.6: `docker info` as the check; `docker desktop start`, and the fallbacks per platform; on Linux with Docker Engine, only the message with `sudo systemctl enable --now docker` and the action **Start Docker**, which runs that command in a terminal after a confirmation. Repeat `docker info` every 2 seconds for at most 2 minutes.
- **Installation plan** (`installPlan` in src/core/docker/dockerSetup.ts; the button **Install Docker** of the sidebar). A modal confirmation lists the exact commands, or the download URL and the target file, and says that an administrator password may be requested in the terminal. The step first looks the CLI up (`lookUpCliNow`): found → "Docker is already installed on this computer." and nothing runs; **Install WSL 2** likewise with `wslReady`. Commands run in a new terminal "Install Docker" (`createTerminal` with `installTerminalOptions`: `/bin/sh` on macOS and Linux, the Windows PowerShell of `%SystemRoot%` on Windows, `cwd` the home folder, `strictEnv` with a fixed `PATH` on every platform (Windows: the folders of `%SystemRoot%` and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WindowsApps`, where winget lives) and a few kept variables of the computer (`HOME`, `USER`, `LOGNAME`, the language, `TERM`, a proxy, the certificate variables `SSL_CERT_FILE`, `SSL_CERT_DIR`, `CURL_CA_BUNDLE`, on macOS the `HOMEBREW_*` settings, on Windows the folders and names of the user), so that terminal profiles, `terminal.integrated.env.*` of the workspace, the search path of the shell that started VS Code, and files of the opened folder change nothing; on macOS the command names the full path of the Homebrew that was found (a path with unusual characters: the download instead); `show`, `sendText`): joined with `&&` on macOS and Linux, one line per command on Windows (Windows PowerShell before version 7 has no `&&`).
  - macOS: Homebrew found (`brew` in `PATH`, `/opt/homebrew/bin`, or `/usr/local/bin`) → `brew install --cask docker-desktop` (the cask `docker` was renamed to `docker-desktop`). If Homebrew still records the cask (the folder `Caskroom/docker-desktop` of the Homebrew prefix, the parent of the `bin` folder of the `brew` that was found, for example `/opt/homebrew` or `/usr/local`) but Docker.app is gone (removed by hand; it counts as present in `/Applications`, in `~/Applications`, and in the folder of `--appdir` in `HOMEBREW_CASK_OPTS`, so that a cask installed with another appdir is never uninstalled), `install` would upgrade the recorded version, remove its services and helpers, and then fail ("It seems the App source '/Applications/Docker.app' is not there"); the plan is then `brew uninstall --cask --force docker-desktop` and `brew install --cask docker-desktop` (joined with `&&`, so the install runs only after a successful uninstall), and the confirmation says that Homebrew first removes its old entry and the Docker data stays. `--force` drops the record although the app files are missing and does not touch the Docker data; never `--zap`, which deletes `~/Library/Containers/com.docker.docker` with all Docker volumes (the workspaces of the user). Otherwise the download of `https://desktop.docker.com/mac/main/arm64/Docker.dmg` (Apple silicon, also for VS Code for Intel under Rosetta, `sysctl.proc_translated`) or `…/mac/main/amd64/Docker.dmg` (Intel) to `~/Downloads`, then `open` of the .dmg; the user drags Docker to Applications.
  - macOS, Homebrew (user decision 2026-09-28): the confirmation ends with `DockerSetupTexts.brewAppManagementNote`. Homebrew removes the quarantine attribute of Docker.app with `xattr`, which macOS refuses ("Operation not permitted") unless the app of the terminal, VS Code, may manage other apps (System Settings › Privacy & Security › App Management).
  - Windows: winget found → `winget install --exact --id Docker.DockerDesktop --accept-package-agreements --accept-source-agreements`. Otherwise the download of `https://desktop.docker.com/win/main/amd64/Docker%20Desktop%20Installer.exe` (arm64: `…/win/main/arm64/…`) to the Downloads folder; it is opened with `vscode.env.openExternal` of the file, so that the shell of Windows handles the elevation prompt of the installer.
  - Linux: Docker Engine from the repository of Docker, by `ID` of `/etc/os-release` (or `/usr/lib/os-release`), with the commands of https://docs.docker.com/engine/install/: `ubuntu` and `debian` with apt (the key to `/etc/apt/keyrings/docker.asc`, the source file `/etc/apt/sources.list.d/docker.sources` written with `printf … | sudo tee`, with the codename (`UBUNTU_CODENAME`, else `VERSION_CODENAME`) and the Debian architecture written out, so that the line works in every shell), `fedora` with `dnf config-manager addrepo --from-repofile` (Fedora 41 and later, DNF 5) or `dnf config-manager --add-repo` (older), `rhel` and `centos` with `dnf-plugins-core` and `dnf config-manager --add-repo`; then `docker-ce docker-ce-cli containerd.io docker-buildx-plugin docker-compose-plugin`, and `sudo usermod -aG docker <user>` with the user name written out (`os.userInfo().username`; a name that is not a plain login name → `manual`), with the note that a new login (or `newgrp docker`) is needed and that Docker's packages replace containerd, runc, or docker.io of the distribution (the package manager asks). With a source of download.docker.com in apt already (`/etc/apt/sources.list`, `sources.list.d/*.list` and `*.sources`; a second one with another key would stop apt) → `manual`. Other distributions and architectures → the installation guide of Docker opens (`manual`). Docker Desktop for Linux is not installed by the extension.
  - Downloads: only HTTPS URLs of desktop.docker.com, also the target of each redirect. Progress notification with Cancel; the file is written as a new `<name>.download` (an existing file or link there is removed first, then created with `wx`) and renamed when complete. Before it opens, it is marked as downloaded: `xattr -w com.apple.quarantine 0081;<time>;Dev Environments;` on macOS, the stream `Zone.Identifier` (zone 3) on Windows; when that fails, the installer is not opened and a message names the saved file.
  - After the terminal or the installer: the CLI is looked up every 5 seconds (without the 10-second waiting time of BootstrapDocker) for at most 30 minutes, until it is found; then the context keys and the sidebar update, and the extension offers **Start Docker**.
  - Start Docker (the notification after an installation, and the action of the error "Docker is not running." in a local window): `ensureDockerRunning` with the progress "Starting Docker". Docker Engine on Linux (`dockerEngineNotRunning`): after a confirmation, `sudo systemctl enable --now docker` in the terminal.
- **Docker socket for the workspace helper:** mount `/var/run/docker.sock` to `/var/run/docker.sock`. This path works with Docker Desktop on macOS, Windows, and Linux, and with Docker Engine. If `DOCKER_HOST`, or else the endpoint of the current Docker context (unit 7), is a `unix://` path, mount that path instead — only on Linux with Docker Engine: with Docker Desktop, the engine runs in a virtual machine, so a path of the computer is not a path of the engine, and the helper always mounts `/var/run/docker.sock`. For a remote Docker host (section 16) the source is a path of that computer: `/var/run/docker.sock`, or the recorded socket of a rootless engine there.
- **Commands in a container** (plan step 11B1; plan step 11I, PR B, the former step 11E5). Every process that the worker runs in a container through its engine is an entry of the script registry (`CONTAINER_SCRIPTS`, `src/core/worker/containerScripts.ts`): a script for `sh -c <script> sh <args…>` (`tokenWrite`, `tokenRemove`, `gitSummary`, `branch`, `homeGitConfig`, `ownershipFix`, `existingPaths`; the scripts stay in the modules that write them: `containerToken.ts`, `gitSummary.ts`, `containerGit.ts`), or a program of the container with its fixed arguments, run without a shell as before (`check`: `/bin/sh -c 'exit 0'`, `gitVersion`: `git --version`, `mountInfo`: `cat /proc/self/mountinfo`, `userId` and `groupId`: `id -u`/`id -g <user>`, `monitorScriptHash`: `sha256sum /opt/devenv/monitor.js`; plan step 11I, decision U2 of 2026-10-08: the commands of the Session Monitor container, `monitorHeartbeat` and `monitorForget`, `node /opt/devenv/monitor.js heartbeat`/`forget` under the lock of the records, and `monitorSettings` and `monitorImages`, `node /opt/devenv/monitor.js settings -`/`images -`). `runScript` is the one way to run one, over the `exec` of the caller's port (DockerEngine in the flows of the worker; EnvironmentDocker in the pipeline, which EngineDocker serves over the same engine): the arguments as positional parameters, of the caller's options only the user, the time limit and the cancel, for the one script that takes the token (`tokenWrite`) the secret of the operation by its name (`secretInputName`, never its value) on standard input, and for the two entries that read a plain standard input (`plainInput`: `monitorSettings`, `monitorImages`, the JSON of the image settings or list) that input, which `runScript` refuses for every other entry; EngineDocker refuses any other secret and a token that the operation does not hold. No call site builds a command of its own (a unit test checks the sources of `src`, `src/core/worker/containerScripts.test.ts`). The steps of the batch helper are no entries: they are the step kinds of its own table, which the helper builds and runs in its own container (section 17).

## 7. Workspace helper

- **Image.** `resources/helper/Dockerfile`, with `ARG BASE_IMAGE=node:24-trixie-slim` (Node.js 24 LTS on Debian 13). It installs `git`, `ca-certificates`, `curl`, `openssh-client`, `tini` (the init process), `util-linux` (`flock`) and `coreutils` (`timeout`) for the lock of the heartbeat records of the remote Session Monitor (`flock` since review round 2 of PR #58, `timeout` since round 3; listed here in round 4), the Docker CLI with the buildx and the Compose plugins (Debian packages `docker-ce-cli`, `docker-buildx-plugin`, and `docker-compose-plugin` from `download.docker.com`; the Compose plugin is not pinned, like `docker-ce-cli`), and `@devcontainers/cli` in a pinned version. It sets `git config --system --add safe.directory '*'`, because the helper runs as root on files of another user.
- **Tag.** `devenv-helper:<first 12 characters of sha256(Dockerfile content + CLI version + helper generation)>` (the generation `HELPER_GENERATION`, below, as `\ngeneration <n>`: review round 3 of PR #64, P5, so raising it gives a new current tag, which is built, and an image of an older generation never stays the current helper), label `nimblescape.devenv.helper=true`. The extension builds the image when this tag is missing (after the installation, and after an extension update that changes the Dockerfile, the CLI version, or the helper generation; in the background right after the activation when the Docker engine of the current context answers, local or remote (plan step 6, PR D), otherwise at the next open: review round 7 of PR #64, R7-2), with `--pull`; without it when the registry does not answer or when the build with `--pull` fails (for example the pull limit of Docker Hub, which counts the pull of the daemon but not the digest request of the extension): the image then comes from the local base image, and it is recorded so that a later check that gets a digest asks for a build with a fresh base image (the check of the refresh below, which has no caller in this version). **Refresh:** (The refresh, like the cleanup below, has no caller in this version, since the open that ran it moved into the worker; the [decision of 2026-10-09](plan-remote-worker.md#2-decisions) restores both in the extension's preparation of the worker for an open and in the background prebuild (a follow-up PR). Today no helper run builds, and the preparation of a worker builds only a missing tag.) Until the open moved into the worker, only the open pipeline did it, and only with the setting `updateImagesOnConnect` on; the other helper runs only built a missing tag. At most once a week per tag, the extension read the registry digest of the base image, with the registry client and the credentials of the image check (section 9) and its own 5-second limit, in the background: the open did not wait for it, so it overlapped with the image check and added no wait (NFR-08). A changed digest made the next open build the same tag again with `--pull --no-cache` (new base image, Debian packages, and Docker CLI; the progress showed "The workspace helper is being updated.") and remove the previous image if it had no tag anymore. No answer from the registry kept the image, and the check ran again at most once a day; a failed build kept the image (Docker moves the tag only after a successful build) and checked again a week later. An image built with `--pull` without a recorded digest (built before `helper.json` existed, or the registry answered without a digest) got the current digest, without a build. A window reused the result of its helper check for an hour, but an open first checked that the tag still had the image of that result (`docker image inspect <tag>`; review round 4 of PR #64, R4-1: another window may have rebuilt the tag and removed the image that it had before, or a prune removed it): when the tag was gone or had another image, the result was not reused; when Docker did not answer, it was. Until plan step 7, which removed the per-step helper runs, a helper run of an open that found its image gone reset the result of the window when it still held that image, so the next open resolved the helper image again instead of pinning the removed ID. **Cleanup:** (The cleanup has no caller in this version either; the [decision of 2026-10-09](plan-remote-worker.md#2-decisions) restores it with the refresh.) At most once a day, of the images with the label `nimblescape.devenv.helper=true`, except the image of the current tag, the extension removes dangling images and helper tags that no window used for 7 days (`lastUsedAt`, written at most once an hour). A helper tag that `helper.json` does not know is foreign (another extension version, possibly of another installation of VS Code such as Insiders, which has its own `helper.json`): it first gets 7 days of grace, so a helper that old windows of another extension version still use is not removed at once. A removed tag keeps a tombstone in `helper.json` for 90 days: when the tag comes back, another installation built it again and uses it, so it is not removed again, and two installations do not remove each other's helper in a loop. Tags of other repositories are never removed, and never with force; a failed removal (for example an image that a helper container uses) is logged and tried again at the next cleanup. Two windows can do this at the same time: each write of `helper.json` is atomic, and the file is advisory, so at worst a check or a build runs twice. **No previous helper image** (user decision 2026-09-29: "there shall be no case where we need a previous helper image"): every start of a stopped environment runs through the Dev Container CLI (`up` and `run-user-commands`, so `postStartCommand` always runs); there is no `docker start` fallback. The one `docker start` left is D-22: stopped side services of a Docker Compose dev container that runs already (the CLI does not call Compose for it). When the current tag is missing and cannot be built (offline after an extension update: Docker Hub, download.docker.com, or npm cannot be reached), no other helper image is used: the ensure of the preparation of the worker fails, and the open fails with `helperFailed`, with “The workspace helper could not be prepared.” as the cause in its message (`environmentLockUnavailable`: the worker could not be prepared, nothing was changed); the next open tries the build again. *Background prebuild* (`HelperPrebuild` in `src/core/helper/helperPrebuild.ts`, started by `extension.ts` without being awaited): when the helper state file of the engine has no record of the current tag (or only a removal mark; review round 5 of PR #64, R5-2: this record alone decides, so after an update that changed the tag the windows that start first build it and later windows find its record), it builds the current helper image in the background, so the first open after an update does not wait for it and the network is needed right after the update rather than at the first open. Plan step 6, PR D (decision "Local and remote work the same" of 2026-09-30): it runs on the Docker engine of the current Docker context, local or remote alike, as one operation on that target (`runWithDockerTarget`), so the engine key, the state file (`helper.json` for the local Docker, `helper-remote-<hash>.json` for a remote host: `helperStatePathFor`) and every Docker call are those of an open on that engine; an endpoint that is neither local nor SSH gets none. It asks Docker nothing when it is not due (so an activation does not wake Docker Desktop from its Resource Saver mode, nor connect to a remote host), and does nothing when the engine does not answer `docker info` (`dockerEngineAnswers`; for a remote host our own `ssh -o BatchMode=yes` check comes first, so no question is ever asked; Docker is never started for it; the next activation or the next open tries again). A switch of the Docker host starts no new prebuild: the next activation, or the first open on that host, builds its tag. PR #77 review round 1: the prebuild has a time limit of 15 minutes (`HELPER_PREBUILD_TIMEOUT_MS`, on every engine; A-R1-1), as nobody can cancel it and an open that joins its build can end only its own wait: a build that stalls (a half-open SSH connection after a sleep) would otherwise block every open and worker preparation on that engine until a reload; at the limit the build is ended (logged as not ready in time), and a caller that waited for it builds for itself. A remote host that our own ssh cannot check (`skipped: 'notAnSshTarget'`) gets no prebuild (A-R1-2), as the ssh of the Docker CLI may ask a question; its first open builds the tag. A failure is only logged. It uses `HelperImages.prebuildImage` (`src/core/helper/helperImages.ts`; plan step 11I, U7: no longer through WorkspaceHelper), which shares the cached promise of the window with `ensureImagePresent`, the preparation of a worker before an operation or a heartbeat (`HelperChannelsOptions.prepare`), the one other call of the extension that builds the tag: a preparation that starts during the prebuild waits for that build, and a prebuild that starts during the build of a preparation joins it, so within a window the tag is never built twice. (Plan step 11I, U7: the helper runs no longer use this cache, they run in the worker on its own image. `HelperImages.ensureImageUse`, the ensure with the maintenance that the open called before it moved into the worker, has no caller in this version; the [decision of 2026-10-09](plan-remote-worker.md#2-decisions) restores the maintenance in the extension's preparation of the worker for an open and in the background prebuild (a follow-up PR).) Across windows there is no lock (review round 6 of PR #64, R6-1: a lock left behind by a window that closed or reloaded during the prebuild would block the prebuild of every window for 30 minutes, which is worse than the duplicate build it prevents): windows that start at the same time may each build the tag once (BuildKit shares the layer cache; the extra images stay dangling: the daily cleanup that would remove them has no caller in this version; the [decision of 2026-10-09](plan-remote-worker.md#2-decisions) restores it in the extension's preparation of the worker for an open and in the background prebuild (a follow-up PR)), and windows that start later find the record in `helper.json` and do nothing. A caller that joins the shared build of the window (a preparation or the prebuild) can cancel its own wait with its signal, without cancelling the build, and gets its progress (`onBuild`) too (review round 5 of PR #64, R5-1). It is cancelled when the extension is deactivated (`dispose`, which ends the `docker build`); a preparation that waited for it then builds for itself. It logs to the Dev Environments output: the build, its output, and its result (a failure is a warning, and the next open tries again). The helper image of an open is resolved once, by its first `prepareHelper` (`HelperImageUse`: the tag and the ID of its image), and passed to every helper run of that open (review round 2 of PR #64, A-N1). `prepareHelper` takes it from the return value of `WorkspaceHelper.ensureImageUse` (the image that this call awaited), never from a callback (review round 3 of PR #64, P1: until plan step 11I, U7, a reset of the cache of the window meanwhile, by another engine or, until plan step 7, by a missing image at another run, could otherwise have left the open with a tag without its ID). Plan step 11I (U7, decision of 2026-10-08): the pipeline runs only in the worker, whose WorkspaceHelper answers `ensureImageUse` and `ensureImagePresent` with the worker's own image (`HelperDeps.ownImage`; it and the socket of the engine, `HelperDeps.socket`, are read from the worker's own container by `readOwnHelper`); it has no cache, no Docker port and no Dockerfile, so it checks, builds, maintains and records no helper image (the helper image of the extension, with its cache, is `HelperImages`). Every helper run of an open uses the image ID (review round 3 of PR #64, P2): a rebuild of the tag by another window (`--pull --no-cache`; such a rebuild has no caller in this version; the [decision of 2026-10-09](plan-remote-worker.md#2-decisions) restores it in the extension's preparation of the worker for an open and in the background prebuild (a follow-up PR)) or a build of another installation never changes the helper image in the middle of an open, and an engine switch in the middle of an open finds no such image. The ID of the current tag is the one of the image that the build of this ensure made (review round 3 of PR #64, P4), or else the one that the existing tag has at the ensure. Review round 4 of PR #64 (R4-2/R4-3): each build gets a label with a random nonce, `nimblescape.devenv.build-id=<32 hex characters>`, and `BootstrapDocker.buildImage` (before plan step 11I2, `ContainerAdapter.buildImage`) then finds its image by that label (`listImagesByLabel`: `docker image ls --filter label=… --no-trunc`, and the same with `--filter dangling=true`, so an image whose tag another build moved meanwhile is found too); exactly one image is expected, otherwise the ID is unknown (with a warning). There is no `--iidfile`: the Docker CLI fails after a successful build when it cannot write that file (for example a VS Code in a Flatpak sandbox with a Docker CLI outside it), and with the containerd image store the file may hold a digest that does not resolve. The lookup never decides whether the build succeeded. `helper.json` records as `imageId` only an ID found by the build label of a build or rebuild of this installation, never one read back by the tag, which another build may have moved meanwhile. Earlier, an open whose ID could not be read at all ran its tag by the tag; now the batch helper takes only an image ID (`parseBatchParams`), and the worker always has the ID of its own image (`readOwnHelper` refuses an inspect without it). Thus the configuration that the Dev Container CLI of that image read and checked is built and started with the same CLI, also when another open in the window (of another repository, since opens are exclusive only per repository) resolves another helper image meanwhile. The later `prepareHelper` calls of the same open do nothing. Plan step 11I (U7): a helper run outside an open (the listing of Select configuration) runs on the worker's own image (before, it used the tag that the window resolved last). `helper.json` keeps only a full image ID (`sha256:` and 64 hex characters). *Helper generation* (review round 2 of PR #64, A-N2; review round 3 of PR #64, P5): the code constant `HELPER_GENERATION` (`src/core/helper/helperImage.ts`, now 1) is part of the tag hash, so a release that fixes a security problem in the helper image (its tools, its Dockerfile) raises it and every user's helper is built again, also when neither the Dockerfile nor the CLI version changed; it is also recorded per tag at each build and rebuild (`generation`, a positive safe integer, for diagnosis; `parseHelperState` drops any other value). When the image of the tag of an open is gone at a helper run (a prune, or the removal of the old image after another window rebuilt the tag; also another open of another repository in the same window, whose ensure an hour or more later rebuilds the tag or runs the daily cleanup while this open waits, for example at a question: accepted, as the outcome is the same and the next open works, review round 21 of PR #64, A-R21-1; neither that removal nor that ensure has a caller in this version; the [decision of 2026-10-09](plan-remote-worker.md#2-decisions) restores the maintenance in the extension's preparation of the worker for an open and in the background prebuild (a follow-up PR)), the engine refuses the start of the batch helper (no such image): the run fails with `helperFailed`, without a build (review round 2 of PR #64, A-N1; review round 3 of PR #64, P2: a build may give another image, with another CLI than the one that checked the configuration; the next open resolves the helper image again). Until plan step 7, which removed the per-step helper runs, the cache of the window was then reset when it still held that image (review round 4 of PR #64, R4-1), and a helper run outside an open that found its tag gone built it again once; the worker has no cache and builds no helper image (plan step 11I, U7). A `helperFailed` of a helper run in Step 8 (the build, `up`, or the restore with the previous environment image) marks the helper unavailable for the rest of the open (no Git setup) and ends the open with `helperFailed` (as a rule "The workspace helper could not be prepared."; a refusal of the batch scope may keep its own message, review round 5 of PR #82, A-R5-4), without the restore (which needs the helper too), also when the container that Step 5 found still runs: after Step 5 the environment is never opened as it is. General rule (review round 1 of PR #68, A-R1-1; round 2, A-R2-1 to A-R2-4): when `up` returned and its `run-user-commands` then fails with `helperFailed` (the lifecycle commands of its container did not run), `runUp` and `runComposeUp` compare the container of `up` with the containers of the environment (and of its Docker Compose project) listed right before `up`: a container that this `up` created is removed (stopped when the removal fails), one that existed and did not run before `up` is stopped again, one that ran before `up` is left as it is; the containers of other services that `up` created or started are stopped again; when the listing before `up` failed (logged), the container is only stopped, never removed, and the containers of the other services are left as they are (it is not known which of them `up` created or started; the log and the detail of a switch of the dev service then say "created or started"; review round 3 of PR #68, A-R3-3). So no later open opens it as it is: the next open creates or starts it with all lifecycle commands. Review round 3 of PR #68 (A-R3-4): Step 9 holds no busy mark, so another window may have started the same container meanwhile and connected to it; before anything is touched, `withdrawAfterHelperFailed` reads the window status files (`EnvironmentServiceDeps.windowStatuses`) and the pending connection files, and `otherWindowUsesEnvironment` (src/core/busy.ts, shared with the rules of the Session Monitor: another window ID, the state `active`, its process alive, `updatedAt` at most `HEARTBEAT_MAX_AGE_MS` = 60 s away, or a pending connection file of another window at most `PENDING_MAX_AGE_MS` = 120 s old, for this environment) decides: with such a window, neither the container nor the other services are stopped or removed (logged), and the detail says "It was left running: another window is connected to it." When the window status files or the pending connection files cannot be read (logged), it is not known whether another window uses the container, so nothing is stopped or removed either (when in doubt, the containers stay; outcome `useUnknown`, "It was left running: it could not be checked whether another window is connected to it."). In both cases a container that did not run before `up` gets the mark `lifecycleIncomplete` (below), so the next open runs its lifecycle commands. Review round 3 of PR #68 (A-R3-5): a container that could be neither removed nor stopped runs without its lifecycle commands: the optional registry field `lifecycleIncomplete` (validated as a non-empty string in `registry.ts`) names its ID, and the detail adds "the next open runs its lifecycle commands" (outside a switch). While that container runs, Step 9 does not open it as it is (`startContainer` runs `up` without `--remove-existing-container` and `run-user-commands` for it), and neither does the `helperFailed` at Step 5 (`opensAsItIs`). The field is removed by the next successful open (`finish`, which also drops a mark that names a container that was replaced meanwhile; a mark of another container has no effect) and when the container of the mark is removed (`withdrawAfterHelperFailed`, the cleanup of a failed switch to Docker Compose). No migration: the field is optional. In a failed switch (of the kind, or of the dev service), the previous configuration stays selected, so the next open does not start the container of `up`: the detail then says only what happened ("It was stopped.", "It could not be removed and was stopped.", review round 3 of PR #68, A-R3-1); when the cleanup of a failed switch to Docker Compose (`removeFailedComposeContainers`) removes the dev container that `withdrawAfterHelperFailed` could only stop, the detail says "It was removed." and does not name it again among the containers that were removed again. After a failed switch from Docker Compose to a single container whose removal failed, the kind of the environment is that of its build record (`existingCompose`: the record first, by the key `compose` (review round 4 of PR #68, A-R4-2), then the dev container), so "Rebuild later" ends with `startFailed` ("The container … is no container of Docker Compose; it is not started until the rebuild.") instead of starting the leftover single container. The sentence "The Dev Container CLI removed the previous dev container." (or "may have removed … before it failed") of a switch from Docker Compose is given only when that `up` ran with `--remove-existing-container` (there was a dev container; review round 3 of PR #68, A-R3-2). Review round 4 of PR #68: (A-R4-6) when the run holds no busy mark (Step 9), `withdrawAfterHelperFailed` first sets one (`update`, `takeStepMark`: never over another mark, neither a live one of another window nor any of this window or process, and it never waits), then reads the files of the windows, and clears only that very mark in `finally` (`releaseStepMark`); an open of another window that begins meanwhile waits for it. When the mark cannot be set because another window holds one, nothing is stopped or removed ("It was left running: another window is working on the environment."); when the registry fails, it counts as `useUnknown`. (A-R4-3) A pending connection file of another window says "another window is opening the environment" (it may still wait or build), a window status file "another window is connected to it". (A-R4-5) Step 9 never stops, removes, or renames a container without that busy mark: before `up --remove-existing-container` of an existing (outdated) container, before the removal of stray containers of other services next to a single container, before the removal of the container of the other kind (`replaced` in `runComposeUp`, for example the single container that a failed switch from Docker Compose left running and marked for another window), and before `movePreviousDevContainer`, `requireNoOtherWindow` sets the busy mark (held for the rest of the run: `releaseBusy` in `finally`, or `finish`) and reads the files of the windows; when another window is connected, opening, or holds a busy mark, or when that cannot be checked, nothing is changed, the mark `lifecycleIncomplete` stays, and the open ends with `startFailed` ("…, but another window is connected to the environment. Nothing was stopped, removed, or renamed. Open or rebuild the environment again when that window is closed."). A marked running container is never opened as it is. (A-R4-1) `finish` clears `lifecycleIncomplete` only when it still has the value that this run decided with (read at the start of the pipeline and again by Step 9 and `opensAsItIs`, which both also count the earlier value) or when it names the container whose `up` and `run-user-commands` this run completed (`lifecycleMarkClears`): a mark that another window set meanwhile for the container that this open opened as it is stays. (A-R4-4) A container that ran before `up` (for example one of the mark) is described as running, not as started ("The container runs; its lifecycle commands could not run and run at the next open." only while the registry mark names it); in a switch, the texts say "created or started" when the listing before `up` failed, in both directions. (A-R4-2) The kind of the environment is Docker Compose when its build record has the key `compose` with an object (`hasComposeRecord`, in `existingCompose`, at a failed switch, and in `isComposeEnvironment`), also for a part that `composeRecordOf` rejects. (B-R4-2) The mark is written at most twice, with a pause of 500 ms; "The next open runs them" is logged only once it is written. When it cannot be written, the stop of a container that this `up` created or started (no other window) is tried once more; when it still runs, or when it was left running for another window, the window remembers it in memory (`unrecordedLifecycle`, consulted and cleared like the mark), an error is logged, a warning (`Messages.lifecycleNotRecorded`) is shown, and the detail says "…, and it could not be recorded that its lifecycle commands did not run: stop or rebuild the environment before working in it." instead of promising the next open. Nothing is ever stopped or removed on the paths of another window, or for a container that ran before this open (B-R4-3: it is not marked either). Review round 5 of PR #68: (A-R5-1) `requireNoOtherWindow` lists the containers of the environment again once its busy mark is set (`anyContainerRuns`: every container with the ID label, so the dev container and the other services of Docker Compose, and every container of its Compose project; never the listing of Step 9). Every state but `exited` and `created` counts as running (`running`, `restarting`, `paused`, and any other), and so does a listing or an inspect that fails. When none runs, a window status file of another window does not count: its connection is lost, as it cannot be attached to a stopped container (the controller lets such an open through as well, concept 6.2), so Step 9 creates an outdated stopped container again (or removes, replaces, or moves stopped containers) while such a window stays open; a pending connection file, a busy mark, and files that cannot be read still end the open with `startFailed`. When one runs, the rule of round 4 stays. (Risk 2) While a container runs, the destructive checks (`requireNoOtherWindow`, and the recreate offer below) also count as "not known" an `active` status file of a live window that is older than `HEARTBEAT_MAX_AGE_MS` but not stale by the Session Monitor's rule (`isStaleLiveWindowStatus` in src/core/busy.ts, shared with src/monitor/rules.ts until plan step 8, PR C removed it, which re-exported `SLEEP_GAP_MS`, `DEFAULT_WAITING_TIME_SECONDS` and `waitingTimeMs` from there: 60 s plus the waiting time of the settings, and any age during the sleep grace, which a window infers from its own status file when that is older than `SLEEP_GAP_MS`, `sleepGraceOfWindow`). The withdrawal after a failed helper keeps the rule of round 3: what it may stop or remove did not run before `up`, so no window whose file is late can be attached to it. (Risk 3) `offerRecreation` clears the busy mark that `requireNoOtherWindow` set for this run (`PipelineContext.stepMark`: only that exact mark, and the run counts as holding none only once it is gone) before its question, so a question that stays open does not make other windows find the environment busy; after the answer it sets its own mark (`rebuild`), checks that nothing changed (`requireUnchangedSinceQuestion`), and then reads the files of the windows as `requireNoOtherWindow` does (with a fresh `anyContainerRuns`): another window that is connected (while a container runs), that is opening the environment, or files that cannot be read end the open with `startFailed` ("To create the damaged container … again, it must be removed, but …. Nothing was stopped, removed, or renamed. …"). This holds for every recreate offer. (A-R5-2) A (dev) container that ran before `up` is never described as created or started: the restore after a failed update says "The update failed. The previous container runs; its lifecycle commands could not run. …" with the outcome (or "… could not run and run at the next open." only while the mark names it, and "The update failed. The previous container runs as before this open." otherwise), and the detail of a switch says "Its dev container runs already, but its lifecycle commands could not run." (`afterUpClause`, and `kindSwitchFailure` with `ranBefore`). (A-R5-3) The guard of a failed switch of the dev service (final review, FF-1) follows what happened in the run: `movePreviousDevContainer` began to move the previous dev container (`devServiceMoved`, `devServiceMovedFrom`, and `previousDevContainer` with the service from its label `com.docker.compose.service`); the build record only adds to it, read by its key (`recordedComposeService`). An older record that `composeRecordOf` rejects, or no record, thus no longer lets the restore run `up` with the new configuration next to the renamed previous dev container (whose files outside the volumes would be lost). The new environment image of an update is removed afterwards. The detail of the error says what happened, per path: the replaced container of an update or rebuild ("The container was created again from the new environment image, but its lifecycle commands could not run. It was removed; the next open creates it again."; on a first open, "The new environment is removed again; open the repository again to create it."), the restore after a failed update ("The update failed, and the container was created again from the previous environment image, …" or "… and the previous container was started again, …"; for a previous container that ran before this open, "The update failed. The previous container runs; …", review round 5 of PR #68, A-R5-2), a switch of the Docker Compose dev service (what happened to the new dev container and to the previous one: kept, stopped; created again by Docker Compose as its own service; or removed because its rename failed), a switch between Docker Compose and a single container (kindSwitchFailure with the sentence about the created container), Step 9 ("The container was started, …" or "… created again, …"), and the recreate offer ("The damaged container was created again, …") (review round 3 of PR #64, P6; user decision 2026-09-29: a helperFailed during an update fails the open; before, a current container that already ran when Step 5 found it opened as it is, review rounds 4 and 10 of PR #64, R4-4 and R10-1). The warning that the update failed (`buildFailed`) is left out when the `helperFailed` is itself the failure of the update (of the build, or of a helper run before or at the `up` of the new image); when the update failed for its own reason and the restore then meets a `helperFailed`, the `buildFailed` warning stays and the open ends with `helperFailed` (review round 11 of PR #64, R11-3; user decision 2026-09-29). The Git setup right before `up` (`prepareGit`) is such a helper run: its `helperFailed` ends the step like one of `up` (no `gitSetupFailed` warning, and nothing is removed for a switch), while the Git setup of a running container that is opened without an update keeps its warning (review round 11 of PR #64, R11-2). The warning `Messages.helperFailedOpenedAsItIs` stays for the `helperFailed` at Step 5 (the helper cannot be prepared at the start, and the running container is current and opens as it is): it names what was not applied, the rebuild (Rebuild, "Rebuild now") or the selected configuration, which then does not stay selected (review round 4 of PR #64, R4-4; review round 14, R14-1). A `helperFailed` in Step 8 has no such warning, and a newly selected configuration is not applied there either (the previous one stays selected); the variant of the warning for the update is gone (user decision 2026-09-29: a helperFailed during an update fails the open). At a switch between Docker Compose and a single container, or of the dev service of Docker Compose, a `helperFailed` of `up` after the switch removed or moved a container keeps the cleanup of the switch (nothing opens as it is: the containers of the other kind are gone, the previous dev container stays stopped) and ends the open with `helperFailed`, with the detail of the switch, not with `startFailed` (R4-5). A `helperFailed` before the switch removed or moved a container (for example when the folders of the bind mounts or the Git configuration are written) changed nothing: it is handled as any other `helperFailed` of Step 8, so the open ends with `helperFailed` without the detail of a switch, and the running container stays as it was (review round 11 of PR #64, R11-1; user decision 2026-09-29: a helperFailed during an update fails the open). A `helperFailed` of `devcontainer up` itself changed nothing either, as its helper container never started; only after `up` returned (for example at run-user-commands) may the previous dev container be gone, and the open then ends with `helperFailed` and the detail of the switch (review round 12 of PR #64, R12-2; round 13, R13-2). A `helperFailed` during the offer to create a damaged container again (its `up`, or the hashes of the other services of Docker Compose) ends the open with `helperFailed`, not with `startFailed` or a claim that other services would be created again (R13-1). When the helper cannot be prepared, a container that runs already still opens without the helper when no container of the environment must be created again (review round 23 of PR #64, A-R23-1) (for a Rebuild or a selected configuration with the warning `Messages.helperFailedOpenedAsItIs`, which names what was not applied; review round 14 of PR #64, R14-1) (review round 1 of PR #64, L2: one that is outdated would be created again, which needs the helper, so the open fails with `helperFailed` at once, without a warning first); the log line then says that the workspace helper is not available and the running environment is opened as it is (review round 2 of PR #64, B2). Whether it opens as it is (a Docker listing for Docker Compose) is asked only when the answer is needed, and a failure of that listing counts as no, so the error of the open is never replaced by it (A-N4).
