Plan step 11D3: the image of a helper tag may carry its monitor tag `devenv-monitor:<the same hash>` (the image of the Session Monitor container, section 16); the cleanup of the helper images removes it with its helper tag, first (review round 1 of PR #101, A-M1: Docker refuses only the removal of the last reference of an image in use, so the helper tag, removed last, stays while a helper or a monitor runs from its image), removes a monitor tag whose helper tag is not on its image (removed, or moved by a rebuild; Docker refuses while a monitor runs from that image, and a later cleanup tries again), and never a tag of the current image; after a rebuild, a previous image whose only tags are monitor tags is removed with them. That cleanup and that rebuild have no caller in this version; the [decision of 2026-10-09](plan-remote-worker.md#2-decisions) restores them in the extension's preparation of the worker for an open and in the background prebuild (a follow-up PR).
- **Run.** Plan step 7 (user decision of 2026-10-01): every helper step runs in the batch helper of an operation (§17, *Batch helper* and *Batch scope of the opens*); the per-step `docker run` that this item describes is removed. Each helper run was `docker run --rm -i` with the workspace volume at `/workspaces` and the label `nimblescape.devenv.helper-run=true`. Only the Dev Container CLI runs (read-configuration, build, up) also got the Docker socket and the cache volume `devenv-helper-cache` (`--user-data-folder`: only what the CLI keeps there itself; CLI 0.89.0 downloads Features into a new folder below the helper's temporary folder at each run, so the volume is no Features cache, and a build or rebuild with Features needs internet access). Git runs (clone, Git summary) and the file-reading scripts got neither, because Git runs programs that the repository configuration names (filter drivers). Runs that needed no network (Git summary, reading and listing configurations) used `--network none`.
- **Token for the clone.** The token never appears on a command line, in an environment variable of a container, or in `.git/config`. The helper receives it on standard input, writes it to a `tmpfs` mount, and a Git credential helper reads it from there. The remote URL is `https://github.com/<owner>/<repository>.git`.
- **Container-only Git** (concept section 9). At each open, before `devcontainer up` (and also when the container runs already), the step `gitFiles` of the batch helper of the open (`GIT_FILES_SCRIPT`; as root, with the Docker socket in reach but without a token: its Git only edits a copy of `/workspaces/.devenv+/gitconfig` in a folder of root in the helper with `git config --file` and runs no program that a repository names, section 17) creates `gitconfig` when it is missing (user.name: the GitHub profile name or the login; user.email: `<databaseId>+<login>@users.noreply.github.com`, from `viewer { databaseId login name }`, with the session account as fallback) and ensures its section `[credential "https://github.com"]` (an empty helper, then the helper of the container, which reads `/run/devenv/github-token`), creates `credentials.gitconfig` with an example in comments when it is missing (never overwritten), creates `docker/` and `gh/` (mode 0700; `gh/` only for gh's `config.yml`). Everything gets the numeric owner of `/workspaces/<name>` (only an existing `credentials.gitconfig` that has a second, hard link keeps its owner); on a first open this runs after the ownership fix, and after each creation of a container the ownership fix also covers `/workspaces/.devenv+`. A link in place of a folder or of a file is removed first. While it reads and replaces `gitconfig`, the step holds Git's lock `gitconfig.lock`, as `git config` does (a `git config --global` in the running dev container meanwhile fails instead of being lost; when the lock exists, the step fails), and it leaves a `gitconfig` of more than 1 MiB as it is (the step fails; follow-up of plan step 11I, review round 1, A-F4 and A-F2). A failure is a warning; the environment opens. Exception: a `helperFailed` of the Git setup right before `up` ends the step like one of `up`, with no warning, and nothing is removed for a switch (section 7; review round 11 of PR #64, R11-2; round 12, R12-1: also for Docker Compose, whose Git setup now runs before the switch removes or moves a container). The question for `viewer` starts as soon as the session is known (it overlaps with the Docker start, the clone, and the image check), is shared per account in the window, has a time limit of 5 seconds, and after a failure the fallback counts for 10 minutes.
- **Token in the memory of the container** (unit 15, user decision 2026-09-26, `src/core/helper/containerToken.ts`). The dev container has the tmpfs `/run/devenv` (`TOKEN_TMPFS`: `rw,nosuid,nodev,noexec,size=1m,mode=0700`). The token is kept in memory, not in the volume, and is gone when the container stops; the kernel may swap it to the swap space of the computer or the Docker VM (a tmpfs is swappable memory). The tmpfs: a single container gets `--tmpfs` as the last entry of the `runArgs` of the override configuration, the dev service of Docker Compose an entry in its `tmpfs` in the up model (the other services none; the build model none). The checks accept exactly this `--tmpfs` value only in the override configuration and in the merged configuration of an existing container (`runArgsFindings` with `cleared`), and refuse every mount of a configuration at or below `/run/devenv` or `/var/run/devenv` (`configFolderTarget`, `TOKEN_FOLDER_ALIAS`, class `unsupported`, also with the checks off; a clearer message only), and a `shared`/`rshared` propagation (`-v …:rshared`, `bind-propagation`, Compose `bind.propagation`) at `/` or a parent of either (`tokenPropagationTarget`, class `protected`): `mounts`, `-v`, `--mount`, `--tmpfs`, Compose `volumes`, `tmpfs`, `secrets`, `configs`, and the targets of `volumes_from` of the dev service. `/run` itself stays allowed: Docker mounts ours on top. Lifecycle token (user decision 2026-09-27): when `up` ran, the token is written right after `up` (`runUserCommands` of the pipeline, into the container of the result of `up`, as the remote user of that result), before `devcontainer run-user-commands` (section 8); otherwise in `finish` of the open pipeline, after the ownership fixes and `~/.gitconfig`, on every path where the container runs (running already, or started by `up`; `finish` skips the write when it was done after `up` for the same container), an exec as root over the Engine API (`sh -c TOKEN_WRITE_SCRIPT sh <remote user> <login>`, the script `tokenWrite`, section 6) gets the token of the session on standard input only (never in argv or a variable; plan step 11I, PR B: the exec names it as the secret of the operation, never its value; the output and errors are logged without it). The script (POSIX sh, no awk, dash and BusyBox; tools: `cat`, `id`, `chown`, `chmod`, `mkdir`, `ln`, `rm`, `tr`, `wc`, `stat`, `find`, `ls`) goes into `/run/devenv` (`cd`, then only paths relative to `.`) and writes nothing unless that folder is our tmpfs (`OWN_TMPFS`, review of unit 15, T1/T2: `stat -f -c %T .` is tmpfs; the device of `.` (`stat -c %d`, as major:minor) is on exactly one line of `/proc/self/mountinfo`, with the mount point `/run/devenv`, the root `/`, no optional fields, type tmpfs, `nosuid,nodev,noexec`, the super options `rw,size=1024k,mode=700` (`TOKEN_TMPFS_SUPER_OPTIONS`; SELinux may add `seclabel` or `context=` in between); no parent up to the root of the container at `/run/devenv` or `shared:`; no mount point below `/run/devenv`) (exit 3); a folder that root cannot enter (mode 000 of the user) is given back to root first, only when it is our tmpfs; then it makes the folder `root:root` 0700, takes everything in it back top down without following links (`find . -mindepth 1 -exec chown -h 0:0 {} \; -type d -exec chmod 0700 {} \;`, review of unit 15, P1: root needs no `DAC_OVERRIDE`) and empties it (so no process of the user changes it while it is written; exit 5 when `ls -A .` fails or lists anything), writes `github-token` (0600), `gh/` (0700) with `hosts.yml` (0600: both forms that `gh` reads, the keys `oauth_token`, `user`, and `git_protocol: https` of the host `github.com` and `users.<login>.oauth_token`, the token copied with `cat` from the token file) and `gh/config.yml` as a link to `/workspaces/.devenv+/gh/config.yml` (gh writes that file with `O_TRUNC` through the link, and never puts a token into it: it writes the hosts to `hosts.yml`), gives everything to `id -u`/`id -g` of the remote user (a numeric user as it is): the files first, then `gh/`, and the folder itself last. The login is checked as a GitHub login (1 to 39 letters, digits, hyphens, and underscores, starting with a letter or a digit), in TypeScript and in the script; a login that fails the check, or a token with characters that YAML would need to escape, gives no `hosts.yml` (a note, Git still works). On a failure after the tmpfs check, the folder is emptied again (trap). Exit 5 when root may not change the owner (`--cap-drop` of `CHOWN`) for a remote user other than root: the trap empties the folder again. A failure is logged and gives `Messages.gitSetupFailed`; the open continues, and the lifecycle commands run without the token. The lifecycle commands run after the write and have the token (user decision 2026-09-27). When a window leaves an environment because of the account (sign-out, or another account), the controller first empties `/run/devenv` of the running dev container (plan step 11I, U4: of each running dev container of the environment; `TOKEN_REMOVE_SCRIPT` as root, and again as the remote user of the entry when that fails, each try within 20 seconds and the whole removal within 60 (with several running dev containers, that limit may end the removal before a later one is tried: the log then says that the token could not be removed from the container of the request, and a container that was not reached keeps the token until it stops); only in our tmpfs (`OWN_TMPFS`: exit 3 when there is no tmpfs or another one, nothing removed); as root after the same take-back, as the user after mode 0700 on its folders; exit 1 when `ls -A .` fails or lists anything); best effort, logged. A stopped container holds no token: nothing runs then. A container that starts without an open (a restart by Docker, `docker start` of the user) has an empty tmpfs until the next open: the credential helper answers nothing, and `gh` cannot read its folder. After each creation of a container, an exec as root (the script `homeGitConfig`; as the remote user when that fails) writes the `~/.gitconfig` of the remote user (`HOME_GIT_CONFIG_CONTENT`: `[credential] helper =`, then an `[include]` of `credentials.gitconfig` and `gitconfig`, for Git older than 2.32 and processes without the variables) when it is missing or empty; a file of the image with content and links stay as they are. The copy of the Git configuration of the computer is switched off only by the setting `copyGitConfig` of the container (section 8), not by this file. Then an exec of `git --version` as the remote user (`gitVersion`): Git older than 2.9 gives the warning `Messages.oldGit`, Git 2.9 to 2.31 a log line, no Git nothing.
- **Ownership.** The helper clones as its Git user (`devenv-git`, section 17), and the files of the clone then get root. Otherwise the user in the container cannot write the files, so the pipeline changes the owner of the files that belong to another user (`find … -exec chown`) to the user and group of `remoteUser` (in the fixes of the batch helper not a file with a second hard link, whose other name may be a file of another user elsewhere in the volume; follow-up of plan step 11I): before the first `devcontainer up` of a new clone, as the step `repositoryOwnershipFix` of the batch helper of the open, with the numeric IDs of the remote user from the `/etc/passwd` of the environment image (plan step 11G1: read through the archive endpoint of a created, never started container, `DockerEngine.imageFile`; before, the fix ran in a short-lived container of the environment image; a user that `/etc/passwd` does not name skips this fix, and the fix after `up` gives the files their owner; the remote user is resolved as Dev Container CLI 0.89.0 does it: the last `remoteUser` of the label `devcontainer.metadata`, else the user of the container, which is the last `--user`/`-u` of the `runArgs` that `up` passes to Docker, else the last `containerUser` of the label, else the user of the image, else root; of `user:group` only the user part counts, and `0` is root), because `onCreateCommand` and `postCreateCommand` run during `up` as that user; and after each creation of a container, as root in the dev container (the script `ownershipFix`, section 6). The CLI substitutes each entry of the label at `up` before it reads the users, so `imageRemoteUser` reads `remoteUser` and `containerUser` substituted with `helperCliVariables` (hotfix review 2, P3: `${localEnv:DEVUSER:vscode}` is `vscode`); when the user that decides holds a variable whose value is not known (a leftover, for example `${localEnv:TERM:vscode}`), the fix before `up` is skipped (logged), and the fix after `up` uses the `remoteUser` that `up` reports; after a failed lifecycle command, no remote user of the label is stored then. Review round 9 (D9-1): the paths of the repository that the other services of Docker Compose mount from the workspace volume (`composeUpModel`'s `serviceFolders`, for example `./data/postgres` of a database; never the repository folder itself) keep their owner with their content: the fix after `up` leaves them out, each pattern one argument (`servicePathArguments`, round 11; round 9 had the shell text `SERVICE_PATH_ARGUMENTS`: only absolute paths below the repository folder, never `.git` or a path in it, with `*`, `?`, `[`, and `\` escaped), never shell text. Review round 10 (D10-3): in these paths the fixes still give their owner to the files and folders of root (uid 0), in the same `find` run: `find <folder> -xdev \( \( -path P -o -path 'P/*' … \) -user 0 -o ! \( … \) \( ! -user <uid> -o ! -group <gid> \) \) -exec chown -h …` (`SERVICE_OWNER_FIX`); files of any other owner there (the data of a service, for example uid 999 of Postgres) keep it. Files of root come there from the clone of the helper (also a resumed clone), whose files get root after its step. Chosen over a pass over the files that Git tracks (`git ls-files`), which the dev container may lack the Git for: a service that runs as root keeps its access to files of another owner through its capabilities (`CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE`, `CAP_FOWNER`, kept by default), and the images that need an owner of their data (postgres, mysql, redis, mongo) run their service as a user other than root and give the data that owner at their start; a root service with these capabilities dropped (`cap_drop`) could lose write access to a file of root that the fix gave the dev user (a known limit). The recorded paths (`composeUpModel`'s `serviceFolders`): the workspace-volume mounts with a subpath of the other services, not read-only ones (D10-3: a read-only mount is no data of the service, and a file that root rewrote there must get its owner back), never `.git` or a path in it (D10-3), and (D10-2) also the real path of the source when a link in the repository leads elsewhere in it (`ComposeModelOutput.realPaths`; Docker follows the link of the subpath), or, for a folder that the pipeline creates, where it lands after the links of its nearest folder (`ComposeModelOutput.mountCreateTargets`, printed by `COMPOSE_MODEL_SCRIPT`). Review round 10 (D10-1) recorded them in the entry (`Environment.serviceFolders`) as a list that never shrank while a container of another service existed; review round 11 (G3, G4, G5) computes the list at each use from facts instead (`serviceFolderFacts`, `boundServiceFolders`), in this order and without duplicates: (1) the paths of the current model with their real paths (`composeUpModel`'s `serviceFolders`, `PipelineContext.modelServiceFolders`); (2) the live mounts: the paths of the repository that the existing containers of the project other than the dev container (`isDevContainer`; running or stopped) mount, from `ContainerInfo.volumeSubpaths` (`HostConfig.Mounts` and `Mounts` entries of `Type` volume whose `Source`/`Name` is the workspace volume, with `VolumeOptions.Subpath` joined to `/workspaces`, not read-only, with the filters of the recorded paths: below the repository folder, never the folder itself, never `.git`; `liveServiceFolders`), read with the list of the containers of the environment and of the project (`composeContainers`: each one inspected over the Engine API); (3) the retired paths: the recorded paths of earlier runs, kept only while they still exist in the volume (`EXISTING_PATHS_SCRIPT`, the script `existingPaths`, `test -e`/`-L` as root in the running dev container after `up`, an exec over the Engine API, in calls of at most 16 K characters of paths; before `up` they are kept whole, and a `find -path` of a path that does not exist simply matches nothing). A path below another path of the list is dropped (its `-path P/*` covers it). When the containers cannot be read, or the existence check fails, the recorded list is kept whole: it never shrinks on an error. Where it is used: the ownership fix after `up` (`finish`, `refreshServiceFolders`, which writes the list back to `Environment.serviceFolders` after each `up`, also for the single-container path after a switch from Docker Compose: round 10 cleared the list there, and the data of Postgres in `./pgdata` was chowned after a `docker container prune` or a switch, G3); the fix before `up` after a resumed clone (the list of `runComposeUp` before `up`: model, live mounts, and the whole record, also written to the entry, so that it only grows before `up` and a failed `up` leaves it recorded); and the confirmation of **Delete** (`EnvironmentService.repositoryServiceData`, the record and the live mounts, together with the record in the controller). `reconcileFromVolumes` fills the list of a restored entry from the live mounts of the containers that it finds (G4). Bound (G5): at most `MAX_SERVICE_FOLDERS` (1000) paths, the model and live paths first; over it (per-run paths such as `./data/${HOSTNAME}` add one each open, and their folders stay) the entry keeps the first 1000 and `Environment.serviceFoldersOverflow` (it stays set, since the paths beyond are not recorded), a warning is logged, and the ownership fixes pass `'repository'` (`ServiceFolders`): the whole repository folder counts as a path of the services, so only the files of root get their owner and no data of a service loses its owner. The arguments of `find` are built in TypeScript in linear time (`servicePathArguments`: `-path P -o -path 'P/*' -o …`, each pattern one `argv` entry with its glob characters escaped), in place of the loop of `SERVICE_PATH_ARGUMENTS`, which rebuilt `"$@"` for each pattern (quadratic: 5000 patterns took 51 s in dash, and about 15000 exceeded `OWNERSHIP_TIMEOUT_MS`). Their length is bounded too (`MAX_SERVICE_ARGUMENT_CHARACTERS`): the `execve` in the container holds them with the script; where the pipeline runs, in the worker on Linux, the bound is 256 K characters (ARG_MAX is 2 MiB on Linux, one argument at most 128 KiB); the constant still gives 24 K characters on Windows (about 300 paths of 30 characters), from the time when the command line of the Docker CLI on the computer held them, which has at most 32767 characters there; over it, as over 1000 paths, the whole repository counts as a path of the services. Chosen over the never-shrinking union of round 10: it lost paths when no side container existed (G3), was empty for a restored entry (G4), and had no bound (G5). The fix before the first `up` of a new clone leaves out nothing (no service has run on the files yet, so the source folders that a service mounts get the owner too), except after a resumed clone (`PipelineContext.resumedClone`). Review round 12 (D12-1): after a resumed clone on the single-container path, where `runComposeUp` does not compute the list, `prepareOwnership` computes it itself (`serviceFolderFacts(env, [])`, the record and the live mounts, overflow → `'repository'`); it still never throws but for a cancellation. Review round 12 (D12-2): `find -xdev` stays only out of other file systems, and a local named volume that the dev container mounts below the repository folder lies on the file system of the workspace volume (`/var/lib/docker/volumes`), so the fix after `up` went into it (a volume that the dev service shares with db, `pgdata:/workspaces/api/.pgdata` and `pgdata:/var/lib/postgresql/data`, had db's files of uid 999 given to the remote user at each create and Rebuild). The fix after `up` now adds the paths below the repository folder at which the dev container mounts anything but the workspace volume (`withDevMountFolders`, `devMountFolders`: named and anonymous volumes, tmpfs, bind mounts) to the paths of the services, where only files of root change: the folder of a dev-only `node_modules` volume, which Docker creates as root, still gets the remote user, and a volume shared with db keeps db's files. They come from `docker inspect` of the dev container (`ContainerInfo.mountTargets`, from `Mounts` and `HostConfig.Tmpfs`, read with `findContainer` when the fix runs), not from the model: it covers the `mounts` of devcontainer.json, the model of Docker Compose, `runArgs`, and single containers alike, and it is what is mounted where the fix runs. They are not recorded (they matter only in the dev container; the fix before `up` mounts only the workspace volume). When the dev container cannot be read, the fix passes `'repository'`. Pruning the mount points of `/proc/self/mountinfo` was not chosen: it would leave the root-owned folder of a new `node_modules` volume to root. Review round 12 (P12-2): `find` does not follow links, and the live mounts (`liveServiceFolders`, `VolumeOptions.Subpath`) give only the path as written, so the data behind a link of the repository (`data -> storage/pg`) of an entry restored by `reconcileFromVolumes` was not left out until the next open (whose model has the real paths). `SERVICE_OWNER_FIX` now resolves each path of its `find` test in the volume (the argument after `-path` that is no `<path>/*` pattern; `cd -P`, a builtin, for a folder, else `readlink -f`) and adds a real path that differs, lies in the repository folder (not the folder itself, not in `.git`) to the test, as further arguments (`set -- "$@" …`, no shell text); a pattern with an escaped character, a real path with a pattern character, or more than `MAX_SERVICE_REAL_PATHS` (64) real paths make the whole folder count as a path of the services (`find -xdev -user 0`). This covers every ownership fix (before `up`, after `up`) without another Docker call. Review round 12 (S12-1): the nested-path filter of `serviceFolderPaths` looked up each ancestor of each path (a `slice` per level, O(depth × length) per path: 1000 paths at depth 2000 took 6 s, synchronously in the extension host); it is now linear (a tree of the path segments, in the input order), and a path longer than `MAX_SERVICE_PATH_LENGTH` (4096) or deeper than `MAX_SERVICE_PATH_DEPTH` (256) is kept, not split, and counts as overflow (`boundServiceFolders`, `servicePathArguments`: `'repository'`, only files of root change); it is never dropped. The case above takes about 20 ms. Review round 13 (D13-1): `devMountFolders` left out every mount of the workspace volume, but a mount of it (whole, or a subpath) below the repository folder is an alias of files of the volume that `find -xdev` walks (the same file system): `./data:/workspaces/api/pgview` of the dev service (rewritten to a subpath of the workspace volume) with db mounting `./data` had db's files of uid 999 given to the remote user through `pgview`, and so had `../..` mounted below the repository. Such mounts are now protected too (only files of root change there); only the mount at `/workspaces` is left out (and the repository folder itself, which `serviceFolderPaths` never names). The same files are still fixed in full under their canonical path, unless that path is protected itself. `find` reports "File system loop detected" for an ancestor mounted below itself and exits with 1 (the fix logs that some owners could not be restored), but it goes on with the rest. Review round 13 (D13-2): the real paths of the services are resolved by shell code of its own. The resolution is `SERVICE_REAL_PATHS`, working on the positional parameters, with its state `whole`, `added`, and `seen` set once per script by `SERVICE_REAL_PATHS_INIT`, never taken from the environment; `service_owner_fix` runs it at the fix. A real path is added once (`seen`; before, a real path reached by two links was added twice), `MAX_SERVICE_REAL_PATHS` bounds the real paths of both resolutions together, and a real path with a newline makes the whole folder count as a path of the services, like one with a pattern character. Review round 13 (D13-3): the uid-0-only rule of round 12 for every mount of the dev container left the content that an image put into a dev-only volume with an owner other than the remote user (for example a `node_modules` copied up as uid 1000 for a remote user of another uid), so `npm install` failed with EACCES; before round 12 it got the remote user. (The trigger of the review, `updateRemoteUserUID`, cannot occur: `upArgs` passes `--update-remote-user-uid-default never`; a mismatch of the uid of the image content is enough.) Data safety first: an anonymous volume of the dev container gets the full fix again, and named volumes and bind mounts keep the uid-0-only rule, because they can be shared with another container or environment (a service that is not started, an external volume). The rule (`devMountFolders`, `isAnonymousVolumeName`): a mount of `Type` volume whose `Name` (in `Mounts` of `docker container inspect`) is 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters, the name that Docker gives an anonymous volume, while the host access checks are on. `docker container inspect` has no field that marks a mount as anonymous (the label `com.docker.volume.anonymous` is on the volume, not on the mount, and needs a `docker volume inspect` per volume; older Docker versions do not set it), and a named volume can have such a name, but with the checks on the host access policy refuses a configuration that names such a volume (`foreignVolumeName`: "another container"), so such a mount of the dev container is its own anonymous volume: fresh per container (a Dockerfile `VOLUME`, `-v /path`, a Compose volume without a source), or inherited by Docker Compose from the previous dev container when it recreates it. With the checks off, a configuration may mount the anonymous volume of another container by its name, so it stays protected. A tmpfs needs nothing: `-xdev` does not go into it. A known limit: a dev-only named volume (for example `node_modules:/workspaces/api/node_modules` in the dev service only) whose image content is owned by a non-root user other than the remote user keeps that owner; the user can change it (`sudo chown -R`) or use an anonymous volume. Review round 14 (P14-1): the rule of D13-1 also protected a mount of the workspace volume at its own canonical path (`..:/workspaces/api` and `../src:/workspaces/api/src` of the dev service: the subpath `api/src` at `/workspaces/api/src`), which is no alias: only files of root changed there, so after a change of the uid of the remote user its files of the old uid kept it (EACCES). `ContainerInfo.mountTargets` now carries the subpath of a volume mount (`MountTarget.subpath`: `VolumeOptions.Subpath` of the entry of `HostConfig.Mounts` with the same volume `Source` and the same normalized `Target`; the top-level `Mounts` have no `VolumeOptions`; two entries with different subpaths at one target give none). `workspaceIdentityMounts` names the mounts of the workspace volume below the repository folder whose `/workspaces/<subpath>` (normalized) equals the normalized target; a mount without a known subpath is never one (the whole volume is one only at `/workspaces`, which was left out already). Lexically equal is not enough: Docker follows a link in the volume when it mounts a subpath (`src -> data` mounts `data` at `/workspaces/api/src`, an alias of db's files). So `withDevMountFolders` reads `/proc/self/mountinfo` of the dev container (the entry `mountInfo` of the registry, `cat /proc/self/mountinfo` as root, only when there is such a candidate), and `verifiedIdentityTargets` keeps a candidate only when the topmost mount at its target lies on the same device (`major:minor`) as the topmost mount at `/workspaces` and its root (the folder in that file system, which the kernel records after links; octal escapes decoded) is the root of the mount at `/workspaces` joined with the subpath. `devMountFolders(…, identities)` leaves out a mount only when it is in that set and a lexical identity itself. When mountinfo cannot be read, gives no clear answer, or shows another folder, the mount stays protected. Review round 14 (S14-1): the second fix after `up`, of `CONFIG_FOLDER` (`/workspaces/.devenv+`: then the token and the Git configuration; since unit 15 the token is only in the memory of the dev container, and the folder holds the Git and Docker configuration of the dev container), passes no paths of the services: a mount of the dev container at or below it had the files of the mounted folder given to the remote user (`./pgdata:/workspaces/.devenv+/pg` while db mounts `./pgdata`: db's files of uid 999; a named volume that db shares; the whole repository through `..`), and it hid the token and the Git configuration. The policy now refuses such a target as not supported (`configFolderTarget`, `configFolderMountItem`: "mounts into the extension's internal folder are not supported"), after normalizing `.`, `..`, double and trailing slashes, on segment boundaries (`/workspaces/.devenv+x` is not below it): in Docker Compose for the dev service in `decideServiceMount` (every mount type) and in the service key `tmpfs` of the Compose rules (`src/core/policy/compose.ts`); for a single container in `mounts` of every source (`MountSpec.target` from `target`, `dst`, `destination`) and in `-v`/`--volume` (`volumeFlagTarget`), `--mount`, and `--tmpfs` of `runArgs`. Other services are not checked: their containers do not have the folder (the workspace volume is refused in them). `workspaceMount` and `workspaceFolder` are replaced by the override configuration of `up` (and ignored by the CLI for Compose), so they cannot place anything there. Other paths of `/workspaces` outside the repository (for example a cache volume at `/workspaces/.cache`) stay allowed. The steps of the batch helper are not configurations and are not checked (plan step 7 removed the per-step helper runs with their tmpfs at `CONFIG_FOLDER`, `hideConfigFolder`; the Compose steps of the batch helper run with `CONFIG_FOLDER` closed to the owner of the repository, unless root owns it, section 17). Review round 15 (K3 = P15-1, D15-1, S15-3): `configFolderTarget` is lexical, and a mount of the dev container can still lie in `CONFIG_FOLDER`: the runtime follows a link of the repository in a target inside the container (`x -> ../.devenv+`, a volume at `/workspaces/api/x`), `volumes_from` of a service that mounts there (checks off), or a tmpfs. So the fix of `CONFIG_FOLDER` no longer runs in the dev container: `fixConfigOwnership` reads the numeric IDs of the remote user in the dev container (the entries `userId` and `groupId` of the registry, `id -u <user>` and `id -g <user>` as root), checks that each is a decimal number (`isNumericId`; otherwise the fix is skipped and logged), and runs `CONFIG_OWNERSHIP_FIX_SCRIPT` (`service_owner_fix` without paths of services, refusing a link or a missing folder in place of the folder) as the step `ownershipFix` of the batch helper of the open (`WorkspaceHelper.fixConfigOwnership`, as root; before plan step 7, a container of its own of the helper image with only the workspace volume, no Docker socket, no cache volume and `--network none`), which mounts the workspace volume and no mount of the dev container, so it sees the folder of the volume and no such mount. For a single container too. A failure is logged, as before. Such a mount can still hide the Git configuration in the dev container (before unit 15 also the token), which only breaks the Git authentication of the owner there. Review round 15 (K4 = D15-2): `serviceFolderPaths(…, gitPaths)` keeps paths in `.git` for the targets of the mounts of the dev container (`devMountFolders`; for example a volume that db shares at `/workspaces/api/.git/pg`, or with the checks off a bind of the computer at `.git/hooks`); `withDevMountFolders` filters the paths of the services without `gitPaths` first, then joins the targets and passes the list to the fix with `gitPaths` (the script `ownershipFix` with `servicePathArguments(…, gitPaths)`; since round 16, below, the set of the targets). The records of the services and the fix before `up` keep the filter of `.git` (review round 10, D10-3). Review round 16 (L2 = D16-2): the real paths that `SERVICE_REAL_PATHS` adds were still filtered for `.git` for every path, so a target of a mount of the dev container that is a link into `.git` (`data -> .git/pg`, a volume of db at `/workspaces/api/data`, which Docker mounts at `.git/pg`) lost its protection. A per-call flag would also lift the filter for the paths of the services in the same list, so the marker is per argument: `withDevMountFolders` returns the list and the set of the targets (`DevMountPaths`), `servicePathArguments` writes the patterns of each target between the arguments `(` and `)` (grouping for `find`, no change of the test), and the loop of `SERVICE_REAL_PATHS` sets `mount` at a `(` and clears it at a `)` that do not follow `-path` (a path is never read as a marker); only with `mount` set is a real path in `.git` added. `gitPaths = true` still marks every path (all targets).
- **`${localEnv:NAME}`.** The values of the computer are not passed to the helper: the CLI resolves the variables in the helper, to the value of the helper for a variable that it sets itself (`HELPER_ENV_NAMES`: `HOME`=/root, `PATH`, `HOSTNAME`, `NODE_VERSION`, `YARN_VERSION`), otherwise to an empty value or to their default. The extension finds the names in the text of `devcontainer.json` and names them in one warning, which also names those that get the values of the helper.
- **Host access policy** (concept section 9, src/core/policy/, entry point `checkContainer` in src/core/policy/index.ts; every restriction and limit: [Container restrictions](container-restrictions.md)): checked on the resolved configuration and the `mergedConfiguration` of `read-configuration --include-merged-configuration` before any build, and on the label `devcontainer.metadata` of the environment image before each `up` that creates a container. Before `up`, the `runArgs` and the `appPort` of the override configuration are checked again as a whole (as Docker gets them; hotfix review 1). A refusal is `UserFacingError('hostAccess')` with each setting named: settings that need access to the computer in `Messages.hostAccess`, settings that the policy does not know (unknown flags and options, arguments that are no flag, entries that are no text) and values that it does not support (for example `--restart=always`) in `Messages.unsupportedOptions` (`hostAccessReport`). `--network container:…` is refused, also in Docker's long form `name=container:…` (CSV, keys and values in lower case). The allow-list of `runArgs` (`RUN_FLAGS`) gives a one-line reason for each flag; it follows Docker's reading of the flags (a flag that takes a value takes the next argument, also one that starts with `-`; a group of short flags such as `-it` is read letter by letter). Values are checked for `--label`/`-l` (no key with the prefix `nimblescape.devenv.` or `devcontainer.`, `isReservedLabel`: the CLI puts its id label before the `runArgs`, and Docker keeps the last label of a key; refused as not supported), `-e`/`--env` (the rules apply to the part of the name before the first `=`, without surrounding spaces, because Docker passes a key `GH_TOKEN=x` as `GH_TOKEN=x=<value>`, and a name with a space or a control character is refused as not supported, `refusedVariable`, hotfix GH; no variable of container-only Git, `isContainerGitVariable`: `GIT_CONFIG` and `GIT_CONFIG_*`, and the names of `containerEnvironment`, and no token or host variable of the GitHub CLI, `isGitHubCliAccountVariable`: `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN`, `GH_HOST`, refused with the reason `GITHUB_CLI_ACCOUNT_REASON` because `gh` prefers them over the sign-in in `GH_CONFIG_DIR` (user decision 2026-09-26; an `ENV` of a Dockerfile is not checked); with or without a value; `containerEnv` and `remoteEnv` of the repository configuration and of each entry of the image metadata are checked for the same names, but not those of the merged configuration, which for an existing container holds the values of the override configuration, also of an earlier version; the variables of the Dev Containers extension, the VS Code server, and GnuPG are not checked), `--env-file` (only a path below `/workspaces` without `..`: `docker run` reads it in the helper; its content is not checked, and Docker applies `-e` after it, so only variables that the override configuration does not set can come from it; like an `ENV` of an image, it can set `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN`, and `GH_HOST`, which `gh` prefers over its sign-in; Git is not affected), `--stop-timeout` (at most `MAX_STOP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` = 20, because the Session Monitor ends each Docker call after 30 seconds), `--restart` (`no`, `on-failure[:n]`), `--oom-score-adj` (0 or more), `--log-driver` (`json-file`, `local`, `none`), `--log-opt`, and `--storage-opt` (`size=`). A value flag at the end of the list is refused, because it would take the next argument that the extension adds. `--name`, `--rm`, `--restart` (review round 8, S8-6: its value is still checked first), `-i`, `-t`, and `-d` (also in groups) are removed from the `runArgs` of the override configuration with the same parser (`overrideRunArgs`), and `runUp` logs each one with its reason (`removedRunArgs`). In a container where root may not change files of the user (for example `--cap-drop ALL`), `~/.gitconfig` is written as the remote user after the run as root failed. A mount may use a volume named like the workspace volume of an environment (`ENVIRONMENT_VOLUME_PATTERN`) only when it is its own. Named volumes that belong to something else are refused (`volumeNameProblems`; by name: `foreignVolumeName`, also a name of 64 hexadecimal characters, an anonymous volume of another container): the helper's cache volume; the additional volumes of the environments of other GitHub accounts, from the registry (`foreignVolumes`; `additionalVolumes` holds the environment's labelled own volumes (`isOwnVolume`), the additional volumes of other environments of the same owner that it mounts, and, after a lost registry, the named volumes without labels that its container mounts (`reconcileFromVolumes`), and a recorded volume stays recorded; a volume that the environment recorded itself is never foreign, because after a lost registry the restored entries of two accounts can both record a volume without labels that their containers mount). Refused as well: Docker's long publish syntax (any `-p`/`--publish`/`appPort` value with `=`: it has no address), and every `volume-*` option of a mount except `volume-nocopy` and `volume-subpath` (`volume-label` could give a new volume the labels of an environment); the volumes of the Dev Containers extension by their names (`vscode`, `vsc-remote-containers`); a name that ends in a hexadecimal MD5 or SHA-256 hash (as the clones of repositories of the Dev Containers extension) only when the volume exists and is not the environment's own (a repository may use such a name: a new one is created with the labels of the environment); existing volumes by their labels (`volumeLabelOwner`: `com.docker.compose.*`, `vsch.*` and `dev.container.volume`, `com.docker.volume.anonymous`); and a volume with `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id` unless it is the environment's own (`isOwnVolume`) or an additional volume of another environment of the same owner (`isSameOwnerAdditionalVolume`: `nimblescape.devenv.volume=additional`, and `nimblescape.devenv.owner-id` is the owner of the environment, both set; `HostAccessInput.environment`), whether that environment still exists or its Delete kept the volume: the environments of one account share such a volume (for example `${localWorkspaceFolderBasename}-node_modules` of a fork and its upstream repository, or a fixed cache name), as before the labels. Refused: a volume of another owner, without an owner label, and a workspace volume (no `nimblescape.devenv.volume`); the `foreignVolumes` of other accounts are checked first. The pipeline records such a shared volume (`recordedVolumes`, in `saveConfiguration`, `createAdditionalVolumes` before `up`, and `recordContainerVolumes`) only to protect it: Delete removes a volume only when it is its own (`isOwnVolume`), no other entry records it, and no Delete of another account kept it (`removableVolumes`), so the Delete of either environment keeps it while the other records it, and the question of Delete does not list it. Before each check, the pipeline reads the labels of the named volumes of the mounts (`mountedVolumeNames`, `docker volume inspect`; no call without named volumes). `remote.localPortHost` other than `localhost` in `customizations.vscode.settings` of any source is refused, because the window applies the settings of the container (VS Code forwards on `0.0.0.0` for any other value); an image or a process in the container that writes `~/.vscode-server/data/Machine/settings.json` is not checked (container restrictions, section 10). When the merged configuration cannot be read within 10 seconds (without a container, the CLI reads the base image and the Features, from the registries when they are not local, without the credentials of the extension), the helper container of the read is removed and the configuration is read again without it (NFR-08); the image metadata check stays. A new environment image of an update that the metadata check refuses is removed, the old container or image starts with `Messages.updateRefused`, and the refusal is stored in the registry entry (`refusedUpdate`: configuration path and hash, digests, settings; the settings at most `MAX_REFUSED_ITEMS_LENGTH`, 4 KB, also when they are read: `refusedUpdateOf` truncates what a registry changed by hand holds; hotfix review 4, Q3); the same update is not built again until a digest or the configuration changes, or a forced rebuild. `${localWorkspaceFolder}` outside of `mounts` and `runArgs` still gets the message of concept RK-10.
- **Variables of the Dev Container CLI in the checks** (hotfix M1 and hotfix review 1, src/core/helper/cliVariables.ts, pure, for the Docker Compose branch too). Dev Container CLI 0.89.0 substitutes the variables of every entry of the image metadata once at `up` (`Yr`/`yG`: `config = raw.map(substitute)`, where `substitute` is `Fo` for the local variables and then `tg` for `${devcontainerId}`), and passes the resulting mounts to `docker run --mount`; `read-configuration` substitutes the configuration and the merged configuration once, and `up` substitutes the override configuration again, whose `runArgs` and `appPort` come from that output (so they are substituted twice). `substituteCliVariables` mirrors one pass: the matches of the pattern `/\$\{(.*?)\}/g`, found by a linear scan (`variableMatches`, because the pattern takes quadratic time on `${${${…`; a test compares it with the pattern on random texts) on string values (not keys), recursively, without substituting a result again in the same pass; the name is the text before the first `:`; `env`/`localEnv` take a variable of the process, else the second argument, else `''`; `localWorkspaceFolder(Basename)` and `containerWorkspaceFolder(Basename)` (the latter substituted first); other names stay; `${devcontainerId}` only when an ID is given. `hostAccessReport` and `mountedVolumeNames` substitute each metadata entry first (`resolvedInput`, `HostAccessInput.variables`); the configuration and the merged configuration are read as `read-configuration` returned them, without a second substitution. `resolveCliVariables` returns the substituted value with its leftovers: the expressions of the raw strings that the first pass keeps as written and that name a variable that the CLI resolves; they are recorded by the resolver, never found by a scan of the substituted text (hotfix review 2, P1: a result can hide one, `dst=/y${localEnv:A:$}{,src=${localEnv:TERM:x}` becomes `dst=/y${,src=${localEnv:TERM:x}`, and it can show one that the CLI passes on as text). `mountEntries` pairs each mount of a resolved metadata entry with the raw mount at the same index (the substitution keeps the structure) and takes the leftovers of the raw string, or, for the object form, the union over the raw `type`, `source`, and `target`, each on its own. Then every expression named `devcontainerId` (also with arguments) in the configuration, the merged configuration, and the resolved metadata is `DEVCONTAINER_ID_PLACEHOLDER` (`withDevcontainerIdPlaceholder`, a scan of the first-pass output as the CLI's `tg` makes it; hotfix review 2, P6): 52 characters of base 32 like the real ID, without `, = " $ { } :`, named like no volume of Dev Environments or the helper; the items name it `${devcontainerId}`, `mountedVolumeNames` skips names with it (so they are neither inspected, created, nor recorded before `up`), and a configuration or label that holds its text (as written or after the first pass) is not supported. The only scans of a text for variables that remain are those of text that the CLI passes on as it is (the mounts, `--mount`, and `-v` of the configuration and runArgs) and of the runArgs and appPort that the CLI substitutes again (`secondPassProblems`, with `SECOND_PASS_VARIABLE_NAMES`: not `containerEnv`, hotfix review 2, P4, because the CLI resolves `${containerEnv:…}` only for the lifecycle commands of an existing container). The size limits (`textLengthProblems`) run first and alone, also before `mountedVolumeNames` and so before any `docker volume inspect`; problems are deduplicated with a Map, and each list of a report, and each list of variables in an item, names at most `MAX_LISTED_ITEMS` (20) and then `and <n> more` (hotfix review 2, P2: 40,000 entries take well under a second), each at most `MAX_ITEM_LENGTH` (200) characters (`truncated`: start and end, `…` for the middle; hotfix review 3, C3-2); an item of a bind mount names its source as `shownBindSource` normalizes it (`path.posix.normalize`, `path.win32.normalize` for a drive path, a relative path keeps `./`), before the cut, so that `/a/./././…/../../x` cannot hide its real folder in the middle; the log and the registry get the same capped items (hotfix review 4, Q2). The pipeline passes `helperCliVariables(repository)` (`/workspaces/<repository>` for both folders, as `up` gets them: `--workspace-folder` and the `workspaceFolder` of the override configuration; `localWorkspaceFolder` is the repository folder for every repository name, as `up` uses it (hotfix review 5, A5-1): `read-configuration` (`Ri(S)`) and the build (`Ri(H)`) read a folder whose `path.posix.extname` is exactly `.code-workspace` as a workspace file and use its parent folder, `/workspaces`, but `up` (`kW`) reads `<folder>/<basename>` as the workspace file, whose parent is the folder itself, and substitutes the label, the user of the image, and the mounts with it; so a repository `x.code-workspace` gets `/workspaces/x.code-workspace` and `${localWorkspaceFolderBasename}` is `x.code-workspace` at `up`; cliVariables.test.ts runs the verbatim `kW` expression and `Ri`/`Rp` of the bundle for `x.code-workspace`, `a.b.code-workspace`, `.code-workspace`, `plain`, `x.CODE-WORKSPACE`, and `a.code-workspace.git`); `HOME=/root`, `HELPER_KNOWN_ENV`, because the helper runs as root: no `USER` in resources/helper/Dockerfile and no `--user` in `batchRunArgs`) in `hostAccessInput` and in `runUp` for the volumes that it creates before `up`, so the volume checks, the labels read before the check, and the additional volumes use the names that Docker gets. The extension does not know the rest of the environment of the CLI process, so the choice is conservative: another variable that may be set in the helper (`HELPER_PROCESS_ENV_NAMES`: `HELPER_ENV_NAMES`, `PWD`, `OLDPWD`, `SHLVL`, `_`, `TERM`, and the proxy variables of the Docker CLI) stays as written, every other one gets its default or `''` (the pipeline passes no variable to the CLI runs). After the substitution, a mount (`mounts`, `--mount`) or a `-v` with a variable that the CLI resolves still in it (`unresolvedCliVariables`: such a variable of the helper, `${containerEnv:…}`, a workspace folder that is not known, `${env}` without a name) is refused as not supported, whatever the switch says, also a bind mount (a default such as `${localEnv:TERM:type=volume}` adds a field; an object mount is read and named as the `--mount` text `type=…,src=…,dst=…` that the CLI makes of it); `${devcontainerId}` (an opaque ID, never the name of an environment) and unknown names stay as written (Docker refuses a `$` in a volume name), and `mountedVolumeNames` skips such names. An entry of `runArgs` or `appPort` of the configuration or the merged configuration that still holds such a variable (with or without a value in the helper, `secondPassProblems`) is not supported, whatever the switch says: then the `runArgs` and `appPort` that the policy checks are what Docker gets, `overrideRunArgs` and `loopbackAppPorts` commute with the second substitution, and `runArgsUser`, `runArgsDecideHostname`, `imageRemoteUser`, and `prepareOwnership` read the values that Docker gets; `imageRemoteUser` also substitutes the users of the label as the CLI does at `up`, and gives no user when the one that decides has a leftover (hotfix review 2, P3; section "Ownership"). The final check before `up` covers the `runArgs` and the `appPort` of the override configuration. Texts longer than `MAX_CLI_TEXT_LENGTH` (256 KB), or more than `MAX_CLI_SOURCE_LENGTH` (1 MB) of text in the configuration, the merged configuration, or the image metadata (`textLengths`, without recursion), are not supported. cliVariables.test.ts runs a verbatim copy of the substitution functions of the installed CLI, checks that the bundle still contains it, and compares a table of cases and random texts. 
- **Host access policy: the switch per repository** (concept sections 8 and 9, user request 2026-09-26; src/core/policy/hostAccessChecks.ts). `parseHostAccessChecksOff` reads the user setting `devEnvLauncher.hostAccessChecksOff` (`readSettings` reads only its `globalValue` through `inspect`; the manifest gives it the scope `application`): entries are trimmed, compared without case, and entries that are no `owner/name` are ignored with one warning (`warnInvalidHostAccessChecksOff`, again only when they change). `hostAccessChecks(repository, settings)` gives `'on' | 'off'`; the pipeline reads it once at the start of each open (`PipelineContext.hostAccessChecks`, a warning line in the log when off) and passes it as `checks` to `checkContainer(stage, input)` (src/core/policy/index.ts), for the configuration, the `runArgs` of the override configuration, the image metadata before `up`, and the Docker Compose model. Each refused item has a class (`HostAccessClass`, `hostAccessClassification`): `computer` (lifted when off), `protected` (account separation, the identity of the owner account, the integrity of the extension, and unclear items; reported with `Messages.hostAccess`), and `unsupported`; an item that two rules name keeps the class that is not lifted. With the checks off, `overrideRunArgs(runArgs, false)` keeps the published ports as written, `buildOverrideConfig` passes `appPort` unchanged and adds `--label nimblescape.devenv.host-access=unrestricted` (`HOST_ACCESS_UNRESTRICTED_LABEL`, allowed in `runArgs` like the other two labels of the override configuration, because the merged configuration of such a container holds it). `containerIsCurrent(labels, configKnown, checks)` counts a container with that label as not current while the checks are on, so `startContainer` creates it again from the environment image after the image metadata check (`Messages.containerHostAccessChecksOn`); the merged configuration of such a container is not checked then (it holds what the checks allowed while they were off; the container is created again anyway, and the image metadata is checked before `up`). A refused configuration or image stops the open, and the container is not started; the controller (`containerOutdated`) closes the connection of a window attached to such a container (`ControllerTexts.unrestrictedContainerClosed`). `RefusedUpdate.hostAccessChecks` is `'off'` for a refusal while the checks were off; `isRefusedUpdate` matches only the same state. The commands `devEnvironments.turnOffHostAccessChecks` (modal warning, `Messages.hostAccessChecksOffDetail`) and `devEnvironments.turnOnHostAccessChecks` write the user setting (`ConfigurationTarget.Global`, `withHostAccessChecks`); the rows carry the flag `hostAccessChecked` or `hostAccessUnrestricted` in `contextValue`, and the description marker `host access unrestricted`. Docker Compose (unit 6) is to use the same `hostAccessChecks` and pass the boolean the same way.
- **Where the policy lives** (unit 11, user decision 2026-09-26 on the request "Ideally it is only one function / small module that centers everything"). All of the container policy is in `src/core/policy/` (pure, no I/O, no `vscode`), with one entry point: `checkContainer(stage, input)` in `index.ts` gives one report (`HostAccessReport`: `hostAccess` and `unsupported`) for the stages `configuration` (devcontainer.json and its merged configuration; for Docker Compose without the properties that the CLI ignores, `withoutComposeIgnored`), `imageMetadata` (the label `devcontainer.metadata` of the environment image), `finalRunArgs` (the `runArgs` and `appPort` of the override configuration, as Docker gets them), and `composeModel` (the merged Compose model and the settings of devcontainer.json that Compose does not support). `input.checks` is the per-repository switch (`hostAccessChecks.ts`): `off` lifts only the class *computer*. The pipeline runs `checkContainer` in the analysis worker (`runAnalysisJob` of `helper/configurationAnalysis.ts`; the job `hostAccess` names the stage) and calls the module in one line per step: `isRefused`, `describeRefusal`, `addRefusedItems` (image labels, image IDs), `cappedReport`, `imageReferencesToInspect`/`inspectedImageItems` (the Docker inspect stays in the pipeline). The files: `rules.ts` (the rules table: classes, protected paths incl. the internal folder, `/run/devenv`, `/var/run/devenv`, the Docker socket, and the kernel folders; allowed capabilities and security options; reserved labels; the variables of container-only Git and the GitHub CLI; log, restart, and stop limits), `flags.ts` (`RUN_FLAGS`, `BUILD_FLAGS` with the checks of their values), `dockerFlags.ts` (the parsers: `parseFlags`, `csvFields`, `networkNames`, mounts, `-v` values, ports), `single.ts` (a single container), `compose.ts` (a Compose model), `volumes.ts` (volume ownership: `isOwnVolume`, `isSameOwnerAdditionalVolume`, foreign volumes, networks of other environments), `images.ts` (image references and labels), `rewrites.ts` (`overrideRunArgs`, `loopbackRunArgs`, `removedRunArgs`, `withoutNameArgs`, `runArgsDecideHostname`, and `decideServiceMount`/`decideServicePort`, which the Compose model rewrite in `helper/compose.ts` applies), `report.ts` (the report, classes, `capped`). `helper/compose.ts` keeps building the models that run (labels, names, pull policy, volumes, the dev image, the token tmpfs); the model types and names that the policy needs are in `helper/composeModel.ts`, so that the imports have no cycle.
- **Host access analysis in a worker thread** (review round 8, the structural fix of the parser DoS class): every host access analysis of the pipeline (`hostAccessReport`, `composeAccessReport`, `singleImageReferences`, `composeImageReferences`, and the references of the image check `collectReferences`/`composeReferences` with `extractBaseImages`; the Dockerfile analysis `dockerfileImageFindings`/`analyzeDockerfileImages` that ran here too was removed 2026-09-27, see the Dockerfile refusals removed note) runs as a job (`AnalysisJob` in `src/core/helper/configurationAnalysis.ts`: `hostAccess`, `single`, `compose`) through `EnvironmentServiceDeps.analyzer`. The worker passes `WorkerConfigurationAnalyzer` (`configurationAnalysisRunner.ts`) with the text of the script of the thread (`src/core/helper/configurationAnalysisWorker.ts`, in the worker's bundle as the module `devenv:analysis-script`, plan step 11E2; until plan step 11F1 the extension ran it from its own bundle `dist/configurationAnalysisWorker.js`): one worker thread per job, `resourceLimits` of 256 MB old generation, 32 MB young generation, and 4 MB stack, and a time limit of 10 s (`ANALYSIS_LIMITS`). V8 ignores `resourceLimits` when the process runs with `--max-old-space-size` (for example in `NODE_OPTIONS`), so the runner also watches the heap of the worker every 50 ms (`Worker.getHeapStatistics`, Node 22.16 and newer; before, the growth of the RSS of the process by twice the limit) and stops it beyond the same limit. On a timeout, a memory limit, a crash, an exit, an error of the analysis, a job that cannot be passed, or an answer that is not a result (`isAnalysisResult`), the worker is terminated and the job resolves `analysisFailure`: the configuration is refused as not supported with `ANALYSIS_FAILED_ITEM` (message `Messages.configurationTooComplex`: "The configuration is too large or too complex to check (it took too long or used too much memory). Change the configuration of the repository."), and the log names the reason; never allowed on a failure. Review round 9 (P9-1, P9-2): the result carries `failure` (`AnalysisFailure`): `limit` (the time limit of a running worker, the memory limits, a stack overflow of the analysis) and (review round 10, P10-3) `size` (a job whose texts exceed `MAX_ANALYSIS_JOB_CHARACTERS`, 32 M characters, counted by `exceedsJobSize` before `postMessage` clones it, and the caps of the pipeline, `tooLargeError`) keep `ANALYSIS_FAILED_ITEM`; `internal` (the worker did not start, also one that is not running when the time is up, ended or crashed without an answer, threw an error, or answered with something else) refuses with `analysisInternalItem` ("The configuration check failed to start (internal error): <reason>", message `Messages.configurationCheckInternal`). The pipeline throws `AnalysisFailedError` (a `HostAccessError`: still refused). A failed check of the new image of an update is not remembered as a refused update (`Messages.updateCheckFailed`, the old environment starts, the next open tries again); review round 10 (P10-3): except for `size`, which the same update always fails (remembered, `Messages.updateTooLarge`). An internal failure does not block an existing environment: the load of the configuration counts as a configuration that cannot be read (an existing single container starts as it is and nothing is built; a Docker Compose environment does not start (`startFailed`, "could not be checked", no `docker start` fallback, user decision 2026-09-29), except that when the dev container runs already and no container of the environment must be created again, the dev container opens as it is, D-22; review round 18 of PR #64, R18-1; review round 23 of PR #64, A-R23-1), and the check of the runArgs is skipped only when `up` creates no container; a new container or build stays refused. Plan step 11F1: the extension runs no analysis and ships no bundle of the thread. The limits of the parser stay the first line: `MAX_DOCKERFILE_LENGTH`, `MAX_DOCKERFILE_INSTRUCTIONS`, `MAX_EXPANDED_CHARACTERS` (16 M characters of all expansions of one Dockerfile, S8-2), and the parser directives without backtracking (S8-3), now of `extractBaseImages` only, which then gives no images for that Dockerfile (never a refusal); the duplicates of the findings by a Map (S8-5). Removed 2026-09-27 with the Dockerfile analysis (see the Dockerfile refusals removed note): `MAX_PATTERN_STEPS` (S8-1), the refusal of a Dockerfile as too complex to check, and `withDockerfileCache` (S8-4). A Dockerfile longer than `MAX_DOCKERFILE_LENGTH` is still refused as not supported (a size limit, not a check of its content: the configuration hash sees only the text that was read, U1). The tests of the pipeline use `inProcessAnalyzer` (the same jobs in the test thread). Plan step 11E2: the worker (§17) carries the script of that thread in its own bundle (the module `devenv:analysis-script` of scripts/workerScripts.mjs, beside the monitor's) and starts each job from its text (`new Worker(text, { eval: true })`, WorkerConfigurationAnalyzer with `{ code }`) with the same limits; the pipeline of every worker operation gets it (`workerAnalyzer`, WorkerServicesDeps.analyzer), so a configuration the worker reads is analysed there, and refused when the analysis fails.

## 8. Dev Container CLI calls (in the helper)

| Step | Call |
|---|---|
| Read configuration | `devcontainer read-configuration --workspace-folder /workspaces/<repo> --config <config path> --id-label nimblescape.devenv.environment-id=<id> --include-merged-configuration` (at most 10 seconds; then again without `--include-merged-configuration`) |
| Build environment image | `devcontainer build --workspace-folder /workspaces/<repo> --config <config path> --image-name <name>:<n>`, then the labels of the image by a commit in the worker (`EngineDocker.labelImage`, section 5; before plan step 11B3a, `docker build --quiet -t <name>:<n> --label … -` with `FROM <name>:<n>`) |
| Create or start container | `devcontainer up --workspace-folder /workspaces/<repo> --override-config <override file> --id-label nimblescape.devenv.environment-id=<id> --user-data-folder /devenv-cache --update-remote-user-uid-default never --skip-post-create --skip-post-attach` (plus `--remove-existing-container` when the container is replaced) |
| Lifecycle commands (after `up` and the token) | `devcontainer run-user-commands --workspace-folder /workspaces/<repo> --override-config <override file> --id-label nimblescape.devenv.environment-id=<id> --container-id <containerId of up> --user-data-folder /devenv-cache --skip-post-attach` (the same helper script, override configuration, and for Docker Compose model and `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME` as `up`) |

- The override configuration is the complete configuration for `up` (the CLI uses it instead of the repository configuration). It contains only: `image`, `workspaceMount`, `workspaceFolder`, `runArgs` (repository values without `--name`, `--rm`, `-i`, `-t`, and `-d`, read with the parser of the policy and named in the log, `-p`/`--publish` without an address with `127.0.0.1:`, or only `--label nimblescape.devenv.container-config=unknown` without a readable configuration; plus `--label nimblescape.devenv.container-version=1`, `--name <container name>`, and last (unit 15) `--tmpfs /run/devenv:rw,nosuid,nodev,noexec,size=1m,mode=0700`), `appPort` (a number `n` as `127.0.0.1:n:n`, a string without address with `127.0.0.1` in front, for example `8080:80` → `127.0.0.1:8080:80` and `80` → `127.0.0.1::80`; a string with a loopback address stays; the long syntax with `=` is refused), `containerEnv` and `remoteEnv`, `customizations.vscode.settings` (`devContainersSettings`), `shutdownAction: "none"` (concept 7.6). `initializeCommand` is never passed (the policy refuses it). Everything else comes from the label `devcontainer.metadata` of the environment image; the CLI appends the properties of the override configuration as the last entry of the label of the container.
- `customizations.vscode.settings`, flat keys, as the Dev Containers extension 0.470.0 reads the settings of a container (class `kv`: the new key `dev.containers.*`, for some settings then the old key `remote.containers.*`): `dev.containers.copyGitConfig` and `remote.containers.copyGitConfig` `false` (a `false` under the new key alone falls back to the old key), `dev.containers.gitCredentialHelperConfigLocation` `none`, `dev.containers.dockerCredentialHelper` `false`, and `dev.containers.githubCLILoginWithToken` `false` (these two only under the new key). The Dev Containers extension writes the settings of all entries of the label into `~/.vscode-server/data/Machine/settings.json` at the first attach of a new container, the later entries winning (to verify in V-8). The CLI collects `customizations` of all entries, so the extensions and other settings of the repository stay.
- `containerEnv` and `remoteEnv` (merged over those of the image metadata), only documented variables of Git, Docker, and the GitHub CLI: `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/workspaces/.devenv+/gitconfig`, `DOCKER_CONFIG=/workspaces/.devenv+/docker`, `GH_CONFIG_DIR=/run/devenv/gh` (unit 15: in the tmpfs of the token; a path, no secret), `GIT_SSH_COMMAND=ssh -o IdentityAgent=none`, and four Git settings of the command line level as `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=4` with `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_n`/`GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_n` (Git 2.31 and newer): `credential.helper=` (removes every credential helper of the configuration files, among them a forwarding helper that the Dev Containers extension writes with `git config --system` and `--global` when its settings of the container do not apply), `include.path=/workspaces/.devenv+/credentials.gitconfig` (the helpers of the user for other hosts), `credential.https://github.com.helper=` (removes them again for github.com), and `credential.https://github.com.helper=<helper of the container>`. Older Git gets the same through the `~/.gitconfig` of the extension. By user decision (2026-09-25), no variable of the Dev Containers extension or the VS Code server is set, blanked, or changed (`SSH_AUTH_SOCK`, `REMOTE_CONTAINERS_IPC`, `REMOTE_CONTAINERS*`, `BROWSER`, `VSCODE_*`), and neither `GNUPGHOME` nor `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`; their sockets stay usable by a process that looks for them (concept section 9, limits). No token in any variable (no `GH_TOKEN`, no `GITHUB_TOKEN`).
- `--skip-post-attach`: the Dev Containers extension runs `postAttachCommand` when it attaches (to verify in V-1).
- Lifecycle token (user decision 2026-09-27, `runUserCommandsArgs`, `WorkspaceHelper.runUserCommands`, `runUserCommands` of the pipeline): `up --skip-post-create` (CLI 0.89.0: `postCreateEnabled: false`, so `setupInContainer` still patches `/etc/environment` and `/etc/profile` but runs no lifecycle hook, probes no user environment, and writes no marker). Then the token goes into the container (section 7), then `run-user-commands` runs, in `runUp` and `runComposeUp`, so on every path of `up` (first open, rebuild, image update, switch of the configuration or of the kind, the restore after a failed update, Try again). The CLI reads the configuration as `up` does (the same `--workspace-folder`, `--override-config`, and `--id-label`, merged with the label `devcontainer.metadata` of the container), takes the container by `--container-id` (with Docker Compose every container of the project has the id label), probes the user environment, and runs `onCreateCommand`, `updateContentCommand`, `postCreateCommand` (each only when its marker `~/.devcontainer/.<hook>Marker` of the remote user differs from the creation time of the container), and `postStartCommand` (marker with the start time), stopping at the first failure; `postAttachCommand` is skipped (`--skip-post-attach`). Not used: `--skip-non-blocking-commands` and `--prebuild` (both stop early; `up` ran all commands), `--stop-for-personalization`, dotfiles, `--remote-env`, `--secrets-file`, `--container-data-folder` (`up` uses none of them). So `up --skip-post-create` plus `run-user-commands` runs exactly what `up` ran. A running container gets no `up` and so no `run-user-commands` (as before, `postStartCommand` does not run for it). Every start of a stopped environment runs through `up` and `run-user-commands`: there is no `docker start` fallback without the helper (user decision 2026-09-29; see section 7, *No previous helper image*); only the stopped side services of a running Docker Compose dev container still start with `docker start` (D-22). The Dev Containers extension finds the markers when it attaches. Its result is `{ outcome: 'success', result: 'done' }`, or for a failed command exit code 1 and `{ outcome: 'error', message, description: '… from … failed.' }` without `containerId`: the helper adds the container ID it passed, so a failed command whose container runs is kept with the existing warning, and one whose container is gone fails the open (startFailed, Try again) as for `up`; other failures count as failures of `up`. The progress shows no new step (the commands run in "Starting environment", as inside `up` before). Cost: one more helper run per open with `up` (a helper container, the start of the CLI, the configuration read, `docker inspect`), about 1 to 3 seconds; the environment probe and the commands only move.
- `build` and `up` get `--user-data-folder /devenv-cache` (the cache volume; `read-configuration` does not get it). CLI 0.89.0 keeps there only its own data, for example an empty folder for builds, not the downloaded Features: it puts them into a new folder below the helper's temporary folder at each run, which is removed with the helper container, so a first build or a rebuild with Features needs internet access. `up` also gets `--update-remote-user-uid-default never`, because the CLI runs as root in the helper and would otherwise build an extra image at each container creation. `build` gets `--no-lockfile` unless the repository has the lockfile of its configuration (`devcontainer-lock.json`, or `.devcontainer-lock.json` next to a configuration named `.devcontainer.json`), so the build does not add a file to the repository. Every build runs through WRITE_AND_RUN_SCRIPT, which takes the lockfile only as a plain file of the repository after links (the CLI runs as root and reads and writes it): a link out of the repository, a FIFO, or a lockfile longer than MAX_LOCKFILE_LENGTH fails the build before the CLI starts. The scripts resolve every link as the system does (`realpath(3)`, never a `..` of a link target as text).
- `build` and `up` run with `BUILDX_BAKE_ENTITLEMENTS_FS=0` (user decision of 2026-10-09; `BAKE_FS_ENTITLEMENTS_OFF` in `src/core/helper/batchSteps.ts`, set after the variables of the request, which cannot name `BUILDX_*`): Buildx 0.37.2 (advisory GHSA-gwr2-q96m-6682) refuses a Docker Compose build through bake whose Dockerfile lies outside the build context (`additional privileges requested: --allow=fs.read=…`), and the CLI writes its Dockerfile with the Features into a folder of its own outside the context; Docker Compose grants only the contexts (docker/compose#14285). The variable turns off bake's checks `fs.read`, `fs.write` and `ssh` (the forwarding of the default SSH agent); `network.host`, `security.insecure`, devices and the removal of a local output stay checked, so these steps build as every build did before Buildx 0.37.2. In the checked mode the host access policy keeps every path of a Compose build in the repository (section 15: the contexts and Dockerfiles, the additional contexts and the cache imports as Buildx reads them, review round 1 of PR #130) and refuses `ssh`, `secrets` and `entitlements`; the helper has no SSH agent. With the checks off, the user allowed access to the computer. Whatever the switch says, the policy refuses those values when they hold `${` or `%{`: bake reads the build definition of Docker Compose as HCL and evaluates them as a template, which Compose escapes only in the build arguments and `dockerfile_inline` (review round 2 of PR #130, `bakeTemplateProblems`). The other steps keep the checks.
- The last line of standard output of `build` and `up` is a JSON result (`outcome`, `imageName`, `containerId`, `remoteUser`, `remoteWorkspaceFolder`). All other output goes to the output channel.
- When a lifecycle command fails, `up` (now `run-user-commands`, see above) exits with code 1 and the result `{ outcome: 'error', containerId, description: '… from … failed.' }` (to verify in V-10). If that container runs, the helper keeps it, and the open continues with a warning, as with the Dev Containers extension. The remote user then comes from the label `devcontainer.metadata` of the image.
- **Recreate offer** (user request 2026-09-26; `offerRecreation`, `upFailedForContainer`, `runningContainerFault`, `recreationImage` of the pipeline, `isContainerFault` in pipelineRules.ts, `PipelineUi.recreateContainer`). An existing container of the current setup (not one that the pipeline creates again anyway: outdated, missing, or after a build) that is damaged gets a modal question instead of a dead end. Where it is asked: (1) `up` of a stopped container, or the `run-user-commands` after it, fails with a `DevcontainerCommandError` of the CLI that is no failed lifecycle command (`lifecycleHookFailure`); (2) a running container: before it is opened as it is (for Docker Compose, the dev container), `docker exec -u <the user of the container> <id> /bin/sh -c 'exit 0'` (the entry `check` of the script registry, section 6; 15 s; review round 3, F1: the user is that of the container's own label `devcontainer.metadata`, by which the Dev Containers extension attaches: its last `remoteUser`, else `containerUser`, substituted as `imageRemoteUser` does, `containerMetadataUser`; never the user that the registry recorded, which can be of an earlier container after an open that was cancelled between `up` and its end; when the label names no user, or one with a value that is not known, there is no check and no offer) fails (the Dev Containers extension could not attach). A failed `up` of the CLI leaves the container running; the next open then offers again only when this check finds the damage: a container whose own user (of its label) is the damaged one fails the check, and a container whose own user (the one it runs as) is missing from `/etc/passwd` does not start at all, so the next `up` fails again and offers through (1); a container that was damaged while it ran and whose label names no user is not checked and gets no offer. Both only when the text names damage of the container itself (`isContainerFault`, an allowlist verified on Docker 29.3.1 with runc 1.3: `unable to find user|group …: no matching entries in passwd|group file` (also when `/etc/passwd` is gone), `unable to start container process: … exec: "/…": stat …: no such file or directory` or `…: permission denied` (the shell of the CLI is missing or not executable, at `docker start` or `docker exec`), `is marked for removal and cannot be started`), the text names no failure of the connection to Docker (`Cannot connect to the Docker daemon`, `error during connect`, `ssh: `, a lost connection) or of the network (`isNetworkFailure`), and `docker info` (`isRunning`, pinned to the Docker context of the operation like every Docker call of it) still answers afterwards. Docker Compose: the fault must be the dev container's (the container of `service`, label `com.docker.compose.service`): after a failed `up`, the check of (2) must find it in the dev container, which the failed `up` left running (`composeDevContainerFault`); a dev container that does not run, or whose check passes, means that the fault cannot be tied to it (for example another service that cannot start), and nothing is offered. Review round 1 (D2): without its dev container, the CLI runs `docker compose up` without `--no-recreate` (CLI 0.89.0 passes it only when the dev container exists; `--expect-existing-container` fails without one: "The expected container does not exist."), so Compose would recreate every other service whose configuration hash or image changed. The offer therefore also needs (`composeServicesStay`): the model of this run is the one of the build record (`UpdatePlan.current`, no build: no changed configuration, no newer image, also none that was refused; not after "Rebuild later" and not in the fallback after a failed update), the Compose version of the build record, no other service with a `build:` section that `up` would build (review round 4, H1, `builtOtherServices`: without its dev container, the CLI runs `docker compose build` of every service with `build:`, or of `runServices` and the dev service, before `up -d` without `--no-recreate`; a new image of such a service, for example after a change of its build context, which the configuration hash does not cover, or a pruned build cache, would make Compose create its container again after the direct check, and a failed build, for example offline, would end `up` after the dev container is gone; `requireOtherServicesKept` refuses the same), no other service that shares a namespace or the volumes of another service (review round 3, G2, `sharedNamespaceServices`: `network_mode`, `ipc`, or `pid` of the form `service:<name>`, or `volumes_from`; Compose hashes such a reference in its resolved form `container:<id>`, so the direct check below could never pass, and a new dev container could make Compose create such a service again), and each container of another service carries in `com.docker.compose.image` the ID of the local image of its reference now; otherwise the log says why, and nothing is offered. These are only pre-checks against a pointless question (review round 2: a record that a changed configuration rewrote without new containers passes the version check, and the host access checks and a tag that moves while the question is open are not covered). The safety gate is the direct check right before `up` (review round 2, E1–E3, `requireOtherServicesKept`): after the busy mark and `requireUnchangedSinceQuestion`, in `runComposeUp` after its checks and before anything is removed, the Compose of the workspace helper (the one that runs `up`) computes the configuration hash of each service from exactly the up model of this `up` (`WorkspaceHelper.composeServiceHashes`, COMPOSE_HASH_SCRIPT: the model at COMPOSE_MODEL_PATH, `docker compose --project-name <project> --profile '*' -f <model> config --hash '*'`, which needs no engine and no network: the step `composeHash` of the batch helper, as the owner of the repository, so without the Docker socket unless root owns the repository, section 17; review round 3, G1: all profiles, so that a service with `profiles:` that runServices starts has its hash too); each container of another service of the project that the model has (not a one-off container) must carry that hash in `com.docker.compose.config-hash`, and in `com.docker.compose.image` the ID of the local image of its service now.
