The hashes of one Compose binary cover a changed model (the host access checks, E1), another Compose version of the containers (E2), and the image ID a tag that moved (E3). A difference, a missing label, or an error: `startFailed` with `Messages.composeServicesWouldBeRecreated` (the log names each service and why), and nothing is changed. Verified with Compose 5.1.1 (test/docker/composeHash.test.ts): the hash needs no engine, does not depend on the folder of the model file, equals the label of the container that `up` created from the same model, changes with a label of the service, and an `up` after the removal of the dev container keeps the other container when they match. Never asked, the existing message stays: Docker not running or not reachable (also a remote host), a refusal of the host access policy, a cancel, a build or pull failure (buildAndReplace, and its fallback to the previous container), the wrong Docker host, no sign-in, a busy environment, a network failure, a published port in use, a missing bind mount source, any other error of the helper or of Docker around the CLI, a failed lifecycle command, without the workspace helper (`helperUnavailable`: a running current container opens as it is, otherwise `helperFailed`; review round 14 of PR #64, R14-2), while the configuration is of the other kind than the environment (`kindKept`), a Docker Compose environment whose configuration cannot be read (no model), and without the environment image (a recreation would need a build). When unsure, it is not asked. The question (`Messages.containerRecreateQuestion`, detail `containerRecreateDetail`, button **Recreate**, the modal adds Cancel) says what is kept (the repository with uncommitted changes, unpushed commits, and stashes, and every file in the volumes) and what is lost (everything else in the container: installed packages, changes to the system, files outside `/workspaces` and the volumes such as the home folder; for Docker Compose it says that only the dev container is recreated, and the other services and all volumes stay), and that onCreateCommand and postCreateCommand run again. No Git safety check precedes it: the volume stays, so no repository work is lost (the safety check of Delete is for the removal of the volume). Volumes without a name of the damaged container (review round 2, V1; `unnamedVolumeFolders`: a volume whose name is 64 hexadecimal digits, for example `- /workspaces/api/node_modules` of a compose file, `VOLUME /data` of the image, a mount without a source) are not carried over: the new container gets new ones (as the image has them), and the old ones stay as unused volumes (`docker rm -f` without `-v`). The question and the progress name their folders (`Messages.containerRecreateDetail`, `containerRecreatedDamaged`), and the texts promise only the named volumes. Carrying them over was not done: such a volume has no labels of the environment, so the host access policy does not count it as the environment's own (an exception would weaken the policy), and how the CLI merges an extra mount with a mount of the image metadata at the same target is not verified. A single container that is created again while the configuration cannot be read (review round 3) gets no runArgs (also none of their mounts) and no published ports, as any container created without the configuration: the question says so (`containerRecreateDetail(…, withoutConfiguration)`), and the log has the usual warning; it is created again once the configuration can be read. Cancel (or a dismissed question): `startFailed` with the cause and "nothing was changed"; nothing is stopped or removed. Recreate: the busy mark `rebuild` (a live mark of another window ends the open as busy), then (review round 1, D1: the question holds no mark) the container of the environment is looked up again (`requireUnchangedSinceQuestion`): only when it is still the damaged one (the same ID; for Docker Compose the same dev container) and the image of the recreation (of the build record of the current entry, else of the container) is still the one chosen before the question does it go on; otherwise `startFailed` with `Messages.containerChangedMeanwhile`, and nothing is changed; the progress detail `Messages.containerRecreatedDamaged`, then as the recreation of a container of an older setup, from the environment image (of the build record, else of the container) without a build: `runUp(…, removeExistingContainer: true, createsContainer: true)`, so the image metadata is checked and the additional volumes are created before `up --remove-existing-container`; the CLI removes the container with `docker rm -f` (without `-v`). For Docker Compose (`PipelineContext.recreateDevContainer`), `runComposeUp` removes only the dev container (stopped first when it runs, `docker rm -f` without `-v`) after its checks and right before `up`, which creates just that one again; the containers of the other services (for example a database with its data in a volume without a name) keep running untouched. A refusal of a check leaves the dev container as it is. No volume is ever removed; a failure of the recreation is `startFailed`, except a `helperFailed` (the helper image of the open is gone, R13-1), a refusal of the host access policy, missing files, or a cancel, which keep their own error (review round 15 of PR #64, R15-1); the next open creates the missing container. The first heartbeat to a remote Session Monitor was sent before (prepareHelper), so the recreation needs nothing more there. A container that runs and is damaged in another way than the allowlist (for example a broken home folder) is opened as before. Accepted cost (coordinator decision 2026-09-27): the check of (2) is one more `docker exec` at each open of a running container (about 100 ms locally, more on a remote host).
- The config hash (`configHash`) is `sha256` of the text of `devcontainer.json` plus the text of the Dockerfile, if there is one. The whole Dockerfile: the helper reads at most `MAX_DOCKERFILE_LENGTH` + 1 characters, and a longer Dockerfile (single container, or any service of Docker Compose with a local build) is refused as not supported (U1). The Dockerfile of a Compose service other than the dev service that could not be read and is no link out of the repository (which is refused, `localPathProblems`) is not in the hash: the update check skips it.

## 9. Image check

- **References** (concept 7.7): `image`; each `FROM` of the Dockerfile (with `ARG` values from `build.args` and from `ARG` defaults; without references to earlier stages and without `scratch`); each Feature key that is an OCI reference. Local Features (`./…`), tarball URLs, and references with `@sha256:` are not checked.
- **Normalization:** Docker Hub is `registry-1.docker.io`, official images get the prefix `library/`, and the default tag is `latest`.
- **Request:** `HEAD /v2/<name>/manifests/<tag>` with the `Accept` types for OCI index, Docker manifest list, Docker manifest v2, and OCI manifest. The digest comes from the header `Docker-Content-Digest`.
- **Authentication:** first without credentials. On `401`, read `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer realm=…,service=…,scope=…` and request a token, with Basic credentials if Docker has credentials for this registry (`~/.docker/config.json`: `auths`, `credsStore`, `credHelpers`; credential helpers through `docker-credential-<name> get`). For `ghcr.io`, the fallback is the GitHub session with the scope `read:packages`, requested only when needed (concept 7.7). The pull of such an image (in the worker's pipeline, `EngineDocker.pullImage`; the operation `pull` of plan step 10A is gone since plan step 11I1, PR B1) uses the same session: the extension answers the worker's `secret registry` with Docker's stored login first, else for `ghcr.io` with the GitHub session (never with a dialog), and the worker sends it only in the header `X-Registry-Auth`. An operation runs only on an engine behind a local socket or SSH (any other endpoint is refused: `dockerEndpointUnsupported`), so the session never goes over another connection (plan step 11I, PR D: the former fallback to a pull without the session and its error `unencryptedDockerConnection` are removed; PR #125 review round 1, A L-1).
- **Time limit:** all requests of one connection run in parallel under one `AbortController` with 5 seconds (NFR-08). If one registry does not answer, the whole update step is skipped (FR-13).
- **Proxy:** the check runs in the worker (plan step 11E3a), whose HTTPS goes through the proxy of the Docker daemon of its engine (decision C1 of 2026-10-05: HttpsProxy and NoProxy of `docker info`, `src/core/proxyTransport.ts`), never through the proxy of the computer or of VS Code (before, the Node.js `https` module of the extension host, to which VS Code applies its proxy settings).
- **Build record:** it stores the digests that the check read right before the build. The comparison uses the build record, not the local images (concept 7.7).

## 10. Git operations

| Purpose | Commands (with `docker exec` in a running dev container, as its user; user decision 2026-10-02: no helper runs them, also not for Delete) |
|---|---|
| Branch | `git symbolic-ref -q HEAD`, a branch only below `refs/heads/` (its exit code 1 is a detached HEAD; a HEAD that names a remote-tracking branch or a tag is no branch; review round 1 of PR #124) |
| Uncommitted files | `git status --porcelain` (count of lines) |
| Unpushed commits | `git rev-list --count HEAD --branches --not --remotes` (HEAD and all local branches, concept 7.5 / 7.14 step 1; with an unborn HEAD only `--branches`) |
| Stashes | `git stash list` (count of lines) |

Plan step 11I (PR B; section 0 of the plan, one function per fact): two scripts of the script registry (section 6) run them: `gitSummary` (`GIT_SUMMARY_SCRIPT`, all four, after an open or a stop, before Delete and the release of a window) and `branch` (`GIT_BRANCH_SCRIPT`, the branch alone: the refresh, an attached window, the branch after a later open; `readBranch`). Both start with `GIT_SCRIPT_PRELUDE` (the C locale, exit code 127 without Git, `safe.directory=*`, no hooks, no fsmonitor, no optional locks, `log.showSignature=false`) and read the branch with the one shell function `GIT_BRANCH_FUNCTION`. The branch script ends with the exit code of Git when it fails (`readBranch`: `undefined`; `null` for a detached HEAD); the summary keeps going with an empty branch then, and its counts decide whether it fails. Before, the refresh ran `git -c safe.directory=* -C <folder> branch --show-current`, which found no branch with a Git before 2.22.

## 11. Connection to the container

- Folder URI: `vscode-remote://attached-container+<hex of {"containerName":"/<container name>"}><remoteWorkspaceFolder>` (to verify in V-2). For an environment on another Docker host the JSON also names its Docker context, `{"containerName":"/<name>","settings":{"context":"<context>"}}`, the form that Dev Containers' own attach writes (user report 2026-09-28): without it, the first `docker inspect` of the Dev Containers extension asks the local Docker, not the current context, and the attach fails with "it no longer exists". The context is the one of the running operation on that host (none when DOCKER_HOST decides); outside an operation the one in the window's own authority for the same container, then the current context on that host, then the context of the host (`ensureRemoteContext`, created when missing). A window counts as having the folder open (reload instead of open) only with the same context. Each window records the context of its authority in its status file (`dockerContext`), and "open in the other window" uses exactly that one (none for a window without one), so VS Code finds the window. A restored window keeps the context of its authority. Accepted leftover: outside an operation, when the current context cannot be read and the window names none, the context is the one that `findRemoteContext(host)` finds (none when it does not exist); the next open from the sidebar names the context of its operation.
- Open with `vscode.openFolder` and `forceNewWindow: false`, always in the current window.
- "Close Remote Connection" is the command `workbench.action.remote.close`.
- Internal details of the Dev Containers extension (NFR-06) are kept in `src/core/devContainers.ts`: the literal `attached-container` and the activation event `onResolveRemoteAuthority:attached-container` (a unit test checks that `package.json` has it), the settings for one container (`devContainersSettings`), `shutdownAction: "none"` of the override configuration, `--skip-post-attach`, the machine settings that reach the window (`remote.localPortHost`), the volume names and labels of the Dev Containers extension (`vscode`, `vsc-remote-containers`, hash-suffixed clone volumes, `vsch.*`, `dev.container.volume`), and the forwarding credential helper behind the Git-version warning (`forwardingHelperReachesGit`). The encoding of the authority stays in `src/vscode/connection/authority.ts` and takes the literal from there.
- Before the window connects (user decision 2026-09-28), the build must have left its image in the engine (`imageExists` after `devcontainer build`, else a failed build), the current Docker context must still be the environment's host, read outside the operation, whose calls are pinned to its own context (else `otherDockerHost`, `Messages.otherDockerHostAfterStart`), and the container must report `running` within 5 checks one second apart (else `startFailed`, `Messages.containerNotReady`; the log names the last state); a refusal removes the pipeline's pending connection file. When the check of the built image fails with an error, the image is removed like after any later failure. Cancel, a newer request in the window, and the account check are evaluated after these checks (Cancel also ends the pauses), and only then are the pending connection file and `vscode.openFolder` written.
- The log shows which Docker the Dev Containers extension will ask (`src/core/docker/attachDiagnostics.ts`, user request 2026-09-28): before the switch ("Before the window connects: …") and when the attached window activates ("While this window connects: …"), it logs DOCKER_HOST and DOCKER_CONTEXT of VS Code, the current context and its endpoint, the environment's context, and `docker inspect` of the container without a context (as the first call of Dev Containers) and with the environment's context. These calls run outside the operation, so they are not pinned to its context. Before the switch, the environment's context is the one of the running operation; the lines run alongside the container checks, and the window waits for them at most 3 s (Cancel ends this wait too). At activation they run in the background, only in a window of an environment that the registry knows, with the current context when it is on the environment's host, else the context of its host (`findRemoteContext`).

## 12. Session Monitor

Plan step 8, PR C (user decisions of 2026-09-30, "One Session Monitor on every engine", and Q1, Q2, Q7 of 2026-10-02): the detached Node.js Session Monitor process on the computer (`src/monitor`: `sessionMonitor.ts`, `monitorLoop.ts`, `rules.ts`, `lock.ts`, `monitorDocker.ts`, `monitorLog.ts`; the bundle `dist/sessionMonitor.js`; `monitor.json`, `monitor.lock`, `monitor.version`, `monitor.exit`, `monitor.log`; `MONITOR_PROTOCOL_VERSION`) is removed. The Session Monitor is the container `devenv-session-monitor` of each engine (section 16). The window does the rest:

- **Release on close and switch (Q1).** `SessionCoordinator` (`src/vscode/sessionCoordinator.ts`) calls its `release` dependency for the environment the window leaves: in `setEnvironment` (and a second `start`) when the environment changes from one to another or to none (not awaited; tracked), and in `deactivate()` for the connected environment, after `deactivateSync()` wrote `closing` and the reopen record synchronously. `deactivate()` waits for that release and for the releases of a switch that still run, all within `CLOSE_RELEASE_BOUNDS.totalMs` (2 s), and never rejects. extension.ts returns this promise from `deactivate()`; VS Code disposes the subscriptions synchronously right after it calls `deactivate()`. Review round 1 of PR #87 (B-R1-7 (a)): `ClosingWork` (`src/core/session/closingWork.ts`, no `vscode`) holds that promise (`begin`), and the subscriptions that the release needs are registered through it (`deferred`, `deferredSubscriptions`): the worker channels (`channels.dispose()`; plan step 11F2: there is no router any more), the heartbeat preparation of `heartbeatWiring` (a worker that must be opened again for the release), and the logger; each is disposed only after the closing work settled (at once when there is none). Before, only the channels waited, so a reopen of the worker at the close was aborted and the release logged to a disposed channel.
- **First heartbeat at once** (review round 1 of PR #87, A-R1-1): `start` calls `windowHeartbeats.tick()` right after its first status write when the window has an environment, and so do `setEnvironment` and a second `start` that change to another environment (not awaited). Before, the first heartbeat came only after the first interval (15 s), and a reload of the window, whose previous activation had just sent the release, could lose the environment while it attached.
- **`releaseEnvironment`** (`src/core/session/windowRelease.ts`, pure but for its dependencies), within `bounds.totalMs` (switch: `SWITCH_RELEASE_BOUNDS`, 10 s; close: 2 s): the environment from the registry (gone: nothing); kept (`keptWhenClosed` of its flags and the settings `stopOnClose`, `respectShutdownActionNone`: Keep Running When Closed, Close and Keep Running, `stopOnClose` off, a respected `"shutdownAction": "none"`): nothing; used by another window of this computer: nothing (review round 1 of PR #87, A-R1-2: all windows of a computer share one record per environment, and a release would shorten it; `SessionCoordinator.otherWindowUses` reads the status files and the pending connection files and asks `otherWindowHoldsEnvironment`: a fresh `active` status file of another live window that shows it or a fresh pending connection file of another window (`otherWindowUsesEnvironment`), or a live busy mark of another window on it (`isBusyMarkLive` with the status files); files that cannot be read count as in use); else (Q2 (c)) `recordGitState` within `bounds.gitMs` (close: 1 s; switch: 5 s; a failure or the bound is logged and the release goes on), then `send` with `releaseLimitSeconds(waitingTimeSeconds)` = the waiting time (invalid: its default 30 s), at least `MIN_LIMIT_SECONDS` (60), plus `RELEASE_MARGIN_SECONDS`, and at most `MAX_LIMIT_SECONDS`. Review round 1 of PR #87 (A-R1-1): `RELEASE_MARGIN_SECONDS` = (`WINDOW_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_MS` + `HEARTBEAT_ATTEMPT_DEADLINE_MS`) / 1000 = 150 s, so the default release is 210 s: a window that comes back sends its first long heartbeat at once, but that heartbeat may take a whole attempt (the lookup of the engine, the check of the container, the worker, a repair), and after a failure the next one follows within an interval. Never throws. A lost release (the window was killed, the bound passed, the engine did not answer) leaves the long limit of the last heartbeat: the safe side.
- **The send** is `WindowHeartbeats.release(id, limitSeconds, signal)` (section 16): one heartbeat with the short limit, the flag of the entry and `release: true` (review round 1 of PR #87, A-R1-2: the monitor never lets it shorten the live record of another computer, section 16 *Rules*), to the engine the window used the environment on as its connected one (remembered, else looked up with that role), only where its container is (A-R2-1), through the worker with the repair of a missing monitor, ended by the signal of the bound. Its success removes the environment from the sent entries of the series, so a later tick that still uses it (a busy mark) sends the long limit again at once. The monitor's records decide by the newest `at`; the release has `seq` = now, so a heartbeat of a tick that was under way with an older `seq` does not replace it, and a newer heartbeat (a reloaded window, another window of the computer at least every 30 s, another computer) does. Review round 1 of PR #87 (A-R1-3): when the signal of the release ends the attempt while its call still hangs, the call counts as expired (as one past `HEARTBEAT_ATTEMPT_DEADLINE_MS`): each due heartbeat of that engine counts as a failure towards the Q4 warning, and `sendFor` (Close and Keep Running) ends at once with "no answer in time" instead of waiting for it.
- **The Git state (Q2 (c))** is the operation `recordGitState` of the worker of the engine of the release (plan step 11D1: `windowHeartbeats.connectedEngine(environment)`, in the scope of the heartbeat preparation), which runs `EnvironmentService.recordGitState(id, signal)` there: the refresh of Delete, `gitSummaryInContainer` in the running dev container (`findContainer`, the rule of the dev container of plan step 11I, U4) as `remoteUser`, recorded through the request `record recordGitSummary`; nothing when the environment is gone, not on the engine of the operation, or its container does not run; no Docker start, no lock, no helper; never throws.
- **Cleanup** (moved from the removed process; monitor cleanup, user decision 2026-09-29): `SessionCoordinator.cleanUpStorage()` at `start` and then every `STORAGE_SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS` (1 hour; the timer stops with the window), one at a time, never throws: `sweepStorage` (`src/core/storage/storageSweep.ts`: R6 `pending/<environment id>.json` whose `createdAt` is more than 1 hour from now, both ways; R7 `disconnect/<environment id>.json` whose `requestedAt` is more than 10 minutes from now; a file of either without a valid time, or larger than 64 KB, counts by its modification time; R8 temporary files of `atomicTemporaryPath` (`ATOMIC_TEMPORARY_FILE`) whose modification time is more than 1 hour from now in the storage folder, `sessions/`, `pending/`, `operations/`, and `disconnect/`; only regular files, never a link; the file is checked to be the same before the unlink), then the status files of other windows whose process ended (`isProcessAlive`) and whose `updatedAt` is more than `HEARTBEAT_MAX_AGE_MS` plus the waiting time from now (so the `closing` file of a window that just closed stays that long). Delete still deletes `disconnect/<environment id>.json` of its environment.
- **Stale keep records** (review note of PR #85): the full sync with clear-only entries of the removed process is replaced by the windows: the first heartbeat of a series on an engine, and the first after a change of `stopOnClose` or `respectShutdownActionNone` (`keepSettingsKey`), carries a `clearOnly` entry for each environment of the registry on that engine (`isOnDockerHost`) that the window does not report and that is not kept, within `MAX_HEARTBEAT_ENVIRONMENTS`; a failed heartbeat carries them again. Chosen because the monitor already writes a clear-only entry only over a keep record of the same source (review round 3 of PR #39, N1) and drops it under the `seq` order when a newer keep came: it withdraws a keep of this computer that is left over (for example Stop When Closed while the engine could not be reached, or `stopOnClose` turned on again) and never overrules another computer, a window that uses the environment, or a newer choice; so no check of "not in use" is needed. The record it writes has the long limit.
- `src/core/busy.ts` keeps the rules that the windows share (`HEARTBEAT_MAX_AGE_MS`, `PENDING_MAX_AGE_MS`, `waitingTimeMs`, the busy marks, `otherWindowUsesEnvironment`); `src/core/session/sessionRules.ts` keeps `isProcessAlive`, `keptWhenClosed`, `keepFlagsOf`, `stopAfterSeconds`.
- No migration (Q7): a Session Monitor process of an earlier version that still runs during the update stops its environments after its waiting time as before and ends when no window is open; nothing asks it to exit any more. `monitor.json` is no longer updated, so such an old process keeps its last settings (for example `stopOnClose` and the waiting time) until all windows close (review round 1 of PR #87, A-R1-5: accepted, Q7).
- Delete's confirmation (review round 1 of PR #87, A-R1-4): the Git state is now recorded by the window (the release, Delete's refresh of a running container), not before every stop; a lost release or a stop by the long limit leaves an older state. When the state that Delete shows (`safetyCheck`, else the registry's `gitSummary`) was recorded before the environment's `lastUsedAt`, or none was recorded, the dialog adds `Messages.deleteChangesUnknownSince(<time>)` or `Messages.deleteChangesNotRecorded` (`recordedStateNote` in `src/core/messages.ts`). Only a message: no Git runs for it. Review round 2 of PR #87 (A-R2-2): the last use is the later of `lastUsedAt` (the open pipeline) and `lastSeenInUseAt` (`lastSeenInUse`), which `EnvironmentRegistry.markSeenInUse` moves forward at the start of every release (before its Git step) and when a window starts connected to the environment (`SessionCoordinator.start`, a reload).
- Keep Running When Closed (concept 7.9; user decision 2026-09-26, "go with the proposal for closing"): the registry entry has the optional field `keepRunning` (validated as a boolean in `registry.ts`; a missing field means false). The commands `devEnvironments.keepRunning` and `devEnvironments.stopWhenClosed` (`Controller.setKeepRunning`) write it with `EnvironmentRegistry.updateEnvironment`, under the registry lock, render the sidebar, and send one heartbeat with the new flag at once (`ControllerDeps.sessionMonitor.sendHeartbeat`, `WindowHeartbeats.sendFor`); a failure keeps the stored choice, is logged, and shows `ControllerTexts.keepRunningNotSent`. The heartbeats carry the flag (`keptWhenClosed`), and the window sends no release for a kept environment. The user's Stop (`EnvironmentService.stop`) does not look at the switch and leaves it set.
- Close and Keep Running (unit 7, PR 2): the optional registry field `keepRunningOnce` (validated as a boolean in `registry.ts`). `Controller.closeAndKeepRunning` acts on the environment of this window only: it sets the field with `updateEnvironment`; on every engine (plan step 8, PR A; only when Docker is set to the host of the environment, else `otherDockerHost`) it sends one heartbeat with `keepRunning: true` first (`ControllerDeps.sessionMonitor.sendHeartbeat`; without it in the window the command refuses) and, when that fails, removes the field again and shows `ControllerTexts.closeAndKeepRunningUnreachable` (the window stays open); then `ConnectionAdapter.closeWindow` (`workbench.action.closeWindow`); the window's release on close then sees the flag and sends nothing. Review round 1 of PR #39 (F1): the command resolves when the close starts, not after the dialog about unsaved files, so the window does not clear the field when it is still open later; a cancelled close leaves the environment kept until the next open or attach, or Stop (the safe side). Accepted leftover (review round 2 of PR #39, L2): an open that starts the container and then fails or is cancelled leaves `keepRunningOnce` set; the next successful open, attach, Stop, or Delete clears it. `keepFlagsOf` counts `keepRunning || keepRunningOnce`. The field is removed when a window connects (`finish` of the open pipeline, in the same registry update as `lastUsedAt`; `Controller.openAttachedEnvironment` and the restored window, `clearKeepRunningOnce`), and by `EnvironmentService.stop`; Delete removes the entry. The row shows ` · kept` and the tooltip line `TreeTexts.keptOnce`; its menu keeps Keep Running When Closed.
- Tree: the `contextValue` of a row with an environment has `canKeepRunning` or `kept` (`treeModel.contextValue`); the menus use `viewItem =~ /;canKeepRunning(;|$)/` and `/;kept(;|$)/`, so exactly one command shows. `rowStateText` adds ` · kept` in every state (`Running · kept`, `Connected · kept`, `Stopped · kept`, also `No container · kept`, `Files missing · kept`, and `Updating · kept`), as the menu offers **Stop When Closed** in every state; the icon stays the icon of the state, because a tree item has one icon only, and the tooltip has the line `TreeTexts.kept`.

## 13. User interface

- Texts: exactly the texts of the concept where it gives them (section 6.5), plain language (NFR-02).
- Progress: one notification with the steps of concept 6.5, and a button **Show details** for the output channel "Dev Environments". User decision 2026-09-28: **Show details** (the hidden command `devEnvironments.showProgressDetails` with the ID of the operation, `hideProgressNotification` in src/vscode/progress.ts) also closes the notification; the operation goes on, the status bar shows it until it ends, and its errors are shown as before. Its Cancel button goes with the notification.
- Tree: groups by owner, rows as in concept 6.2. `contextValue` lists the possible actions, for example `repository;canStart;canDelete;multiConfig`, and the menus use `viewItem =~ /canStart/`.
- Repository groups (concept 6.2, setting `repositoryGroups`, scope `application`): `repositoryGroups.ts` (no `vscode` import) checks the entries (`parseRepositoryGroups`) and splits a name into levels and label (`matchRepositoryGroup`, first match wins; a pattern that throws while it is matched, for example a `RangeError` of a stack overflow, does not match that name and keeps the error in `RepositoryGroupPattern.failure`, which the sidebar logs once per window session, `logGroupFailures`). The sidebar compiles the entries once per model build and passes the valid ones to `buildTreeModel` (`TreeInput.repositoryGroups`); each distinct problem is logged and shown as a warning once per window. Without valid entries, `buildTreeModel` takes the path of the plain list, so the model is the same as without the setting; so does each owner where no repository matches (`groupRows` returns `undefined`). Group nodes have `kind: 'group'`, `contextValue` `group` (no row action matches it), and the ID `group:<owner-lower>:<index of a named entry, or ->:<level values, URI-encoded, joined by />`; row IDs stay `repo:<owner/name>`. `repositoryRows`, `recentEnvironments`, and `getParent` of the tree walk the nested nodes. A render with patterns that takes `SLOW_GROUPING_MS` (200 ms) or more names the setting once per window session and in the log (`warnIfGroupingIsSlow`), because the patterns run synchronously in the extension host.
- Repository groups editor (concept 6.2, command `devEnvironments.editRepositoryGroups`): a webview panel (`repositoryGroupsEditor.ts`, thin glue) with the script and style sheet in `resources/groupsEditor/` (plain HTML, CSS, and JavaScript; packaged through `resources/**`). The pure part is `repositoryGroupsEditorModel.ts` (no `vscode` import): `entriesFromSetting`/`toSettingValue` (strings for entries without name and flags), `checkEntries` (the rules of `checkRepositoryGroupEntry`, which `parseRepositoryGroups` uses per entry), `parseEditorRequest` (exact message shapes, limits, flags `ius` only; an entry has `name`, `pattern`, and `flags`, nothing else), `buildGroupsPreview` (`buildTreeModel` with and without the patterns on `Sidebar.groupingInput()`, the input of the last render, so no GitHub request and the same tree as the view), `testRepositoryName` (`matchRepositoryGroup`), `describeSettingList`/`describeSettingEntry` (the list of settings.json for the question of Save: one element per line, each cut after `MAX_SHOWN_ENTRY` characters, at most `MAX_SHOWN_LINES` lines or 2000 characters with the rest counted, the first element always shown), and `editorHtml` (CSP `default-src 'none'`, style from `webview.cspSource`, script with a nonce). Save re-checks the entries, needs a run of the worker that neither timed out nor failed (`previewTooSlow`, `failed`; `editorState` keeps `canSave` off for both), reads `inspect().globalValue` and writes the draft with `update(key, value, ConfigurationTarget.Global)`, which changes only that key in the user `settings.json` (user decision A, 2026-09-26: no merge of entries), while the stored value equals the base (the value loaded or last saved; `sameSettingValue`). Otherwise, and for a stored value that is not a list, it asks (modal; `GroupsEditorTexts.changedMeanwhile` or `notAListConflict`, the detail from `describeSettingList` or `notAListDetail`): **Load settings.json** loads the stored value and writes nothing, **Save Mine** writes the draft, and Cancel (dismissed) writes nothing and keeps the draft. After the question it reads the value again; Save Mine counts only for the value that the question showed, otherwise it asks again. Nothing awaits between that read and `update`. It stops without writing when the panel was closed after any await. After a write, the written value is the new base; when settings.json already holds exactly the draft, Save writes nothing, asks nothing, and loads it as the new base (`GroupsEditorTexts.alreadySaved`). While Save runs, every state has `saving: true`, and the webview keeps its controls (the `fieldset#form`) disabled from its Save until a state with that `seq` and `saving: false`. `onDidChangeConfiguration` of the key only sends a state: it sets `changedOutside` (the stored value differs from the base), and the page shows the banner "settings.json changed this setting." with **Load settings.json** (the `reload` message, which also drops an update that still waits in the page). The editor never replaces the draft on its own, also not a draft without edits (review round 7 of PR #21: the automatic reload with its `external` offer and `accept` answer raced with keystrokes that wait for the 150 ms delay and with refused updates, so it was removed). Only **Load settings.json** of the banner or of the question of Save replaces the draft; until then the base stays, so Save asks. The page shows the banner of every state, also of one that answers an older update. The panel is created with `retainContextWhenHidden: true`: a hidden tab keeps its page, so its `seq`, the update that waits for its delay, and its draft survive hide and show, and no second page starts with its own counters. The cost is the memory of the hidden page of one settings editor, which is accepted. The panel also keeps the draft on the extension side, so a page that does start again (`ready`, for example after Developer: Reload Webviews) gets it back: the load message carries the test name and the `seq` of the extension, which the page continues from, and every state with `saving: true` keeps the form disabled. Save and `reload` carry the test name as well (checked like that of an update), so a test name that still waits for its delay is not lost; a running page keeps the text of its test field at every later load and takes the test name of a load only at its first one. Each load has a `generation`; an update or Save of an earlier one writes nothing, and its entries come back as the draft with the current generation and the status `GroupsEditorTexts.staleKept` ("…your edits are kept, press Save again"), except for a generation before the last processed Load settings.json (`reloaded`): that draft stays dropped, and the message only gets a state. During Save, an update, Save, or stale message (of another page, for example after Developer: Reload Webviews) does not change the draft, but it raises `seq` and gets a state (`saving: true`), so the state after Save answers it and that page is never left read-only; a `reload` during Save is queued (`reloadQueued`) and runs when Save ends, unless Save already loaded the value of settings.json. Load settings.json drops the update that waits in the page and keeps the form disabled until the load arrives, the same way as Save. Every message that `parseEditorRequest` refuses is answered with a state of the draft of the extension, with the `seq` of the message when it has a valid one (`refusedRequestSeq`) and the status `GroupsEditorTexts.refusedMessage`, so a page that waits for the answer of its Save is not left read-only; Add Entry is disabled at `EditorLimits.entries` (200), which the extension refuses above. The page checks `EditorLimits` itself (entries, name, pattern): a draft over them (for example a pattern of more than 5000 characters in settings.json) is not sent, the entry over the limit shows its error at that entry (the checks of the extension, which are for the draft before, are not shown then), Save is off, the status names the entry and says that the preview shows the entries before, and the edits stay in the page. `Sidebar.onDidRender` refreshes the preview. `checkEntries` counts the capturing groups in the source (`capturingGroups`, a small scanner) and runs no regular expression of the draft in the extension host. Closing the panel disposes the runner: its worker ends, the jobs queued before resolve `{ failed: true }` without starting a new worker, the running job resolves with its result when the worker had already posted it and otherwise with `failed: true` (with the preview, if that was posted), and the next run (the editor opened again) starts a new one. A state computed after the panel closed is dropped without a log line. The preview and the test run in a worker thread (`groupsPreviewWorker.ts`, bundle `dist/groupsPreviewWorker.js`, `runPreviewJob`) through `PreviewWorkerRunner` (`groupsPreviewRunner.ts`) with a limit of 1 second (`PREVIEW_TIME_LIMIT_MS`): the worker reports each entry before it runs on the names of the view, and after the limit it is terminated and the entry is named as too slow (`previewTooSlow`, `slowEntry`); Save runs the same check and refuses. The sidebar still runs the saved patterns in the extension host (`warnIfGroupingIsSlow`).
- The sidebar, Search, and the switcher get only the environments of the signed-in account (`availableEnvironments`); without a sign-in, the view is empty and shows the welcome view with the sign-in. A listed repository with an environment of another account is a row like any repository without environment: Start creates the environment of the signed-in account (D-3). On an account change (`authentication.onDidChangeSessions`), a window connected to an environment that the account may not use closes its remote connection at once (after a sign-out with `ControllerTexts.signedOutConnection`), empties the tmpfs `/run/devenv` of every running dev container of the environment (unit 15; the operation `tokenRemove` of the worker, plan step 11B1; every one since plan step 11I, U4), and checks after about 10 seconds (`LEAVE_CHECK_MS`) whether it is still attached: then a modal message, and the close again. When the account may use the environment again, the window reloads instead. A restored window whose container has no current `nimblescape.devenv.container-version` and whose open pipeline fails leaves the container the same way; Start on such a connected container does too. The view loads the stored list of the new account.
- Sign-in state: `auth.ts` alone sets the context key `devEnvironments.signedIn` (welcome view **Sign in with GitHub**). Signed in means a session with `repo read:org` exists and GitHub did not reject its token. Every GitHub caller reports an HTTP 401 to `reportRejectedToken`: `GitHubApi` (`onUnauthorized`, all GraphQL requests: the repository list, single repositories, the identity of the container Git), the clone (Git "Authentication failed" for github.com, `isGitHubTokenRejected`), and the token service of ghcr.io for the GitHub session (`RegistryClient.onCredentialsRejected`). The rejection is kept per scope set and session (ID and token), and cleared by a session with another ID or token or by a request that succeeds (`reportAcceptedToken`). While it holds, an interactive request, for example the unchanged command `devEnvironments.signIn`, calls `getSession(…, { forceNewSession: true })` instead of `createIfNone`, which would return the rejected session without a dialog. Workaround for VS Code keeping such a session; remove it when VS Code handles it. The button, the welcome texts, and the sign-in row are unchanged; the account and its environments stay available (`getAccount` gives the account of a rejected session without a dialog, so Stop and Delete ask for no new sign-in; requests that need a working token do), and the one-time warning with the action **Sign in** (`SIGN_IN_AGAIN_DETAIL`) stays.
- While an environment is Updating, Start, Stop, and Rebuild are hidden (concept 7.9 rule 1). Delete stays (concept 7.15), except while the operation is itself a delete: after the confirmations, it waits until the operation of the other window has ended, in a progress notification with Cancel.

## 14. Repository discovery

- **Scan scope** (concept 7.4, setting `owners`): `DiscoveryService` reads the setting at each refresh (`DiscoveryOptions.scope`); `scope.ts` normalizes it (trimmed, case-insensitive, without duplicates). Empty: `DISCOVERY_QUERY` over `viewer.repositories`, one sequential cursor. Configured: `SCOPE_VIEWER_QUERY` (login, `databaseId` for the account check, organizations; no repositories), then per owner `OWNER_REPOSITORIES_QUERY` (`repositoryOwner(login:)` with `... on Organization` and `... on User` (`ownerAffiliations: [OWNER]`)), or `VIEWER_REPOSITORIES_QUERY` (`affiliations` and `ownerAffiliations` `[OWNER]`) for the login of the account. `repositoryOwner: null` with only NOT_FOUND, SAML, OAuth, or FORBIDDEN errors is a missing owner: hint kind `notFound` (`Messages.organizationNotFound`, the row opens `https://github.com/<login>`), unless a SAML or OAuth hint names the owner. The probe of organizations asks only organizations of the scope. The result stores `scope`; the sidebar shows a stored list, and the result of a refresh, only when its scope equals the setting (`sameScope`), and calls `Sidebar.onScopeChanged` when the setting changes (clears a list of another scope, then a refresh with `again`). `repositoriesToLookUp` skips repositories outside the scope, so no `getRepository` request is about them.
- **List and lookups:** the list queries (`DISCOVERY_QUERY`, `OWNER_REPOSITORIES_QUERY`, `VIEWER_REPOSITORIES_QUERY`) never contain the configuration lookups and read 100 repositories per page (`DISCOVERY_PAGE_SIZE`). `RepositoryCollector.addPage` returns the repositories that need a lookup, and `LookupQueue` starts a batch of `configurationsQuery` (aliases `r<i>`, variables `o<i>`/`n<i>`, `LOOKUP_BATCH_SIZE` 50) as soon as 50 are listed; `finish` starts the rest and waits for all batches. First load: every repository gets a lookup. A refresh with a stored list (`flushEachPage`) also starts the rest of a page at once when a slot for lookups is free (`RequestLimiter.lookupIdle`): the few new and changed repositories come first (last push first), so their batch runs beside the other pages instead of after the last one.
- **Concurrency:** one `RequestLimiter` of 4 (`DISCOVERY_CONCURRENCY`, `concurrency.ts`) per refresh covers every request (account, list pages, lookups, organizations, probes): it starts a request only while fewer than 4 run, whatever their kind, so there are never more than 4. Every request of a refresh goes through `DiscoveryService.request` with its kind (`list`, `lookup`, `other`). While a list is open (`openList`, one per cursor: the viewer list, or each owner of the scope), lookups use at most 3 slots, so the next page never waits for a slow batch (before, the fourth batch took the last slot and the page waited up to one batch time, about 3 seconds, several times per load); list pages and other requests go before waiting batches. More open lists share the free slots; the lookups keep 3, because they are the larger part of the work. When the last list closes, the lookups use all 4. The refresh has one `AbortController`: the first failure of an owner or a batch (`guard`), and any other failure of the refresh (for example an HTTP 403 of a later page of the empty scope), aborts all other requests, and the refresh fails with that failure as a whole (the stored file stays); an abort of the caller gives an AbortError. Every request of the run (also the organizations and the probes) gets the signal of that controller. A failed or aborted refresh rejects only when all its requests settled (`RefreshRun.pending`, also those that wait for a slot), and reports no part after its abort; since the sidebar starts the next refresh only after it, the requests of two refreshes never run at the same time. The 30-second time limit per request and the smaller page on a timeout (100, 50, 25, 12, 10) stay; a lookup batch that times out is split in halves (`allOrAbort`). The GitHubApi 401 hooks cover the list, lookup, and account requests, because all go through `GitHubApi.graphql`.
- **Request count of the first load:** empty scope, n repositories: ⌈n/100⌉ list requests and ⌈n/50⌉ lookup requests (664 repositories: 7 + 14 = 21 requests), plus further pages of organizations (more than 100) and probes. Configured scope: 1 account request, ⌈n_owner/100⌉ list requests per owner, and ⌈n/50⌉ lookup requests. The lookups are the larger part: with a batch time T and 4 at the same time, the first load needs at least one list page plus ⌈n/50⌉ · T / 4 (666 repositories: 14 batches; T = 3 seconds: about 13 seconds; T = 6 seconds: about 25 seconds). The empty scope also needs its ⌈n/100⌉ pages one after another (7 pages of 3 seconds: about 21 seconds, plus the last batch). A refresh with few changes needs about the time of its list pages (7 pages one after another for the empty scope).
- **Incremental detection:** with a stored list (`loadStored`, also of another scope), `needsConfigurationLookup` (new repository, other `pushedAt`, or other default branch) selects the repositories for the lookups; unchanged repositories keep their stored `configPaths`. A repository that an error of its list page points into, or whose lookup GitHub did not answer, is not kept in `withoutConfiguration` and is read again at the next refresh; one that has a configuration is listed in `uncertain`, which `storedDetections` skips, so it is read again too. Each refresh logs "loaded in N seconds with M requests", "configurations of N repositories read with M requests", and one line with the requests of each kind (list, lookup, other) with their average time, the most requests that ran at the same time, and the time until the first repository with a configuration was known (the first rows of the progressive display). The average times show which estimate applies (list page about 0.5 to 1 second, batch about 3 seconds, not measured yet).
- **Progressive display:** `DiscoveryService.onPartialResult` gets the repositories with a configuration found so far after each list page and each lookup batch (account ID, scope); on the first load they join as their batches finish. extension.ts passes them to `Sidebar.onPartialResult`, which shows them only during a refresh that started while the view had no list of the account, and only for this account and the current scope; the complete result replaces them. The sidebar shows the complete result as soon as `refresh` returns; the single lookups of environment repositories that the list lacks (`lookUpUnlisted`, `getRepository`) follow, at most 4 at the same time (`mapLimit`; the refresh has ended, and refreshes run one at a time), and a second render adds their `not on GitHub` labels. At start, the stored list is shown before any request (`initialize`); the refresh runs in the background.
- **Refreshes one at a time:** `Sidebar.refreshDiscovery` runs one refresh at a time (`CoalescingTask`). `onSessionChanged` (VS Code reports a change of the GitHub sessions, also for a session of other scopes, for example `read:packages`, or of another extension) starts one more refresh after a running one only when the account changed, the user was not signed in, or the running refresh does not use the token of the current session (`refreshToken`); otherwise it reuses the running refresh, whose list would be the same, so that Refresh does not wait for two refreshes.
- **Select Organizations…** (`ownerSelector.ts`, rules in `ownerChoices.ts`): multi-select Quick Pick with the account ("your account"), its organizations in alphabetical order (from the list of the view when it belongs to the account, else `DiscoveryService.viewerOrganizations`, which lists no repository), and the owners of the setting in neither list ("from your settings"); the setting is pre-selected. OK writes `owners` with `ConfigurationTarget.Global`; an empty selection writes `[]`. Not signed in: `Messages.signInRequired` with **Sign in**.

## 15. Docker Compose

Unit 6 (user decision 2026-09-26). A configuration with `dockerComposeFile` and `service` (and optionally `runServices` and `workspaceFolder`) opens like an image or Dockerfile configuration: one `devcontainer up` starts the dev service and the other services. The code: `src/core/helper/compose.ts` (model, names, rewrite; the model types and names in `src/core/helper/composeModel.ts`), `src/core/policy/compose.ts` (policy; the decisions for the mounts and ports of each service, `decideServiceMount` and `decideServicePort`, in `src/core/policy/rewrites.ts`), `COMPOSE_MODEL_SCRIPT` and `WRITE_AND_RUN_SCRIPT` in `scripts.ts`, and the Compose branches of `environmentService.ts`. The facts about CLI 0.89.0 that the design relies on are checked by `devcontainerCli.contract.test.ts`.

- **The checked model is the model that runs** (decision D-1). The step `composeModel` of the batch helper runs `docker compose -f <files> --profile '*' config --format json` with `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=<project>` (`WorkspaceHelper.composeModel`), as the owner of the repository, with `/workspaces/.devenv+` (the internal folder) root's and 0700 during the step: so without the Docker socket and that folder, unless root owns the repository (the known limitation of section 17). The helper has network; Docker Compose fetches no remote resource there (`COMPOSE_EXPERIMENTAL_GIT_REMOTE` and `COMPOSE_EXPERIMENTAL_OCI_REMOTE` are `false`). The compose files are those of `dockerComposeFile`, resolved against the folder of the configuration as the CLI does, and must be in the repository (`resolveComposeFiles`; a missing `dockerComposeFile`, a variable, or a path outside is not supported). The script also reports the Compose version, whether `config` escapes `$` as `$$` (a probe; review round 19, S19-1: when it does, the script unescapes every text value of the model right after `config`, and since review round 20 (D20-1) every key too (the maps of `labels`, `environment`, `build.args`, `sysctls`, `annotations`, `driver_opts`, …; the names of services, volumes, networks, secrets, and configs cannot hold a `$`), so the Dockerfiles of `dockerfile_inline`, `realPaths`, `missing`, `mountAncestors`, `mountCreateTargets`, the `env_file`s of `inputsHash`, their keys, and the model that the extension checks and hashes hold the texts that Compose and BuildKit use; every model that the extension writes is escaped with `escapeComposeDollars`, whatever the probe said), the Dockerfile of each built service (a file in the repository, also after links, or a file outside of it that is no path of the workspace helper) the real path of each bind mount source, `env_file`, local build context, and Dockerfile (links), and `inputsHash`, the sha256 of the files that Compose read (the compose files, the `.env` of the project folder, the `env_file`s; only the hash leaves the run). The pipeline checks this model (`composeAccessReport`, container restrictions section 13) before any build, and again, with the labels of the volumes then, before each `up` that creates containers. `build` and `up` get our rewrite of exactly this model as the only compose file (`/tmp/devenv-override/compose.json`, written by `WRITE_AND_RUN_SCRIPT` from standard input), named with an absolute path by our copy of devcontainer.json (`composeConfigOverride` for `read-configuration` and `build`, which has no `--override-config`; `buildComposeOverrideConfig` for `up`). The repository's compose files are read once, so nothing can change between the check and `up`, and no `!override` YAML tag is needed.
- **Project name** (D-2): the name of the environment (`composeProjectName` = `resourceName`, the same as the repository of the environment image, the volume, and the dev container; user decisions 2026-10-03), set by `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME` of each helper run (it wins over a `.env` of the repository) and by `name:` of the model. The CLI finds the dev container only by the project and the service; `up` fails when its result names another project (L-2).
- **Environment image** (D-3, D-8): `devcontainer build --image-name <name>:<n>` with the build model (`composeBuildModel`): the dev service builds `<project>-<service>` (an image-only dev service gets a synthesized `FROM <image>` build, so the CLI never uses its shared `vsc-<repository>-<hash>` name), which the CLI tags as the environment image. The Dockerfile of the dev service is always ours (review round 20, P20-1): `composeBuildModel` writes the checked text (`ComposeModelOutput.dockerfiles`) of its `dockerfile_inline`, of the synthesized build, and also of a Dockerfile file of the repository to `/tmp/devenv-override/dev.Dockerfile` (`COMPOSE_DEV_DOCKERFILE`) and points `build.dockerfile` there (the context stays); a local build of the dev service without a checked text throws (fail closed). The reason is **the CLI's escaped view**: the Dev Container CLI 0.89.0 does not use our model object; it runs `docker compose config` on the model file that we write and parses that YAML output, which an escaping Compose (`dollarEscaped`) prints with every `$` doubled. The CLI's view of the dev service is therefore `escapeComposeDollars` of our unescaped model, while Compose and BuildKit use the unescaped texts: with a `$` in `build.dockerfile` or `build.context`, the CLI (which reads the Dockerfile itself and, with or without Features, builds its copy `Dockerfile-with-features`) and our check would read two different files, and the file could change between the check and the build. The configuration hash does not change for P20-1 (the written build model is not hashed; `composeConfigHash` hashes the checked model and the Dockerfile texts); it changes once for a model with a `$` in a key (D20-1), handled like the change of review round 19. The up model (`composeUpModel`) runs the dev service from the environment image (`image`, no `build`, `pull_policy: never`). Other services: images that the pipeline pulls before `up` with the credentials of the image check (`pull_policy: missing`), and images that Compose builds as `<project>-<service>`. Every built image gets `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id` through `build.labels`, and every network that the model declares and that is not external gets it through `labels` (user decisions 2026-10-03). Build numbers, the metadata check of the image before `up`, refused updates, and the fallback to the previous image work as for a single container. The build record gets `compose: { service, images, serviceImages, version, inputsHash }`: the built images, which Delete removes; the images of the other services that are not built (`composeServiceImageReferences`), which `removeUnusedBaseImages` never removes (they are the user's images, for example `postgres:16`, not base images of the environment image; concept 7.7 removes base images: the `FROM` images and the image of the dev service; a record without the list removes none); and the Compose version and the hash of the files as written (`composeInputsHash`: devcontainer.json, `inputsHash`, the Dockerfiles). `configHash` is `composeConfigHash`: devcontainer.json, the merged model (keys sorted), and the Dockerfiles of the built services. Whether the configuration changed (`composeConfigurationChange`, review round 1, P-4): the files differ, or they are equal and the same Compose version printed another model. When only the Compose version (the helper installs the current plugin) and with it the printed model differ, it is no change: the open takes the new model hash and version into the record without a question. The image check covers every service image and each `FROM` of a built service (`composeReferences`).
- **The rewrite** (`composeUpModel`, logged as "Changed in the Docker Compose model"): every container gets `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id`; the dev container also `nimblescape.devenv.container-version`, the name of the environment (`container_name`), the workspace volume at `/workspaces` (the templates' bind mount `../..:/workspaces` is dropped), the tmpfs of the token (`TOKEN_TMPFS` added to its `tmpfs`, unit 15), and `hostname: <repository name>` (`containerHostname`, as `--hostname` of a single container) unless the service sets `hostname`, uses `network_mode` `host`, `service:…`, or `container:…`, or `uts: host` (`serviceDecidesHostname`); the other services get `nimblescape.devenv.compose-service=<service>` and lose `container_name` (D-12, two environments of one repository would collide). Published ports without an address get `127.0.0.1`. A bind mount of a path inside the repository becomes the workspace volume with `volume.subpath` (Docker Engine API 1.45 or newer, `docker version --format '{{.Server.APIVersion}}'`; older: refused) and `nocopy`, read-only as before: the service can read and change those files of the repository, and nothing else of the volume. Every named volume is declared `external`. Every text value of a written model is escaped (`$` → `$$`, `escapeComposeDollars`), whatever the Compose version, because Compose interpolates the written model again; the keys are written as they are (Compose never interpolates a key; review round 20, D20-1). `network_mode: service:<name>` of a service of the same model stays (D-9).
- **Volumes** (D-7): before `up` creates containers, the pipeline creates each missing named volume with the labels of the environment: the volumes of the project (`<project>_<key>` of the model, and `<project>_<source>` of the `mounts` of devcontainer.json and of Features, which the CLI puts into the project) with `nimblescape.devenv.volume=compose`, other named volumes with `nimblescape.devenv.volume=additional`. Review round 17 (D17-1): the `mounts` are substituted first as the CLI substitutes them at `up` (`composeMountVolumes` with `helperCliVariables` and `environmentDevcontainerId`; review round 18, D18-1: with `env` `{ HOME: '/root', COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME: <project> }`, the variable that the pipeline passes to the CLI runs of Docker Compose, also in the host access input of Docker Compose, `hostAccessInput` with `composeMounts`; not in `HELPER_PROCESS_ENV_NAMES`, whose values are not known; the CLI's `Q_` over the object of the single `--id-label` `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id=<id>`, `ht`), never with `DEVCONTAINER_ID_PLACEHOLDER`; a source that is then still no key of Compose (`COMPOSE_VOLUME_KEY`, `^[a-zA-Z0-9._-]+$`, the compose-go schema; Compose does not interpolate keys) is left out and logged. `compose` volumes are never shared with another environment (`isSameOwnerAdditionalVolume` needs `additional`). All of them join the additional volumes of the entry by the unit-5 rules (`recordedVolumes`, over every container of the environment).
- **Containers and lookups** (D-4): `EngineDocker.findContainer` (before plan step 11I2, `ContainerAdapter.findContainer`) skips containers with `nimblescape.devenv.compose-service` unless they have the name of the environment (`isDevContainer`; review round 1, D2: an image could give a single container that label), so it always returns a dev container: by the one rule of plan step 11I (U4, decision of 2026-10-08; `devContainerOf` in `src/core/worker/environmentContainers.ts`), the one with the recorded name whatever its state, else the newest running one, else the newest one. The open and the Git state of Delete's check and of a release use it; the refresh, Stop and the token removal take the same rule (Stop and the token removal for every running dev container, the named one first), and a window reads only the container that it is attached to (`windowState`). The labels `nimblescape.devenv.…` and `devcontainer.…` (except `devcontainer.metadata`) of the environment image and of the existing images of the other services are refused before containers are created (`imageLabelItems`, class *protected*), also for a single container. The labels of Docker Compose on images are allowed (review round 2, D2-1: an image that Compose built for another project, inherited through `FROM`, was refused before): Compose sets its own on its containers, and `buildOverrideConfig` gives a single container `--label com.docker.compose.project= --label com.docker.compose.service=` (`COMPOSE_CLEARED_LABELS`), so that `docker compose -p <project> down` of the user does not remove it; the final check of the override `runArgs` (`HostAccessInput.overrideConfiguration`) and the merged configuration allow exactly these two, the repository's `runArgs` do not. A container of Compose is recognized by the project label together with a label that Compose sets only on containers (`com.docker.compose.container-number` or `.config-hash`, `isComposeContainer`, review round 2, D2-4), not by the project label alone, which an image can carry. Stop, the Session Monitor, `inspectStates`, Delete, and the restore find all containers by `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id`; `inspectStates` takes the state of the environment from its dev container (`isDevContainer`; plan step 11I, U4: the one by the rule `devContainerOf`) and sets `servicesRunning` when another service runs (plan step 11I, U4: or another dev container) (review round 7, P7-2: before, any running container made the environment running, so the sidebar hid Start while the dev container was stopped).
- **Start** (D-15, D-20, D-22): a stopped dev container starts with `up` (Compose starts the services of `runServices`, default all). When the dev container runs, the CLI does not call Compose, so stopped services are started with `docker start` first. Without the workspace helper the open fails with `helperFailed` (user decision 2026-09-29: no previous helper image), except that when the dev container runs already and no container of the environment must be created again, the dev container opens as it is (review round 23 of PR #64, A-R23-1), and its stopped side services start with `docker start` (D-22; review round 2 of PR #64, B1); when the configuration cannot be read or used (no model, so no `up`), the start fails with `startFailed` and nothing starts (user decision 2026-09-29: no `docker start` fallback, no start of the containers as they are), except that when the dev container runs already and no container of the environment must be created again, the dev container opens as it is (review round 23 of PR #64, A-R23-1), and its stopped side services start with `docker start` (D-22; review round 19 of PR #64, R19-2).
- **Stop** (D-20): `docker stop` of the dev container first (after the Git summary), then of the running other services; also when the dev container does not run. The Session Monitor checks again before each further container that no window uses the environment (review round 1, D4); if one does, the others keep running.
- **Host access checks off** (container restrictions section 12): the check, the rewrite, and the metadata check follow the switch of the repository. Each refused item has the class that the same setting has for a single container (`composeAccessClassification`): with the checks off only `computer` is lifted. The rewrite then keeps the published ports and the lifted mounts as the model has them, and every container gets `nimblescape.devenv.host-access=unrestricted`; with the checks on, every container gets `nimblescape.devenv.host-access=checked` (review round 2, D2-2: the model sets the label on every service either way, so a label of an image that Compose builds or pulls during `up`, which is not checked before, cannot make a container look unrestricted and have it created again at every open). With this update, the model of every existing Compose environment changes once, so Compose creates its side services again once at the next open (their named volumes are kept). With the checks on again, an environment whose dev container or any other container has this label is not current: `up --remove-existing-container` creates the dev container again, and Compose the services whose model changed (the label is gone from it).
- **Delete** (D-19): the containers with `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id`, then the other containers of the project (label `com.docker.compose.project`, for example of `docker compose run`; never one whose `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id` names another environment, review round 1, D3), the networks of the project, the images of the build record's `compose.images` and every `<project>-*` image labelled with the environment's ID (user decisions 2026-10-03; also without a build record, after a failed first open or a lost registry; not one whose `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id` names another environment), the environment images, and the workspace volume. The question of Delete lists the other additional volumes as before (modal, Remove or Keep), then the data of the services in a multi-select list with none ticked ("hold data of the services, for example of a database"): only ticked ones are removed, Escape cancels the Delete (`removableServiceDataVolumes`; `removableAdditionalVolumes` leaves them out). The data of the services are classified by use, not by the label (review round 1, D1; relabelling would break `isSameOwnerAdditionalVolume`): an own volume with `nimblescape.devenv.volume=compose`, an own volume that a service other than the dev service mounts in the model (`composeServiceVolumeNames`, recorded at each open in the entry as `serviceVolumes` and kept once recorded, so that it works when the configuration cannot be read at Delete, or has changed since), or one that a container of another service mounts now (read at Delete, which needs no helper run). Review round 2 (D2-3): `createComposeVolumes` also labels each volume that a service other than the dev service mounts `nimblescape.devenv.service-data=true` (`LABEL_SERVICE_DATA`; `nimblescape.devenv.volume` unchanged), which counts as service data and from which `reconcileFromVolumes` restores `serviceVolumes`; an entry without `serviceVolumes` and without a build record (for example restored from its volumes) lists every own additional volume in the question about the data of the services, none ticked. A failed first open removes the containers, networks, and images of the project the same way.
- **Switching kind only with a build** (review round 1, P-1): a configuration of the other kind (Docker Compose or a single container) than the environment (its dev container, else its build record) applies only with a build. Without one (**Rebuild later**, a failed or refused update), the open keeps the environment as it is (`configurationOfKind`): the containers of Docker Compose are kept but not started (the start fails with `startFailed`, user decision 2026-09-29: without a Docker Compose configuration there is no `up`), except that when the dev container runs already and no container of the environment must be created again, the dev container opens as it is (review round 23 of PR #64, A-R23-1), and its stopped side services start with `docker start` (D-22; review round 19 of PR #64, R19-2); a single container starts as a container whose configuration is not known. With a build, a single container is replaced by the containers of Docker Compose (the progress says that files outside the repository are removed, `Messages.containerComposeCreated`).
- **A configuration that no longer uses Docker Compose** (after a build): the dev container of Compose is created again (the progress says so, `Messages.containerComposeReplaced`: the named volumes of the services are kept; their volumes without a name are no longer used and stay in Docker as unused volumes, which the extension does not remove), its merged configuration is not checked (the CLI finds containers by `--id-label`, which every container of the project has, so it could merge the metadata of another service; the image metadata is checked before `up`), and before `up` creates the single container the containers of the other services are removed (their volumes stay); after `up`, the networks of the project. The images that Compose built go with the next build record (`removeEnvironmentImages` with the old record's `compose.images`). Review round 2 (D2-4): when `up` fails after a build that switched the kind (Compose to a single container or back), the pipeline does not fall back to the previous environment image in the other kind (a single container from the image of the Compose dev service, or Compose from the image of a single container): the new image is removed and the open ends with `startFailed`, whose detail names what the switch removed before `up` (the containers of the other services, or the single container; `kindSwitchFailure`) and that the volumes are kept; a rebuild tries again.
- **Restore after a lost registry**: `reconcileFromVolumes`: the `compose` volumes carry `nimblescape.devenv.volume` and the labels of the environment, so they join the restored entry (`isOwnVolume`); the volumes with `nimblescape.devenv.service-data=true` become its `serviceVolumes` (review round 2, D2-3); the containers of all services are found by `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id`. Without a build record, the next open builds and runs `up --remove-existing-container` in the same project, and Compose keeps or recreates the services.
- **Ignored** (D-18): `runArgs`, `appPort`, `workspaceMount`, and `build.options` of a Compose configuration have no effect in the CLI; they are not checked and not passed, with a log line.
- **Volumes before each `up`** (review round 1, P-2): the missing volumes of the model and of the `mounts` are created with the labels of the environment before every Compose `up`, also when `up` only adds containers (a service that the model gained, started after **Rebuild later**); the check of the model and of the image metadata runs again only when `up` creates the dev container.
- **Build paths and networks of other environments** (review round 1, S1, S2): a local build context or Dockerfile is checked with its real path (a link out of the repository, a missing path, or a path of the workspace helper: `isHelperPath`, class *protected*; the same helper paths are refused for `build.context`/`build.dockerfile` of a single container, checked as written). A local build whose Dockerfile the model run could not read is refused. The labels and the attached containers of every network that the model names (`composeNetworkReferences`) are read before the check (`EngineDocker.inspectNetworks`; before plan step 11I2, `ContainerAdapter.inspectNetworks`); a network of another environment is refused (`foreignNetworkItem`), as the network of another environment in `runArgs` of a single container.
- **Images of other environments** (review round 1, S4): `imageReferenceFinding` normalized the reference (`docker.io/`, `index.docker.io/`, `registry-1.docker.io/`, `library/`, tags and digests; since 2026-09-28 only the check of the environment images does, through `localImageRepository`; now `environmentImageNames`, user decisions of 2026-10-03) and refused the namespace `devenv-` (class *protected*; removed 2026-09-28, see the note "Images of other accounts by their ID") and refuses image IDs (not supported) in `image` of each service, `docker-image://` additional contexts, and for a single container in `image` and `--build-context` of `build.options`. Review round 2 (S2-02): the images of each Dockerfile and `dockerfile_inline` (`extractImageReferences`, `dockerfileImageFindings`: `FROM`, `COPY --from=<ref>`, `RUN --mount=…,from=<ref>`, `# syntax=<ref>`, with the build arguments) were refused the same way (removed 2026-09-27, see the Dockerfile refusals removed note). Review round 2 (S2-05): by its form only `sha256:…` and 64 hexadecimal characters are IDs; for every other reference that exists locally the pipeline reads `RepoTags`/`RepoDigests` (`EngineDocker.imageNames`; before plan step 11I2, `ContainerAdapter.imageNames`) and refuses it as not supported when none of them names the normalized reference (`resolvedByImageId`: Docker took it for an ID prefix). Review round 9 (S9-3): only when the configuration is not refused already, with one `docker image inspect` per 100 references (then `ContainerAdapter.inspectImageNames`; since plan step 11I2, `EngineDocker.inspectImageNames` asks the Engine API one request per reference, there is no batch; at most `MAX_IMAGE_ID_REFERENCES`, 1000, beyond which the configuration is too large), and `imageIdResolvedReferences` resolves each reference as Docker does (a name of a found image first, then a prefix of an ID or a digest that is an ID). Review round 10 (P10-1): when Docker fails for a batch for another reason than missing images only (every line of stderr "No such image"; before, one such line anywhere counted as all missing), for example "invalid reference format" of one reference, `inspectImageNames` inspects the references of that batch one by one (a batch that ran out of time is not retried); a reference whose own inspect fails for another reason than a missing image, and every reference when the answer cannot be read, is refused as not supported (`imageUncheckedItem`: "image … (the image reference could not be checked)"), never left unchecked, so one invalid reference in an unused service no longer lets an ID prefix of another reference pass. Review round 11 (G1, G2): before any inspect, each reference is checked against Docker's reference grammar (`isValidImageReference`: `parseImageReference`, as github.com/distribution/reference: lowercase path components, the separators `.`, `_`, `__`, `-`, a tag of at most 128 characters, a digest; no surrounding whitespace); one that it rejects is refused as not supported ("image … (not a valid image reference)", `imageInvalidReferenceItem`), conservatively: nothing that the grammar rejects is accepted, whatever Docker would do. `inspectImageNames` returns, for each reference that it could not check, the reason (`ImageUncheckedReason`): `invalid`, a definitive answer about the reference itself (matched loosely: "invalid reference format", "must be lowercase", an ID prefix of several images, "multiple IDs found with provided prefix" of the classic image store, "ambiguous …" or "multiple images …" of the containerd image store), or `transient` (a timeout, a daemon that cannot be reached or fails, any unknown error or output, an error thrown by `run`, for example `dockerNotInstalled`, which it catches itself; it throws only an AbortError). `invalid` references stay refused items of the configuration (so a refused update is remembered); any `transient` one makes `imageIdItems` throw `AnalysisFailedError` of kind `internal` (P9-1: no new or changed configuration is used, the existing environment starts at the load, and during an update it is not remembered but warns with `Messages.updateCheckFailed`); before, a timeout or a daemon error was a refusal ("needs access to your computer"), remembered as a refused update, and refused the start of an existing environment. Review round 12 (P12-1): `isValidImageReference` (not `parseImageReference`, which other callers use) also applies the rules of go-digest (`sha256` with 64, `sha384` with 96, `sha512` with 128 lowercase hexadecimal characters; any other algorithm, length, or case is refused) and the bound of 255 characters to the name as Docker normalizes it (`docker.io/library/<name>` for a short name of Docker Hub, `docker.io/<path>` for `docker.io` and `index.docker.io`), and `IMAGE_REFERENCE_ERROR` also matches `checksum digest`, `digest algorithm`, and `must not be more than`, so Docker's answers to such references count as `invalid` (before: `transient`, and every open failed with the internal error). A `transient` failure is `AnalysisFailure` of kind `internal` with `docker: true`: its item is `dockerCheckItem` ("Docker could not check the image references (…)") and its message `Messages.configurationCheckDocker` ("… Check that Docker is running and try again."), in place of the "reinstall Dev Environments" of an analysis that could not run; it behaves as any `internal` failure. Review round 13 (P13-1): a failure of Docker no longer hides a refusal of the policy: `imageIdItems` returns the grammar-invalid references as the refusal before any inspect (the configuration is refused for them anyway, as by the `isRefused` short-circuit of its callers; before, a transient answer for another reference dropped them, the existing environment started with the message about Docker, and a refused update was not remembered); after the inspect it returns the items of the `invalid` answers (and of image IDs) also when other references are `transient`, which it logs; it throws `AnalysisFailedError` only when there is no definitive item, and a `transient` answer never becomes `imageUncheckedItem`. So a refused update with a reference that Docker calls invalid is remembered also when Docker could not answer for another reference. Review round 14 (P14-2): `imageIdResolvedReferences` ran over all references with the images of the batches before a transient failure, so a reference that was never inspected (`cafe`, with a local `cafe:latest`) was refused as an image ID when another found image had an ID starting with `cafe`, and, since P13-1, remembered as a refused update. The ID test now runs only over the references with a definitive answer (without the `transient` ones), which take the transient path: a logged warning when there are other definitive items, else `AnalysisFailedError`. The test double of `inspectImageNames` gives, like the adapter, the first transient reference and all after it `transient`, the ones before it their answers. The one-by-one fallback of the CLI adapter (only for a batch whose errors were all missing or invalid references) made at most `MAX_IMAGE_INSPECT_SINGLE_CALLS` (100) calls (the references beyond were `transient`), and stopped at the first timeout or daemon failure of a batch or a single call (that reference and all that were not checked yet were `transient`); `ctx.signal` went to each `run` and was checked between the calls (before: up to about 1000 calls of up to 60 s each, which Cancel could not stop). Plan step 11I2 and PR D of 11I: `EngineDocker.inspectImageNames` asks one reference at a time, with the signal: a reference that the engine refuses as such (400) is `invalid`; after the first other failure it asks no more, and that reference and the rest are `transient`; the batches, `IMAGE_INSPECT_BATCH`, `MAX_IMAGE_INSPECT_SINGLE_CALLS` and `IMAGE_REFERENCE_ERROR` are gone. Review round 2 (S2-01): the Dockerfile of a single container is read at the path of the configuration as `read-configuration` resolved it (`readConfigFiles` with `dockerfile`, `READ_FILES_SCRIPT` `argv[3]`); a configured Dockerfile that cannot be read is not supported (`HostAccessInput.dockerfileUnreadable`).
