- **Inputs of the build client in the helper** (review round 2, S2-03): local `additional_contexts` (also `oci-layout://`), the SSH key files of `build.ssh`, and the `file` of top-level secrets that `build.secrets` names are read by the build client in the workspace helper: `COMPOSE_MODEL_SCRIPT` records their real paths, and a path of the workspace helper, a link out of the repository, a missing path, or a relative path is refused whatever the switch says (`helperInputProblems`); otherwise the rule of the setting (class *computer*) applies. Runtime secrets and configs of services stay class *computer* (bind mounts of the engine). `--build-context` with a folder of a single container follows the same helper-path rule (a relative folder too). Review rounds 2 and 3 of PR #130 (`localContextPath`, `ociLayoutFolder`): every value except an image (`docker-image://`), a URL (`http://`, `https://`) and a target (`target:`, and `service:` of Docker Compose), each by its exact prefix, is a folder for Buildx, read as written (not trimmed; `oci-layout://` only by its exact prefix, its tag and digest taken off as Buildx's `ocilayout.Parse` does): bake reads `cwd://<path>` as `<path>`, and another scheme or a prefix in another case or after a space is a relative folder, so it is refused whatever the switch says. A Git reference by SSH or `git://` is refused the same way (Buildx fetches it when it can parse it, else reads it as a folder): a Git context only by `https://`.
- **Networks** (review round 2, S2-04, P2-2): `NetworkInfo.id`; each written reference (`--network`, `network_mode`, named and external networks) is resolved as Docker resolves it (full ID, name, unique ID prefix; `resolveNetworkReference`), the state is keyed by the reference, and the name rules apply to the resolved name too. The environments of the containers attached to a network are mapped to their owners through the registry (`NetworkState.sameOwnerEnvironments`): only an environment of another owner, or without an entry, makes it another environment's network; a `<name of another environment>_` project network or a network with the project label of another environment stays refused also for the same owner.
- **Cache volume** (L-5): the CLI writes a compose file into `/devenv-cache/docker-compose/` at each container creation; before each Compose `up`, `WRITE_AND_RUN_SCRIPT` removes those files older than 30 days (the CLI writes a missing one again without a build).
- **Review round 3.** P3-1: `READ_FILES_SCRIPT` and `COMPOSE_MODEL_SCRIPT` tell a Dockerfile or build context that does not exist in the repository (no link leads to or through it: `dockerfileMissing`, `missing`) apart from one that cannot be read, links out, or keeps a variable; only the first is a plain error of the configuration (`Messages.buildFileMissing`, code `buildFailed`), so the existing environment starts and nothing is built (a Docker Compose environment does not start then: there is no model for `up`, and no `docker start` fallback, user decision 2026-09-29, except that when the dev container runs already and no container of the environment must be created again, the dev container opens as it is (review round 23 of PR #64, A-R23-1), and its stopped side services start with `docker start` (D-22; review round 19 of PR #64, R19-2)); the others stay refused (for the images of the Dockerfile, removed 2026-09-27, see the Dockerfile refusals removed note; since U1/U2 a Dockerfile of a single container that links out or cannot be read is refused as protected, whatever the switch says, as the same link of Docker Compose; a Dockerfile with a variable that is not resolved is that case too). P3-2: `configHash` of a single container hashes the Dockerfile at the resolved path in the open. D3-1, P3-3: a failed `up` of a switch to Docker Compose removes the containers of Compose that it created (`removeFailedComposeContainers`; their volumes stay), and a single-container `up` counts every container of the environment with `nimblescape.devenv.compose-service` as a leftover: removed before an `up` that creates the container, and without one the start fails and asks for a rebuild (the CLI would take such a container for the dev container). D3-2: the CLI 0.89.0 sets no `devcontainer.config_file` on containers created with `--id-label` (`dg`: `if(t)return{…,idLabels:t}`), so the configuration path of a restored entry cannot be read from its containers; an entry without build record whose containers are of the other kind than the configuration asks (`configurationChanged`): **Rebuild later** keeps the kind: a single container starts as it is, while a Docker Compose environment does not start (`startFailed`; since user decision 2026-09-29 there is no `docker start` fallback), unless its dev container runs already and no container of the environment must be created again (the dev container then opens as it is, D-22; review round 18 of PR #64, R18-1; review round 23 of PR #64, A-R23-1); **Rebuild now** switches. P3-4: an entry that knows neither the volumes of its services nor its build lists its additional volumes in the question of Delete as "additional volume (possibly data of services)". S3-1 to S3-6: see container restrictions sections 1, 10, and 13 (folders of the kernel as helper paths, a single build context outside the repository refused, `build.options` merged over `build.args`/`build.target` with `BUILDKIT_SYNTAX`, all stages whatever the target, unresolved references with `devenv`, flags unquoted as BuildKit's `extractBuilderFlags`, the files of `--secret`, `--ssh`, `--output`).
- **Review round 4.** D4-1: `runComposeUp` records the IDs of the containers of Compose of the project after it removed the single container (`PipelineContext.composeSwitch`); after a failed `up`, `removeFailedComposeContainers` removes only the others, and only when the single container was removed in this run (a cancelled earlier switch may have left containers that the user works with); `kindSwitchFailure` names the kept ones. The previous kind is still taken from the build record (a container of Compose with a record of a single container never falls back to the single image). D4-2: every container that `up` creates carries `nimblescape.devenv.config-path` (the override configuration of a single container, and the up model of Compose for every service; the policy allows exactly this label of the override configuration, `isConfigPathLabelValue`), and `reconcileFromVolumes` restores the configuration path from it (the dev container first). The question of D3-2 also comes when the dev container of Compose is gone but containers of other services exist; **Later** then starts nothing and removes nothing (`Messages.composeDevContainerMissing`). D4-3: the question is `PipelineUi.configurationKindChanged` with `Messages.configurationKindChanged` (the switch, what **Rebuild now** removes, **Select configuration…**); **Later** is the answer of a dismissed dialog. P4-1: `missingInRepository` of both scripts follows a chain of links (readlink, targets resolved against the real folder of the link, at most 32 links, every step in the repository): a chain that leads nowhere in the repository is a missing path; a link out, in a circle, or a path that exists for `stat` is not. P4-2: in the Compose path, devcontainer.json (without the properties that Compose ignores) is checked before `composeMissingBuildPaths`. P4-3: containers of other services next to a single dev container are removed (`docker rm -f`, volumes stay) before a start without a new container; the start goes on. S4-1 to S4-6: see container restrictions sections 1 and 13 (`--build-arg NAME` without a value, URL-like build contexts resolved as paths, the pattern operators of BuildKit's shell lexer, only the official Dockerfile frontends, `--output` parsed as buildx does, registry images with `devenv` in the name).
- **Review round 5.** D5-1: the up model sets `nimblescape.devenv.config-path` only on the dev service; a label of the other services would change their Compose configuration hash at each switch of the selected configuration between two devcontainer.json files that share one compose file, and Compose would recreate them (data in their container file systems lost). Trade-off: without the dev container, `reconcileFromVolumes` takes the default configuration (old side containers that still carry the label are read as before), and the kind question of the pipeline still comes from the count of `nimblescape.devenv.compose-service` containers. D5-2: `buildOverrideConfig` and `composeUpModel` add the label only when `isConfigPathLabelValue` accepts the path, which now follows `isValidFolderName` of detect.ts (a backslash and white space allowed; not empty, `.`, `..`, or with `/`). D5-3: the question about a switch between Docker Compose and a single container in a connected window was removed with the command that switched branches (user decision 2026-10-01). The command Rebuild keeps its behaviour (no question). P5-4: `Messages.configurationKindChangedDevContainerMissing` when the dev container of Compose is missing (Later starts nothing). P5-2, S5-1 to S5-4: `detectSyntax` (dockerfile.ts) ports BuildKit's `DetectSyntax` (BOM, `#!` line, `#`, `//`, JSON) and cuts the value at the first space (`cutAtSpace`, also for `BUILDKIT_SYNTAX`); `SHELL_NAME` reads variable names as `processName` of BuildKit's lexer (cross-checked against BuildKit's lex.go with a Go harness outside the repository), ARG names are any non-empty text, and an unresolved `$NAME` before an escape or a quote is kept as `${NAME}`; `unresolvedVariants` (hostAccess.ts) gives the texts of a reference with its unresolved variables left out or replaced by the word of `:-`/`:+` (at most 256), refused when one holds `devenv` or has an image-ID form; `singleBuildArguments` makes each `build.args` value a text with `String(value)` (an object is refused as unsupported); a remote Compose build context is refused as unsupported, and `dockerfile_inline` is checked whatever the context. These checks of D-17 are superseded by the isolated builder (one BuildKit per build) of the next unit.
- **Review round 7.** P7-1: `rewriteModel` rewrites `restart` other than `no`/`on-failure[:n]` to `no` and a `deploy.restart_policy.condition` other than `none`/`on-failure` (also a missing one) to `none`, and logs it; the check no longer refuses them (the templates set `restart: unless-stopped` on the database). P7-2: `EnvironmentRuntimeState.container` is the state of the dev container; `servicesRunning` keeps Stop in the sidebar (`rowActions`) and adds `services running` to the state text (plan step 11I, U4: `containers running`, set also for another running dev container of the environment). D7-1: `stopServiceBeforeRemoval` runs `docker stop` (the container's own stop time, at most 20 s by the policy) for a running container with `nimblescape.devenv.compose-service` before `docker rm -f` in Delete, `removeComposeServices`, and the removal of strays; a failed stop is logged. S7-1: the pattern forms of the Dockerfile (`${A#p}`, `${A%p}`, `${A/p/r}` and their doubled forms) use `matchShellPattern`, a simulation of the pattern's automaton with threads by priority (leftmost-first, as Go's regexp and JavaScript choose) in O(value × pattern) instead of a backtracking regular expression; one budget of `MAX_PATTERN_STEPS` (10⁷) per Dockerfile, beyond it `tooComplex` (unsupported). S7-2: `analyzeDockerfileImages` keeps one ordered list of stage names and a count per reference (`stagesBefore`), a Map of the references, and refuses a Dockerfile over `MAX_DOCKERFILE_LENGTH` (1 MiB) or `MAX_DOCKERFILE_INSTRUCTIONS` (20,000) as too large.
- **Review round 8.** The host access analysis runs in a worker thread with limits of time and memory (section 7, "Host access analysis in a worker thread"). S8-6 (D-14): `rewriteModel` rewrites every `restart` other than `no` (also `on-failure[:n]`) to `no` and every `deploy.restart_policy.condition` other than `none` (also `on-failure`) to `none`: Docker starts a container with `on-failure` again when the Docker daemon starts, outside the Session Monitor. P8-1: `deploy.restart_policy` `delay`, `window`, and `max_attempts`, `deploy.replicas: 1`, and `deploy.mode: replicated` are allowed; `rewriteModel` removes `max_attempts` where the condition is `none` (Docker Engine 25 and newer refuse a count of retries with the policy `no`) and logs it. `stop_grace_period` over 20 s is capped at `20s` and logged (before: refused); one that cannot be read stays refused. P8-2: a bind mount of a folder of the repository that does not exist yet (for example a data folder in `.gitignore`) is allowed when `create_host_path` is not `false` and the nearest path at or above it that exists is a folder whose real path is in the repository and no path of the workspace helper (`ComposeModelOutput.mountAncestors` of `COMPOSE_MODEL_SCRIPT`); `decideServiceMount` mounts its subpath of the workspace volume as for an existing folder and names it in `createFolder`, `composeUpModel` returns the folders in `createFolders`, and `runComposeUp` creates them before `up` (`WorkspaceHelper.createRepositoryFolders`, `CREATE_FOLDERS_SCRIPT`: the step `createFolders`, as the owner of the repository, so without the Docker socket unless root owns the repository, section 17; each missing part created one at a time and checked, never through a link out of the repository). A nearest folder that is a link out of the repository is refused (protected), one that is no folder or not known (an older helper) as before. P8-3/D8-1: `stopServiceBeforeRemoval` also runs before `docker rm -f` in `removeFailedComposeContainers`, `removeComposeProject`, and `removeFailedFirstOpen`.
- **Review round 11.** G1, G2: see "Images of other environments". G3, G4, G5: see section 7, "Ownership".
- **Review round 12.** P12-1: see "Images of other environments". D12-1, D12-2, P12-2, S12-1: see section 7, "Ownership".
- **Review round 13.** P13-1: see "Images of other environments". D13-1, D13-2, D13-3: see section 7, "Ownership".
- **Review round 14.** P14-2: see "Images of other environments". P14-1, S14-1: see section 7, "Ownership".
- **Review round 15.** K1 (S15-1), K2 (S15-2): for Docker Compose, Dev Container CLI 0.89.0 does not give `mounts` to `docker run --mount`: `iW` reads a text with `lQ` (`split(",")`, `split("=")`, case-sensitive keys, only `src`/`dst`/`destination` renamed, the value ends at a second `=`), takes an object as it is, and writes each mount of `[...config.mounts, ...additional]` unquoted as `- ${source}:${target}` (`nW`, type dropped, nothing escaped) into the `volumes` of the dev service of its generated compose file; the mounts with `type === "volume"` and a source also become top-level volumes (`oW`). The `mounts` of the configuration reach `up` through the image metadata written at the build (the override configuration of `up` replaces devcontainer.json). `HostAccessInput.composeMounts` makes `readHostAccessFindings` (since PR #32, `hostAccessFindings` of `src/core/policy/single.ts`) check every `mounts` entry of every source (configuration, merged configuration, image metadata) also with `composeCliMountProblems`, besides the Docker-style `mountProblems`; both see the entry after `resolvedInput` (the image metadata substituted with `helperCliVariables` as for a single container, `${devcontainerId}` as `DEVCONTAINER_ID_PLACEHOLDER`), and an entry with leftovers (`mountEntries`) gets only the item of `mountEntryProblems` (review round 16, L3: the raw label had made every `$` of the image metadata refused); the pipeline sets it for the check of devcontainer.json, of the merged configuration after `read-configuration`, and of the image metadata before Compose `up` (`checkMetadataHostAccess(…, composeMounts)`). `cliComposeMount` reads like `lQ` and refuses a field of `source`/`target`/`type`/`src`/`dst`/`destination` in another case or with spaces, twice, without a value, or with `=` in the value; the reading must equal Docker's (`parseMountEntry`) in source, target, and type; `$` (except `${devcontainerId}`, which the CLI resolves before it writes the file), white space, quotes, `#`, `:`, and a leading YAML indicator are refused in the source and the target, and so are a target that is not absolute and a source of a type other than `volume` (other than a path of type `bind` or without a type, which is a bind mount): all as not supported ("mount … is written differently by the Dev Container CLI and is not supported"). Then the line as Compose reads it: a path source is `bind mount <source>` (class *computer*), a name gets `volumeNameProblems`, no source is an anonymous volume; the target gets `targetProblems` (`CONFIG_FOLDER`) and the rule of `decideServiceMount` for `/workspaces` (repeated here: `policy/single.ts` does not use `decideServiceMount`). The test `hostAccess.composeMounts.test.ts` keeps a copy of `lQ` and `nW` and checks that the vendored CLI still has exactly that code. Single containers are not affected (the CLI passes a text to `--mount` as it is, and an object as `type=…,src=…,dst=…`, as `parseMountEntry` reads it). K3, K4: see section 7, "Ownership".
- **Review round 16.** L1 (D16-1 = S16-1): `iW` also writes `user: ${containerUser}`, `- '${name}=${escaped value}'` for `containerEnv` (the name is not escaped), the `entrypoints` joined raw inside the double-quoted `/bin/sh -c` script, and `- ${value}` for each `capAdd` and `securityOpt`, all of the merged configuration (`ji` of the metadata: the last `containerUser`, every `entrypoint`, the union of `containerEnv`, `capAdd`, `securityOpt`). `composeTextProblems` (`policy/single.ts`, only with `composeMounts`, so only for Docker Compose) checks each source of `configurationSources` (the configuration, the merged configuration, and every entry of the image metadata after `resolvedInput`) with `COMPOSE_USER`, `COMPOSE_ENV_NAME`, `COMPOSE_ENTRYPOINT_REFUSED`, `COMPOSE_CAPABILITY`, and `COMPOSE_SECURITY_OPTION`; a value that is not a text is refused too (the CLI stringifies it, and a list of one text is that text); all as *unsupported*, so the switch does not lift them. `hostAccess.composeText.test.ts` has the vectors of the findings, and a guard that extracts `iW`, `lQ`, `nW`, and `oW` from the vendored bundle, runs `iW` on candidates (plain values combined with quotes, `$`, `#`, `: `, YAML indicators, line breaks including `\r`, NEL, U+2028) that the policy allows with the checks off in any source, parses the output with js-yaml (in `package-lock.json` through `@vscode/vsce`), and requires exactly the expected keys of the dev service (`entrypoint`, `command`, and the key of the value) and the values as given, and no line break that go-yaml reads but js-yaml does not; it found that `securityOpt` may not end with `:` (YAML would read `- x:` as a mapping). The single container is not affected: `-u`, `-e`, `--entrypoint`, `--cap-add`, and `--security-opt` are separate arguments of `docker run`, without a shell. The rule of `environment` of the dev service uses `refusedVariable` of the merge of #27 (the part before `=`, trimmed; names with a space or a control character); `composeAccess.test.ts` and `composeAccess.checksOff.test.ts` cover `GH_TOKEN=x`, ` GH_HOST `, `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=1=2`, and names with a space or a line break. Dp: the build-time compose file of `Dp` writes `- _DEV_CONTAINERS_BASE_IMAGE=${stage}` (`build.target`, else the label of the last `FROM` line by `yj`/`Fj`; in `jj` and `Wj`) and, with Features (`Wj`), `- _DEV_CONTAINERS_IMAGE_USER=${user}` (`Tj`: `QG` over the USER instructions of the chain of the target, resolved by `Cg`/`Lj` with the build arguments, the ARG and ENV values, and `Config.Env` of the image, else `Config.User` of the image, else `root`) unquoted into `build.args`; a line break (go-yaml also breaks at NEL, U+2028, U+2029) adds keys to the build (`ssh`, `secrets`, `additional_contexts` with a file of the helper) or to the dev service. The `target:` line is the constant `dev_containers_target_stage`; the paths, the image name, and `cache_from` are the CLI's own or ours; `version:` comes from our JSON model (`wp`). `devBuildTextProblems` (`policy/compose.ts`, the dev service only, *unsupported*) refuses a target or a last-stage name that is not `BUILD_STAGE_NAME`, a Dockerfile with NEL, and, when a USER instruction uses a variable, a build argument with a line break. `checkComposeBuildImages` (`environmentService.ts`, with Features, in `buildAndReplace` after the downloads and before `helper.build`) inspects the FROM images of the dev service (`composeDevBuildImages`: all stages, or the image of an image-only dev service; one that is not here is downloaded first, which also makes the CLI read the same local image) and refuses with `composeBuildImageItems` a `Config.User`, or with a USER instruction that uses a variable a `Config.Env` entry, with a line break, and a FROM image whose variables could not be resolved; the refusal is handled like a refused new image (remembered as a refused update when an old environment can start). The check of the user (`checkComposeBuildImages`, `composeDevBuildImages`, `composeBuildImageItems`, and the USER rule of `devBuildTextProblems`) was removed in the slim-down (below). `composeAccess.buildText.test.ts` checks the parts of `Dp`, `jj`, `Wj`, `Tj`, `Bg`, `DQ`, and `wp` in the vendored bundle and renders a copy of the template with the allowed values through js-yaml. L3 (P16-1): gone with the merge of #27; the label is substituted before the Compose reading (`hostAccess.composeMounts.test.ts`, `environmentService.compose.test.ts`).
- **Review round 17.** D17-1: for Docker Compose, the named volumes of `mounts` (devcontainer.json at the build, which read-configuration leaves with `${devcontainerId}` while no container exists; the image metadata at `up`, read raw from the label) became top-level volume keys of our build and up models with `${…}` in them, which Compose refuses (`^[a-zA-Z0-9._-]+$`, keys are not interpolated), so the docker-in-docker Feature (`dind-var-lib-docker-${devcontainerId}`) and `${localWorkspaceFolderBasename}-node_modules` failed. `composeMountVolumes` (`pipelineRules.ts`) now substitutes each mount with the variables of `up` (`helperCliVariables`, the same values that the host access check resolves for the metadata) and the real ID (`environmentDevcontainerId` in `cliVariables.ts`: `devcontainerIdOf` is `ht` plus `Q_`, `JSON.stringify` of the label object with sorted keys, SHA-256, `BigInt` in base 32, padded to 52; `cliVariables.test.ts` compares it with the CLI's own `Q_` and `ht` from the bundle), and skips a source that is still no Compose key (the CLI writes it; never a command). The volume is then created with the labels (`nimblescape.devenv.volume=compose`), recorded, and listed by Delete like the other project volumes. The host access check keeps `DEVCONTAINER_ID_PLACEHOLDER` for the ID (the ID is only a suffix of 52 characters of `0-9a-v`, inside the project prefix `<project>_`, so the check and the written name differ only there). `test/docker/compose.test.ts` mounts `history-${devcontainerId}`. P17-1, P17-2: the image and the user that the CLI computes for the build of the dev service (`composeDevBuildImages`, `cliDockerfile.ts`, a port of the CLI's reader, `composeBuildUserItems`, `ComposeAccessInput.features`, `cliHasFeatures`); removed in the slim-down (below). P17-3: `capped` (exported from `policy/report.ts`) after the merge and dedupe of `composeReport`, and for `imageIdItems` (items and log lines).
- **Review round 18.** S18-1: build arguments are built with own properties (`buildArgumentTexts` in `imageCheck/dockerfile.ts`, `Object.fromEntries` over the filtered entries, keeping `Object.prototype`), in `policy/compose.ts` (`buildProblems`, `composeImageReferences`), `compose.ts` (`composeReferences`), and `imageCheck.ts` (`collectReferences`); `singleBuildArguments` collects `build.options` `--build-arg` in a Map (the CLI passes `build.options` to `docker build` unchanged); `build.args` of a single container keeps its assignment (the CLI's parser of devcontainer.json drops a text key `__proto__` too), and its object values stay refused. An argument named `__proto__` was lost by the setter of `Object.prototype`, while Docker keeps it. P18-2: the expression `^\s*FROM.*` (multiline) of the name of the last stage (`lastStageName` of `devBuildTextProblems`, the CLI's `yj`) takes quadratic time on a run of blank lines; `hasLongBlankRun` refuses a run of whitespace with more than `MAX_BLANK_LINES` (200) line breaks before it runs (only without a build target, when the name is read). S18-2, P18-3, P18-1: the platform variables of the CLI, the architecture of the Docker Engine, and the base image that the CLI reads (`cliBaseImageCheck`, `checkCliBaseImage`); removed in the slim-down (below). D18-1: see "Volumes".
- **Review round 19.** S19-1: see "The checked model is the model that runs". Before, with a Compose that prints a literal `$` as `$$`, the checks read the escaped texts (a `dockerfile_inline` of another service `FROM $$X`, a bind mount or `env_file` path with `$$`) while Compose and BuildKit used the unescaped ones; `literal()` of `compose.ts` is gone (the dev service's `dockerfile_inline` and image are written as the model holds them), and `finish` always escapes. S19-3, P19-2: the build arguments and the empty base image of the CLI's reader; removed in the slim-down (below). S19-4: a single container is read first with `merged: false`; the check of the configuration and `imageIdItems` run before the read of the merged configuration (which inspects and pulls the image or the base image and merges its metadata), then the merged configuration is checked (again with the whole input, `imageIdItems` only for references not asked yet), with the handling of an unrestricted or Compose container as before, and the missing Dockerfile after it, as before. One more helper run per open of a single container. D19-1: the first read of a Docker Compose configuration (`merged: false`) and `composeConfigurationHash` get `env` `{ COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME: <project> }` too, so `${localEnv:COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME}` in the `mounts` of devcontainer.json is the project name (the CLI substitutes devcontainer.json at read-configuration) for the mounts, their volumes, and the checks, also when the merged read gives no merged configuration. P19-1: the helper image built and run for the platform of the Docker Engine (`--platform`, the platform in the tag, `checkHelperArchitecture`), which only served the platform variables of the CLI's reader; removed in the slim-down (below).
- **Review round 20.** P20-1, D20-1: see "Environment image" (the Dockerfile of the dev service that is always written) and "The checked model is the model that runs" (the keys). Before, since round 19, the CLI read the dev service's Dockerfile path with `$$` from the output of `docker compose config` while the checks used the unescaped texts. S20-1 (the build arguments of the CLI's view) and the copy of `<Dockerfile>.dockerignore` of P20-1 were removed in the slim-down (below).
- **Review round 22.** The guard rails on the final model (`policy/compose.ts`, container restrictions section 13): `links` to services of the model are allowed (H22-1); `volumes_from` of another service that is not the dev service is allowed, the dev service as its source and every `container:` source (and `--volumes-from` of a single container) are *protected*, and when the dev service takes the volumes of other services (also through their own `volumes_from`), the targets of their mounts follow the rules of the dev service (`volumesFromProblems`, H22-2); `capabilityProblems` allows Docker's default capabilities and `SYS_PTRACE` (H22-3); secrets and configs from `environment:` and configs with `content:` are allowed, a `file:` of the repository is not supported (a read-only bind mount is), one of the workspace helper is *protected*, any other one is *computer* (`secretConfigProblems`, H22-4); a driver or driver options of a top-level volume are not supported (the pipeline creates every volume itself, so they would be dropped; H22-5); `ipc: service:<name>` and `pid: service:<name>` of another service are allowed, but any `pid` sharing with the dev service (`devPidGroup`, followed in both directions), `pid: container:…`, and `--pid container:…` are *protected* (H22-6); `ComposeRewriteParams.dollarEscaped` is removed (`ComposeModelOutput.dollarEscaped` stays: the Docker tests read it; H22-7). D22-1: `runComposeUp` renames a dev container of another service of the project (`movePreviousDevContainer`: `docker rename` to Compose's default name, else stop and remove), removes Compose's leftovers in the state `created`, and stops the container of the new dev service before the CLI removes it; a failed start restores the previous configuration path of the entry. Final review (FC-1): a renamed previous dev container is stopped (never removed, nor its volumes), since a configuration whose `runServices` or `depends_on` leave its service out would otherwise leave it running past Stop, and `findContainer` prefers the container with the environment's `containerName` over any other label-matching one. Final review (FF-1): a previous dev container that already has Compose's default name (an earlier failed switch) is not renamed again (Docker refuses a rename to the current name) but only stopped; a failed switch of the dev service runs no restore `up` with the new configuration: it ends with the start failure, leaving the previous dev container stopped and intact, so the next open with the previous configuration starts it.
- **Slim-down: no model of the Dev Container CLI's reader.** Decision: the extension no longer keeps a port of the CLI's reader of the Dockerfile (`cliDockerfile.ts`) or any check that predicts the CLI's internal reading or rewriting, because the CLI can change them with any version. The safety comes from the checks of what Docker, Compose, and BuildKit do by their documented behaviour and of the final configuration: bind mounts of host files, the Docker socket, privileged mode, capabilities, devices, host namespaces, ports and `localPortHost`, the token, the volumes of other environments, and the images that Docker's own reading of the Dockerfile names (`imageCheck/dockerfile.ts`, `dockerfileImageFindings`: `FROM`, `COPY --from`, `RUN --mount from`, the official `# syntax` frontends; removed since, see "Dockerfile refusals removed"). Removed: `cliDockerfile.ts`; `cliBaseImageCheck`, `singleCliBaseImageCheck`, `usesCliDockerfile`, `dockerViewImages`, and `CLI_BASE_IMAGE_WHAT` (`hostAccess.ts`); `composeCliBaseImageCheck`, `composeDevBuildImages`, `composeBuildImageItems`, `composeBuildUserItems`, `devBuildPlan`, `devBuildUserItems`, `cliBuildArguments`, `cliHasFeatures`, and `ComposeAccessInput.features` and `.dollarEscaped` (`composeAccess.ts`); `checkCliBaseImage`, `checkComposeBuildImages`, and `LoadedConfiguration.cliPlatform` (`environmentService.ts`); `ContainerAdapter.engineArchitecture` and `imageArchitecture` (of the CLI adapter, itself removed in plan step 11I2), `--platform` of `buildImage`, `helperPlatform`, the platform in the helper tag, and `checkHelperArchitecture` (the tag of the helper image is the one of before round 19 again, so the first open after the update builds or reuses that tag); the read and copy of `<Dockerfile>.dockerignore` (`readDockerignore`, `ComposeModelOutput.dockerignores`, `COMPOSE_DEV_DOCKERIGNORE`, the size refusal and the count in `composeModelLimit`: the copy never took effect). Kept, because they model Compose and BuildKit or the files that the CLI writes, not its reader: the unescaping of the model for the checks and the escaping on write (S19-1, D20-1), the always written `COMPOSE_DEV_DOCKERFILE` (P20-1), `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME` in the reads (D19-1), the read of a single container with `merged: false` first (S19-4), the own properties of build arguments (S18-1), and the texts that the CLI writes into its compose files (`composeTextProblems`, `BUILD_STAGE_NAME` of the target and of the last stage, U+0085, with the blank-line limit of P18-2). Known limits that follow (container restrictions section 10): within one Docker Engine, a repository can reach the local images of other environments through its Dockerfile, which is not checked (only a Docker of its own for each account would close it, see `docs/concept-vm-isolation.md`, not planned; not by parsing); and the user that the CLI writes for a Compose build with Features is not checked.
- **Dockerfile refusals removed (user decision, 2026-09-27).** Under the trust model (README, "Security: what to expect": repositories are trusted code; the checks are a guard rail on the final configuration, not a sandbox), the Dockerfile is not checked, for single containers and for Docker Compose. Removed: the refusals of image references in the Dockerfile (`FROM`, `COPY --from`, `RUN --mount=…,from=`, `# syntax=`, `BUILDKIT_SYNTAX`: images of other environments, image IDs, variable forms that cannot be evaluated, references that are too long, Dockerfiles that are too large or too complex to check) and of a Dockerfile that could not be read (except for the dev service of Docker Compose, whose build writes the text that was read, P20-1; so is one longer than `MAX_DOCKERFILE_LENGTH`; see U1/U2 below for what came back); `dockerfileImageFindings`, `dockerfileImageReferences`, `singleBuildArguments`, `withDockerfileCache`, `unresolvedVariants`, `isOfficialFrontend`, `HostAccessInput.dockerfileText` (`hostAccess.ts`); `extractImageReferences`, `analyzeDockerfileImages`, `extractBuilderFlags`, `detectSyntax`, `cutAtSpace`, `UncheckedClass`, and the pattern matcher of variable forms (`trimShellPrefix`, `trimShellSuffix`, `replaceShellPattern`, `matchShellPattern`, `MAX_PATTERN_STEPS`) (`dockerfile.ts`); the Dockerfile images as references for the question of image IDs (`singleImageReferences`, `composeImageReferences`). Kept: `extractBaseImages` for the update notice (`collectReferences`, `composeReferences`, the helper image), which never refuses: a reference that it cannot evaluate (a variable that is not resolved, a pattern form) is skipped, and its limits (`MAX_DOCKERFILE_LENGTH`, `MAX_DOCKERFILE_INSTRUCTIONS`, `MAX_EXPANDED_LENGTH`, `MAX_NESTING`, `MAX_EXPANDED_CHARACTERS`, `MAX_REFERENCE_LENGTH`) protect the extension host and lead to no base images for that Dockerfile or reference; the reads of the Dockerfiles (update notice, configuration hashes, P20-1); every check of the final configuration, also the images that it names itself (`image`, `image:` of a service, `--build-context`/`additional_contexts` `docker-image://`); `composeTextProblems`, `BUILD_STAGE_NAME`, the `$$` escaping; the refusal of a remote build context of Compose (the value of the context is not checked); `--build-arg NAME` without a value. Review of the removal (U1, U2; a size limit and a path check of the configuration, not checks of the content, no refusal of image references): U1: a Dockerfile longer than `MAX_DOCKERFILE_LENGTH` is refused as not supported, whatever the switch says, for a single container (`HostAccessInput.dockerfileLength`: "the Dockerfile (longer than 1048576 characters; the Dockerfile is too large)") and for every Compose service with a Dockerfile that was read (`buildProblems`; the dev service keeps its reason of P20-1), because the helper reads at most one character more and the configuration hash sees only that text, so an edit after it would offer no rebuild. U2: a Dockerfile of a single container that the configuration names and that exists but has no text (`resolvedDockerfile` → `unreadable`, `HostAccessInput.dockerfileUnreadable`: a link out of the repository, for example to the internal folder `/workspaces/.devenv+` or the cache volume, a real path outside of it, also the path that the CLI resolved from a variable, or a path with a variable that is not resolved) is refused as protected, whatever the switch says ("Dockerfile … (the Dockerfile is a link out of the repository or could not be read)"): the CLI reads it with `readFile` (it follows the link) and BuildKit prints lines of it in its errors. Compose refuses the same link as protected (`localPathProblems`). The Dockerfile of another Compose service that could not be read and is no link out of the repository is not refused; the update check and the configuration hash skip it.
- **Images of other accounts by their ID (user decision, 2026-09-28).** The rule on the name `devenv-…` (imageReferenceFinding) is gone: it refused any image that a user named like that, one environment's image to another environment of the same account, and missed a copy under another name. Instead, when a reference of the configuration found a local image in the `docker image inspect` of imageIdItems, or Docker found it missing under the name of an environment image (review round 3, S1), `hostEnvironmentImageIds` lists the images of the environments on the Docker host with one `docker image ls --filter reference=devenv-*` (`listEnvironmentImages`: `<name>:<build>` and the images that Docker Compose built, `<project>-<service>`, whichever computer built them, so also an older build, a new one before its build record, and one that a Delete could not remove), and links each image to its environment (user decisions 2026-10-03): by its label `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id` (one `docker image inspect` of the listed images, `imageLabelsOf`), else by its names (`environmentImageNames`: each start of the repository up to a `-` that has the shape of an environment name, `environmentImageClass`); an image whose names include a known environment of another account counts as that account's whatever its label says. The owner of the environment comes from the registry entries on the host (and the checked environment), else from the labels of the volumes of that environment (one `docker volume ls`, only when an image or a reference belongs to an environment that the registry does not know, `hasUnknownEnvironment`: an environment that another computer created on a shared host), never from a label of the image; an image of no known owner counts as another account's. `environmentImageIds` splits the image IDs by owner, and `otherAccountImageItems` refuses (protected, `… (an image of an environment of another GitHub account)`) a reference whose image, as Docker gives it by the name, has an ID of another account and none of the account's own: the image ID says what an image holds, not whose it is, and two accounts with the same configuration can build the same image. Since 2026-10-03 the extension labels its environment images (above): their IDs differ per environment. A failure to list the images or the volumes is never a refusal as another account's (the own IDs could be incomplete): it is a check that failed (AnalysisFailedError, docker), unless the check has a definitive item of another kind (an image ID, a reference with a definitive answer of Docker about it, `invalid`: not valid, or an ID prefix of several images; review rounds 9 and 10, Y1: a transient answer is no item), for which the configuration is refused and the failure only logged (P13-1; review round 8, X1); a cancellation stays a cancellation. Review of the change (F1–F12): the first version used the registry of this computer only, which missed the environments of other computers on a shared host and the images left behind by a Delete, and listed the tags of each environment one by one. Review round 2 (R1–R12): the volumes are listed only when an image that a reference found (since round 3 also a missing environment image name) has an unknown environment; volumes with different owner labels leave the owner unknown. Review round 3 (S1, S2): a reference that finds no local image but has the name of an environment image of another account or of no known owner (`isOtherEnvironmentImageName`, `EnvironmentImageIds.ownNames` and `otherNames`) is refused by its name, as the removed name rule did: no registry has such a name, and the image could appear between the check and the start (the merged-configuration re-check does not ask a reference again, and a failed pull falls back to a local image); the cancellation tests assert that no Docker failure is logged. Review round 4 (T1, T2): only a reference that Docker definitively found missing is decided by its name (`otherAccountImageItems` gets the set `missing`); a reference that Docker could not inspect (transient or invalid) never is, so a failure of Docker fails the check (or stays an item of its own), and such a reference never makes the check list the images of the environments (a reference that Docker answered for still can, in the same check; review round 7, W1). Review round 5 (U1): `missing` is a required parameter. Accepted limits: a copy with other labels or layers (another ID) and without the name of an environment, and the Dockerfile (not checked since 2026-09-27).
- **Review round 10.** D10-1, D10-2, D10-3: see section 7, "Ownership". P10-1: see "Images of other environments". P10-3: a failed check of the new image of an update for a size limit (`AnalysisFailure` `size`: the job size of the worker, the caps of `composeModelLimit`, `MAX_IMAGE_ID_REFERENCES`) is remembered as a refused update (`RefusedUpdate.reason` `size`, the same key as the other refusals, `Messages.updateTooLarge`), so that the same update is not built at every open; a time or memory limit of the worker (`limit`) and an internal failure stay not remembered (P9-1). S10-1: `rewriteModel` builds the map of the top-level volumes once per model, not once per service; `composeModelLimit` refuses more than `MAX_COMPOSE_TOP_LEVEL_ENTRIES` (5000) entries of the top-level `volumes`, `networks`, `configs`, or `secrets`. S10-2: `composeInputsHash` and `composeConfigHash` feed the hash piece by piece and turn each distinct Dockerfile text into JSON once (the digests are byte for byte those of before); `composeModelLimit` with the Dockerfiles of the output refuses more than `MAX_ANALYSIS_JOB_CHARACTERS` characters of Dockerfiles over all services (a shared text once per service) before any hash, also in the check for a changed configuration. S10-5: `NodeProcessRunner` keeps only the last `MAX_CAPTURED_STDERR_CHARACTERS` (1 M) characters of standard error (all of it still streams to `onStderr`); the checks for a missing object read the end, where Docker prints its errors.
- **Review round 9.** D9-1: the ownership fixes leave out the paths of the repository that the other services mount (section 7, "Ownership"; since review round 10 `Environment.serviceFolders`). D9-2: the confirmation of **Delete** names them ("Service data in the repository will be deleted: ./data/postgres.", `Messages.deleteRepositoryServiceData`, `repositoryServiceDataFolders`): they are in the workspace volume and go with it. D9-3: `stopServiceBeforeRemoval` stops every running container, also a dev container, before `docker rm -f` (Delete, also a dev container without the ID label; the single container that `runComposeUp` replaces). S9-1: the lookups of the extension host use Sets (`createFolders` and `serviceFolders` of `rewriteModel`, `missing` of `ComposeAccessInput` (`src/core/policy/compose.ts`), `findLocalEnvNames`, `COMPOSE_MODEL_SCRIPT`); the extension host refuses as too large (`ANALYSIS_FAILED_ITEM`) a devcontainer.json over `MAX_CONFIG_TEXT_LENGTH` (1 M characters, `READ_FILES_SCRIPT` reads at most one more), and a model with more than `MAX_COMPOSE_SERVICES` (500) services or `MAX_COMPOSE_MOUNTS` (5000) mounts (`composeModelLimit`), before it works on them (`src/core/helper/analysisLimits.ts`); a message names at most 20 names and "and N more" (`listSome`). S9-2: `READ_FILES_SCRIPT` and `COMPOSE_MODEL_SCRIPT` read at most one character more than `MAX_DOCKERFILE_LENGTH` of a Dockerfile (`readLimited`: at most 4 · (limit + 1) bytes, so the length check still refuses), and the model run prints each Dockerfile once (`dockerfileFiles` and `dockerfileTexts`, which `parseComposeModelOutput` turns into `dockerfiles`); `NodeProcessRunner` keeps at most 64 MB of standard output (`MAX_CAPTURED_OUTPUT_BYTES`), stops the program beyond, and fails (`OutputTooLargeError`), never with a cut output; the job size is checked before `postMessage` (section 7). S9-3: see "Images of other environments". P9-1, P9-2: see section 7, "Host access analysis in a worker thread".
- **Limits**: a running dev container of the environment can swap a link of the repository between the model run (which reads the real paths) and the build (minutes later, in a helper run that mounts the cache volume), so the builder follows the new link; only a process of the environment itself can do so, but the link can lead into the shared cache volume, not only to the files of the environment. The labels of images that Compose builds during `up`, and of an image of another service that Compose pulls during `up`, are not checked before `up`; the model sets `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id`, `nimblescape.devenv.host-access`, and (dev service) `nimblescape.devenv.container-version` or (other services) `nimblescape.devenv.compose-service` on every container, which win over the labels of the image for these keys (review round 2, D2-2: before, `nimblescape.devenv.host-access` was set only with the checks off, so an image label could decide it). Other `nimblescape.devenv.…` labels of such an image reach the container (they are not used for the containers of other services). A change only in a file that `include` or `extends` names changes the model hash, not `inputsHash`: it counts as a change only when the Compose version is the same. L-3 a stopped side service of a running dev container is started by the extension, not by the CLI; L-4 `--remove-existing-container` replaces only the dev container, Compose recreates the other services only when their model or image changed; L-6 a new key of a newer Compose version is refused as not supported until the policy knows it; L-7 remote `include`/`extends` (Git, OCI) do not work (Docker Compose fetches no remote resource in the helper: `COMPOSE_EXPERIMENTAL_GIT_REMOTE` and `COMPOSE_EXPERIMENTAL_OCI_REMOTE` are `false`; the model run of the former per-step helper had no network); L-10 old images of rebuilt side services become dangling; L-11 a project volume whose key looks like the name of a workspace volume is refused by its name; review round 3 (D3-1): the containers of the other services are recognized before a single-container `up` by `nimblescape.devenv.compose-service` also without a dev container of Compose or a build record (before, `up` could find one of them by `--id-label`).

## 16. Remote Docker host

Unit 7 (user decisions 2026-09-27; concept 7.16). The remote host is the current Docker context; nothing of the extension points Docker anywhere else.

- **Modules.** `src/core/docker/dockerHost.ts` (pure): `classifyDockerEndpoint` (local: empty, `unix://`, `npipe://`, `tcp://` to the loopback; remote: `ssh://`, host = the part after `ssh://`; everything else `unsupported`), `parseContextInspect`, `parseSshAddress` and `isUsableSshAlias` (the checks of an address and an alias: no spaces, no leading `-`, no path, query, or shell character, IPv6 in brackets), `sshCommandArgs` (`-o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=… -T [-p] [-l] -- <host> <command>`), `sshCommandLine` (the command line of the host key advice: `ssh [-p <port>] [<user>@]<host>`, IPv6 without brackets, `ssh <alias>` for an alias), `remoteContextNames`/`ownContextDescription`/`isOwnContextDescription` (the names and the description of the context per host), `dockerHostProblem` (the plain reason of an error of `docker info` or `ssh`), `environmentsOfHost`/`isOnDockerHost` (the registry filter), `rootlessSocketPath`, `isRootlessEngine`. `src/core/sshConfig.ts` (pure, with a small file interface): the concrete `Host` entries of `~/.ssh/config` (`%USERPROFILE%\.ssh\config` on Windows), `Include` followed (relative to `~/.ssh`, `~`, globs with `*` and `?`, at most 16 levels, at most 256 files), patterns and `Match` blocks skipped, the first `HostName`, `User`, `Port` of each alias. `src/core/docker/dockerTargets.ts`: `DockerTargets` reads the current target (`docker context inspect --format '{{json .}}'`, or `DOCKER_HOST` of VS Code) and runs operations with it (`withOperation`, an `AsyncLocalStorage`). `src/core/docker/remoteDocker.ts`: the SSH check (`checkSshLogin`, `SshLoginCache`), the test of a host (the SSH check, then `docker -H ssh://<host> info --format …`), `ensureDockerHostReachable` (the step "Docker start" of a remote host: the SSH check, then `docker info`), `startDockerFor` (local → `ensureDockerRunning` as before; remote → the check; unsupported → refused), the context commands (`useRemoteContext`, `chooseLocalContext`), the rootless socket. `src/core/storage/remoteDockerState.ts`: `remote-docker.json` in the global storage folder (the previous context; the rootless socket per host). `src/vscode/remoteDockerCommands.ts`: the two commands and the switch back of a restored window.
- **A context per host (review C1; names: user decisions 2026-10-03).** "Use a Remote Docker Host…" uses the context of the host (`ensureRemoteContext`, `remoteContextChoice`): named after the alias of the SSH config, or the host name of an address without user and port (`remoteContextNames`, only `[a-zA-Z0-9_.+-]`, at least 2 characters: `remote-<name>` for a name of one character, review round 3 of PR #88, A-R3-2; `remote` for an empty name or `default`), for example `htldvm`; when a context of that name exists and points elsewhere, `<name>-<adjective>-<scientist>` with the pair of the host (`namePair(host)`). An existing context with the endpoint `ssh://<host>` is taken as it is (also one that the user made); a missing one is created with `host=ssh://<host>` and the description `Dev Environments: remote Docker host <host>` (`ownContextDescription`), never updated afterwards, then `docker context use`. When both names point elsewhere, the switch is refused ("remove or rename one of them"). One context for all hosts, updated at each switch, moved the running operations of other windows (which pin the context by name, see below) to the new host mid-way. The contexts of earlier hosts stay (a pinned call on a removed one fails cleanly); a context is the extension's own by its description (`isOwnContextDescription`, `isOwnContext`), which replaces every comparison with one name (the remembered context, the switch back). The window arguments of an environment on another host take the context of the host the same way (`Controller.windowArgs`, created when missing; review round 1 of PR #88, A-R1-5: a create that fails because another window created the context a moment ago takes that context); the heartbeats of a window without a context of its own only take an existing one (`HeartbeatEngineSources.remoteContext`, `findRemoteContext`), so a tick never creates a context, also not one that the user removed.
- **The switch back (review C2).** "Use the Local Docker" uses the remembered context only when `docker context inspect <name>` classifies as local (`classifyDockerEndpoint`), else `default`. The message follows where Docker points afterwards: "Docker now uses the local Docker (context …)" only when it does; otherwise a warning names the context and its endpoint.
- **Per operation, not for ever.** Each command, the flows of the window roles, and each `operation` of the controller run in `DockerTargets.withOperation`: the context is read once at the start, and every Docker call of the operation (`BootstrapDocker.run` and `start` without an explicit `env`, among them the start of the worker; before plan step 11I2, `ContainerAdapter.run`) gets `DOCKER_CONTEXT=<that context>`, so a switch in the middle does not move the rest of the operation to another engine; the flows of the operation go to the worker of that engine (`HelperChannels`, one per context and endpoint, section 17). Outside of an operation, each call reads the current context afresh. `DOCKER_HOST` is never set by the extension (the former exception, the credentialed pull of section 9 with its own `docker --config` call, is gone: the pulls run in the worker over the Engine API); when `DOCKER_HOST` is set for VS Code it decides the endpoint, and the calls get no `DOCKER_CONTEXT`.
- **No questions.** The Docker CLI starts `ssh` for an `ssh://` endpoint in a new session (`Setsid`, docker/cli `connhelper/commandconn/session_unix.go`) with `-o ConnectTimeout=30 -T` (`connhelper.go`), and has no documented variable for more ssh options; so the test uses the Docker CLI path with `SSH_ASKPASS_REQUIRE=never` (ssh(1), OpenSSH 8.4 and later) and a time limit of 45 s: without a terminal and without an askpass program, ssh cannot ask for a password, a passphrase, or a host key, and fails at once (checked here with OpenSSH 9.2 and Docker CLI 29.3: unknown host key, wrong key, closed port, Docker missing each fail within a second). Our own `ssh` calls add `BatchMode=yes`. The Docker calls of the extension (BootstrapDocker: the bootstrap, among them the start of the worker) get no extra variable; their own time limits apply.
- **The SSH check (review C3).** On Windows the Docker CLI starts `ssh.exe` without `Setsid`, and the OpenSSH client of Windows asks on the hidden console (host key, password) until the time limit ("does not answer"). So, on every platform, before the test of a host and before each operation on a remote host (`ensureDockerHostReachable`, the step "Docker start"), our own `ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=15 -T [-p] [-l] -- <host> true` runs (`checkSshLogin`, time limit 30 s): it fails at once instead of asking, and its error gives the reason (`dockerHostProblem`: host key, login, unreachable, closed before the login); then no Docker call is made. It is one connection instead of the several of the Docker CLI, which also lowers the risk of a lock-out by `PerSourcePenalties`. A success counts for 60 s per host (`SshLoginCache`, one per extension host), a failure never; the test before a switch always connects. A host that is no usable alias or address (a context of the user with a path) is left to the Docker CLI. `NodeProcessRunner` ends the whole process tree on Windows at a time limit, an abort, or too much output (`taskkill /T /F /PID <pid>` from `%SystemRoot%\System32`, arguments without a shell; `windowsTreeKillCommand`), so an `ssh.exe` that `docker.exe` started does not survive; elsewhere `child.kill()` as before.
- **Unsupported endpoints (review D2).** One check in `EnvironmentService` (`dockerTarget`, from `DockerTargets.current` of the operation): every operation (open, stop, delete, …) refuses an endpoint that is neither local nor SSH with `dockerEndpointUnsupported` before Docker is asked; the reads (`reconcileFromVolumes`, `inspectStates`, the reads of an attached window, `windowStateInWorker` since plan step 11C1 in place of `currentBranch`, the volumes and service data of Delete) do nothing on it (0, an empty map, `undefined`, `[]`), and the refusal is shown once per endpoint (`ui.warn`). So no environment ever records such a `dockerHost`. The controller and the window roles leave the check of a running Docker to `reconcileFromVolumes` (no `docker info` before the check of the target).
- **Registry.** `Environment.dockerHost` (missing = local, `REGISTRY_VERSION` stays 1). `openFirst` and `reconcileFromVolumes` record the host of the operation; `findForAccount(repository, account, host)` and the check of `add` use the key (repository, account, host); `inspectStates` and the sidebar (`Sidebar.availableEnvironments`, the render) filter by the current host; the service refuses every operation on an environment of another host with `otherDockerHost` before Docker is asked; the controller skips pending operations and the reopen record of another host, and removes no token there. The computations over all entries that only protect data (foreign volumes of the host access policy, volumes kept at Delete, base images still in use) keep looking at every host: that is the conservative side (a volume or image name of another host is kept or refused, never removed or allowed).
- **Mismatch of a restored window.** `Controller.openAttachedWindow` compares the host of the environment with the current context before the open pipeline (`onWindowHost`). VS Code activates the extension in the restored window before it resolves the `attached-container` authority (V-2), so a switch there reaches the Dev Containers extension; without the switch the window closes its remote connection.
- **Workspace helper.** `HelperImagesDeps.engine` (the helper image of the extension, `HelperImages`; plan step 11I, U7: no longer of WorkspaceHelper, which runs in the worker with the socket of the worker's own container, `HelperDeps.socket`) gives only the key of the engine ('' for the local Docker, else the host; review round 1 of PR #129); the source of the socket mount of the worker is `engineSocket` of `extension.ts` (remote: the recorded rootless socket or `/var/run/docker.sock`; local: the socket of the endpoint), and the Session Monitor gets the same one from the worker. The cached helper image is not reused for another engine, and a remote engine has its own state file (`helper-remote-<hash>.json`).
- **Messages.** `dockerHostUnreachable` (Show details, Try again; never Start Docker or Install Docker…), `dockerEndpointUnsupported`, `otherDockerHost` (warnings).
- **Session Monitor.** Plan step 8, PR C: the Session Monitor process of the computer, which stopped the containers of the current host after the waiting time through its context, is removed (section 12). The container of the next item does every stop, on every engine; a window that closes or leaves its environment sends it the short release.
- **Session Monitor on the remote host** (unit 7, PR 2; user decisions 2026-09-27). Remote containers stop when closed by default, like local ones, unless kept.
  - *Container.* One per engine: `devenv-session-monitor` (`REMOTE_MONITOR_CONTAINER` in `src/core/remoteMonitor/protocol.ts`), image = the image ID of the helper image that the open pinned on that engine (review round 1 of PR #64, S1; review round 2, B4; review round 3 of PR #64, P2; the tag only when its ID could not be read; review round 4 of PR #64, R4-6), since plan step 11D3 (option B of 2026-10-03) by its second tag `devenv-monitor:<the hash of the helper tag>` (`monitorImageTag`, `src/core/helper/helperState.ts`), which the worker sets on its own image before the create (`monitorImage`, `src/helperChannel/flowOperations.ts`; by the ID as before when the tag fails), so the container shows as `devenv-monitor:<hash>` in the Containers view; the create checks the image ID of the new container against the pinned one before its start (`MonitorRunSpec.imageId`; another image, a tag moved by another window, fails the create, and the container is removed by its nonce; that open is refused, and the next ensure tags again: review round 1 of PR #101, A-L1, accepted). The name does not stick in two cases (review round 1 of PR #101, A-L2, cosmetic only): a monitor created before 11D3 has the same label and is kept, showing its image ID until its label changes; and after a rebuild of the same helper tag the new image takes the monitor tag while the kept monitor runs from the previous image, which then shows by its ID, while its label and the log lines keep the helper tag (Node.js 24 and the Docker CLI; of them the monitor runs Node.js only since plan step 11I, U1). `RemoteSessionMonitor.ensure` (`src/core/remoteMonitor/remoteSessionMonitor.ts`) decides by the inspect of the container (in the worker over the Engine API, `MonitorEngine` of `src/core/worker/engineMonitor.ts`, plan step 11D2) and its label `nimblescape.devenv.session-monitor` = 12 hex digits of sha256(script, helper tag, the pipe loader, and `image-maintenance` when the image maintenance is on: see *Images on the remote host*); what it keeps, starts, or replaces, and how a replace and a name conflict go, is the *Replace table of the remote Session Monitor* in section 18 (review round 3 of PR #69, A-R3-3: that table is the one description). Create (plan step 11D2: the worker's operation `monitorEnsure` makes it over the Engine API, `DockerEngine.createAttached`: create with an open input, attach, start, write the line, wait for the ready line; with the same settings as this former CLI form): `docker run -i --sig-proxy=false --pull never --name devenv-session-monitor --label nimblescape.devenv.session-monitor=<hash> --label nimblescape.devenv.monitor-create=<a new UUID> --restart on-failure --network none (only without image maintenance) --cap-drop ALL --security-opt no-new-privileges --log-driver json-file --log-opt max-size=1m --log-opt max-file=2 -v <socket>:/var/run/docker.sock -v devenv-session-monitor:/state [-e DEVENV_IMAGE_…] <helper image ID of the open (the helper tag only when the ID is unknown)> node -e <pipe loader> /opt/devenv/monitor.js <sha256 of the script> startMonitor`, attached (before plan step 11D2, by the Docker CLI of the extension; `--sig-proxy=false` kept the container running): the worker writes the script as the first input line, waits for the monitor's line `Session Monitor started` (`REMOTE_MONITOR_READY_TEXT`, which `run` prints) for at most 60 s (`REMOTE_MONITOR_DOCKER_TIMEOUT_MS`) or until a cancellation, then closes the input, and the container goes on alone. A name conflict (another window at the same time; the engine's refusal of the name, 409 with its message, and no container of this create's nonce: review round 4 of PR #69, A-R4-2; plan step 11D2) is handled as section 18 describes (never removed: not ours); any other failure (no ready line in time, the container ended before it, for example with the loader's exit 3 and its `devenv loader: …` line, which goes to the log; a cancellation) removes the container of this create (best effort; plan step 11D2: the wait for the end of the CLI client, review round 4 of PR #69, A-R4-1, is gone; review round 1 of PR #69, A-R1-2: by the nonce label `nimblescape.devenv.monitor-create` of the create, the containers with it and then each by its ID, never by the name, which may be the container of another window by then, for example when the create failed with "No such container" because that window replaced it; the nonce is not part of the label of the version), and is logged as a warning with the short lines of stderr only (a cancellation passes). Neither the script line nor any input is logged. Never a pull (review round 4 of PR #64, R4-8: `--pull never` like the helper runs; plan step 11D2: the create of the Engine API never pulls). The socket is the one that the worker mounts for that engine (on a remote engine the recorded rootless socket, else `/var/run/docker.sock`; on the local Docker the socket of its endpoint, section 6). Monitor cleanup, user decision 2026-09-29 (R5): the log of the container is capped at two files of 1 MB (`REMOTE_MONITOR_LOG`); the driver is named, as `max-size` alone fails on a host whose default log driver is `journald` or `syslog`. The log options are not part of the label, so a running monitor keeps its log settings until it is replaced for another reason (a new script, a new helper tag, a new loader, the image maintenance turned on or off); the release that brought the cap changed the script, so its first open replaced the monitor, and so did the release of the pipe loader. The restart policy is ours (the refusal of restart policies is for repository containers). Plan step 8, PR B (user decision Q5 of 2026-10-02): `on-failure` (`MONITOR_RESTART_POLICY`; before `unless-stopped`), so the monitor that exits with 0 when it is idle (*Exit when idle* below) stays exited, while a failure (an uncaught error, the loader's exit 3) is still restarted; a monitor that `docker stop`, `docker kill` or the shutdown of the daemon ended is not started with the daemon (no environment container is either: their restart policies are refused); every monitor of an older version is replaced at its first open, as its script and so its label changed with this policy. No published port, no network (outbound network only with image maintenance). The loader stores the script at `/opt/devenv/monitor.js` in the container's own file system and calls its `startMonitor` (`REMOTE_MONITOR_ENTRY`, which runs `run`); after a restart of the container (`docker start`, `docker restart`, the restart policy after a failure) it finds that file with the hash of its command and starts it again without reading its input. The subcommands that the worker runs in it (entries of the script registry, each an exec over the Engine API, section 6) run the same file (`node /opt/devenv/monitor.js …`). The script is the module `devenv:monitor-script` of the worker bundle (plan step 11D2; before, `dist/remoteMonitor.js`; esbuild entry `src/remoteMonitor/main.ts`, always minified; Node.js built-ins and pure modules of `src/core` only). It is never on the command line, so its length has no limit there (plan step 3, user decision 2026-09-29: `MAX_SCRIPT_LENGTH`, the check of the Windows command line and `src/remoteMonitor/bundle.test.ts` are gone; a test builds the script and checks that the command line holds no part of it); `ensure` refuses only a script whose JSON line is longer than the memory guard of the loader (8 MiB, `MAX_BUNDLE_LINE_LENGTH`), before an old monitor is removed. The labels of the helper channel are defined in `src/core/names.ts` (the channel's protocol re-exports them), so the script does not bundle the protocol of the channel. `EnvironmentService.prepareHelper` calls `EnvironmentServiceDeps.sessionMonitor.ensure` (plan step 11E4e: in the worker, `workerSessionMonitor` of `src/core/worker/workerServices.ts`, whose ensure is `ensureWorkerMonitor` of `src/helperChannel/flowOperations.ts`: `RemoteSessionMonitor.ensureOrThrow` over `engineMonitor` with the worker's own image and the socket of its engine, as the operation `monitorEnsure` does it, which a window sends for the repair of a heartbeat; before plan step 11D2, `src/vscode/sessionMonitorEnsure.ts` in the extension) right after `ensureImageUse` (`ensureSessionMonitor`; plan step 8, PR A: for every engine, local and remote, before was only an SSH host), once per run, so before the container is created or started, and then its `heartbeat`: the first heartbeat of this computer for the environment (in the worker with the time limit of the settings of the operation) (the keep-running flag by the rule of `keptWhenClosed`, the limit of the setting), so that the remote monitor, which acts only on environments with a record, covers every remote environment from its start. A failed first heartbeat is a warning; the window's heartbeats send it again. Plan step 8, PR A (user decision Q3 of 2026-10-02, D1): a monitor that cannot be ensured refuses the open: `UserFacingError('sessionMonitorFailed', Messages.sessionMonitorUnavailable(repository, cause))` (Show details, Try again); the helper image of the run is then not taken for resolved (`ctx.helperImage` is set after the monitor), and `isSessionMonitorFailed` is rethrown before the rules that open a running container as it is. Review round 1 of PR #86 (A-R1-1): `ensureSessionMonitorAfterStart` ensures it once more, once per run and only when `ensureSessionMonitor` ran in it, right after `helper.up` returned in `runUp` and `runComposeUp` (the container was created or started; before run-user-commands, so lifecycle commands that fail and leave the container running come after it), and in their `catch` after a failed `up` that was no cancellation (it may have left a container running; the error of `up` stays). The monitor may have exited when idle (*Exit when idle*) between the first ensure and the start, and the window's heartbeat repair needs the container (review round 2 of PR #85, A-R2-1), so a sleep, quit or crash before it connected would leave the container running without a monitor. A running container that opens as it is gets no second ensure (it ran all along, so the monitor stayed). A failure there does not refuse the open, unlike Q3: the container runs already and a refusal would not stop it, while a connected window repairs the monitor with its heartbeats (Q4); it is logged as a warning and shown (`ui.warn`, `Messages.sessionMonitorAfterStartFailed`); a cancellation cancels the open. `RemoteSessionMonitor.ensure` (logs a warning, `failed`) stays for the Docker tests. The helper image cleanup never uses force (`removeImage`), so Docker refuses to remove the image of the monitor while its container exists; the cleanup has no caller in this version; the [decision of 2026-10-09](plan-remote-worker.md#2-decisions) restores it in the extension's preparation of the worker for an open and in the background prebuild (a follow-up PR). A newer helper tag replaces the monitor at the next open (another label).
  - *Records.* `/state/heartbeats/<source>.<environment id>.json` = `{ at, keepRunning, limitSeconds, seq }` (plus `release: true` for the record of a release; review round 1 of PR #87, A-R1-2), `at` the clock of the remote host at the write (no clock skew between computers), mode 0600, written atomically (a temporary file, then a rename). Review round 2 of PR #39 (L1): `seq` is the local wall clock (ms) at which the sending computer read the keep flag: the Session Monitor uses the time of its tick (taken before it reads the registry), the window of Close and Keep Running the time right after it set the flag, the open pipeline the time before it reads the entry again for its first heartbeat. Under the kernel lock `flock -w 5 /state/.heartbeats.lock` (util-linux, in the helper image) around the command of the heartbeat (`heartbeatCommand`, since plan step 11I (U2) the entry `monitorHeartbeat` of the registry of the container scripts with the same command line, which the worker runs as an exec over the Engine API; PR #58, found in review round 10 of PR #57, and review round 2 of PR #58: every design of a lock file with a stale time, a token, and a guard left a race, because plain files cannot tell that a holder is gone; the kernel releases the lock when its process ends, also when it is killed, so no lock is left over and none is taken over), two heartbeats read and replace the records one after the other; a heartbeat that does not get the lock within 5 s fails (exit code 75, `-E`; review round 3, F7: the log names the busy lock, and exit code 137 a kill, by the time limit of 10 s or from outside, `monitorExecFailure`; review round 4, H2: these texts only for the commands under the lock, `isUnderRecordsLock`) and writes nothing, and `timeout -s KILL 10` (coreutils) ends a heartbeat that holds it longer, so at most 15 s, within the 20 s of the exec. `forget` runs under the same lock (review round 3, F6), so a heartbeat that read a record cannot write it back after its removal; the Session Monitor's own removal of old records (see *Rules*) takes the same lock too (review round 1 of PR #63, F2): the loop runs `forget <source> <env id> <at>` (`forgetIfUnchangedCommand`, a child process under `flock` as the heartbeats), which removes the record only while the file still holds the `at` that the loop read, and prints `removed` then. Review round 2 of PR #63 (R2-1): the removals come after the stops of the tick again (round 1 had put them before, where each, up to 20 s, delayed the first stop); the `at` check makes a late removal safe. Before round 1, the loop's removal of old records removed the file by name without the lock after the stops, so a heartbeat written meanwhile (a computer back after more than 7 days) was deleted and its environment stopped at the next tick. A removal that fails is tried at the next pass and logged once per record (`<source>.<env id>.<at>`, R2-3; also across a tick that ends early, R3-8); the log names a timeout and a failed start of the child process (R2-2). Review round 4 of PR #63 (N4-5, replacing the per-tick budget of round 3, R3-1, which could starve removals): the removals run in the background, one pass at a time, and never lengthen a tick (the next tick measures its gap from the time of this one, and slow removals, up to 20 s each, would otherwise hold every stop by the gap rule); a tick while a pass runs starts none; a tick that reaches the end of its stops while no pass runs starts the next one, with the records it read at its start (review round 5, R5-7); a duplicate removal finds a missing file or another `at` and does nothing, and the "Removed…" log line comes when the removal ends. The `at` check under the lock keeps a removal safe while later ticks run, and a removed record never matters to a stop: a superseded one is never the newest, and (review round 5, R5-6) the forgotten records of an environment are removed oldest first by their `at` (of equal `at`, a keepRunning record last; review round 6, R6-1: in the order of `decide`, by the times as the rules see them, so a time in the future counts from when it was first seen, and none whose `at`, as the rules see it (clamped), is later than that of a record of its environment that stays, nor a keepRunning one with the same `at`; review round 7, R7-4), and once one of them is not removed (failed, changed or already gone meanwhile) the rest of that environment stay for the next pass, with their logged failures (R6-3). So every partial state keeps the newest records of the environment, and no stop or keep changes until all are gone, also when containers of it are created while a pass runs (possibly many ticks after the tick that decided it) or a tick runs between two removals. At the end of the process nothing waits for a pass. Review round 3, R3-2: the time limit kills only `flock`, whose child may still remove the record, so the log says "no answer within 20 s (it may still end)"; another signal is logged as "killed by <signal>". A file with a record name but no valid record (`readRecords` skips it) is ignored and never removed by the loop: such files appear only after a change of the format (a record of a newer monitor, which a monitor of another version on the same engine must not delete while its environment runs) or a corruption, and the plain `forget` of Delete removes the file of its computer and environment by name (review round 4 of PR #63, N4-1: R3-9 of round 3, which removed such files after 7 days, is reverted). Each heartbeat first removes the temporary files `.<record name>.<pid>.tmp` of heartbeats killed between the write and the rename (review round 3, F5). `heartbeat` ignores an entry whose `seq` is lower than the `seq` of the existing record of the same source while that record's `at` is within 60 s of the host's current time (`SEQ_ORDER_WINDOW_MS`; review round 3, N2: the in-flight window, a `docker exec` ends after 20 s), so a heartbeat of a tick that read the registry before Close and Keep Running set the flag, and reached the host after the window's heartbeat, cannot turn the record back to keep=false. An older record is replaced whatever its `seq`: `seq` is the wall clock of the computer, and after that clock stepped back (for example by an hour) every later entry would otherwise be dropped and `at` never refreshed, so an environment in use would be stopped, or a new keep lost, for as long as the skew lasts; now at most one heartbeat in the first 60 s after the step is dropped. Review round 3 (N1): an entry may be `clearOnly` (only with keepRunning false): it is written only when the existing record of the same source says keepRunning, otherwise nothing is written for it (no record created, no `at` refreshed). A record without a valid `seq` is invalid (greenfield). Subcommands of `monitor.js`: `run` (the loop); `heartbeat <json>` (`parseHeartbeatInput`, strict: exactly `source` (32 hex digits), `limitSeconds` (an integer, clamped to 60..86400), `environments` (at most 200 of exactly `id` (36 characters of hex digits and `-`, as `newEnvironmentId`), `keepRunning` (a boolean), and `seq` (a safe non-negative integer)), and optionally `release` (only `true`, review round 1 of PR #87, A-R1-2; written into the records); exit 0, or 2 and nothing written); `records <id>` (printed `{ now, records: [{ source, at, keepRunning }] }`; removed in plan step 11I, U10, as only tests used it); `forget <source> <id> [<at>]` (review round 1 of PR #63, F2: with `<at>`, 1 to 16 digits and a safe integer, it removes the record only while the file holds a valid record with that `at` and then prints `removed` (a missing file or one without a valid record is kept, and nothing is printed; review round 4, N4-1/N4-2); without it, it removes the file by name, valid or not, and prints nothing; a third argument that is not such a number, or a fourth argument, gives exit 2 and removes nothing; review round 2, R2-7); `images -` and `settings -` (see *Images on the remote host*). Every other argument gives exit 2. Files whose names are not of that form, that are no regular file, larger than 4 KB, or not valid records are ignored.
  - *Rules* (`src/remoteMonitor/rules.ts`, pure). A tick every 15 s: the containers with the label `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id`, stopped ones included, from the engine over the Engine API (plan step 11I, U1: `DockerEngine.containerSummaries` of the worker's client over the socket of the container, the list as it is without an inspect of each container (review round 1 of PR #126, F1: an inspect waits for the lock of a container that a start holds and fails for one whose layer is broken, so one such container would hold the stops of the whole engine), read as their ID, `State`, first name and labels by `remoteContainersOf`; the answer of the list may hold 16 MiB (F2); before, `docker ps -a --no-trunc --filter label=nimblescape.devenv.environment-id --format '{{.ID}}\t{{.State}}\t{{.Names}}\t{{.Label "nimblescape.devenv.environment-id"}}\t{{.Label "nimblescape.devenv.compose-service"}}'`) (time limit 30 s), the records, `decide`. Per environment with a running container (`running`, `restarting`, `paused`): no record at all → never acted on (coordinator decision of PR 2: a container in use must never be stopped by a monitor that has no records for it, for example an environment of the host's own local Docker use or of a build without heartbeats); otherwise the newest record decides (review round 2 of PR #39, M1): the record with the latest `at` of all sources (the time the host received it; for equal times one with `keepRunning`). It says `keepRunning` → it keeps running; otherwise it stops when more than its `limitSeconds` has passed since its `at`. So any later real choice of any computer (the heartbeats of use in a window, the first heartbeat of an open, Keep Running When Closed turned off, Close and Keep Running; not the clear-only entries of the full sync) overrules an older keep of another computer, also of one that no longer sends (a `computer.id` replaced after lost storage, a retired computer on a shared engine); the keep of a computer that still sends holds until someone makes a newer choice. Review round 1 of PR #87 (A-R1-2): when the newest record is a release (`release: true`, the short limit of a window that left the environment), it decides only while no record of another source that is no release is still within its own `limitSeconds`; otherwise the newest record that is no release decides.
