So a release never shortens the heartbeats of another computer that still uses the environment (before, it overruled them until they sent again: up to 30 s plus the time of the `docker exec`, or longer after a failed heartbeat), while an old record of a computer that no longer sends (beyond its limit, also an old keep) does not hold it back. A label change of the script replaces running monitors, so a monitor that does not know `release` refuses the heartbeat (the long limit stays) only until the next open. Gap rule: a time since the previous tick of more than 60 s (the host or the container was paused, the clock was changed), and the first tick, hold every stop for 120 s, so that the computers send heartbeats again first. Removal of records (monitor cleanup, user decision 2026-09-29): a record whose environment has no container at all and whose `at` is more than 7 days (`RECORD_MAX_AGE_MS`) from now, both ways (`Math.abs`; R2: before, a record in the future stayed until its time had passed by 7 days), is removed (`decision.forget`, log "… (no container of it exists)"), but none whose `at`, as the rules see it (clamped), is later than that of a record of its environment that stays, nor a keepRunning one with the same `at` (review round 6 of PR #63, R6-1; round 7, R7-3); and (R1) a record that does not say `keepRunning`, whose `at` as the rules see it (a time in the future counts from when it was first seen) is older than 7 days, and for which another record of the same environment has a strictly later `at`, is removed too (`decision.superseded`, log "… (a newer record of it exists)"). So `superseded` never removes the newest record of an environment, a record on a tie of `at` with it, or a `keepRunning` record; nor (review round 1 of PR #63, F1) a record while a `keepRunning` record of another source of the same environment has an `at` not later than its own (review round 2 of PR #63, R2-6: by the written `at` of both, as the records hold them (and `records` printed them until plan step 11I, U10) and that check compares them, not the time of the rules): it is the only record of its computer, and without it the check of that computer (*Shared engine*, `inUseByOtherComputer`) would count an older keep. A tie is included only as a precaution (review round 3 of PR #63, R3-4): that check counts a keep at the same `at` whether the record stays or not. `forget` has neither guard (review round 12 of PR #63, A-R12-1): once every record of an environment without a container is old, it removes all of them, the newest included, oldest first by the `at` as the rules see it (a `keepRunning` record last on a tie), so a record can go while a `keepRunning` record of another source with the same `at` still exists; a `keepRunning` record is removed only this way, that is only while no container of its environment exists. At the start of `run` the monitor deletes its own temporary files in `/state` (`writeStateFile`: `^(images|image-settings|replaced-images)\.json\.\d+\.\d+\.tmp$`) whose modification time is more than 1 hour from now (R4; only regular files, never a link, errors ignored; the log says "Removed <n> leftover temporary file(s) of the volume."). Only at the start of `run` (review round 9 of PR #63, A2): a leftover that is younger than 1 hour at a start stays until the next start. At the end of each image pass the store `replaced-images.json` drops the repositories with an empty list (R3, `pruneReplacedImages`); review round 1 of PR #63 (B1): the repositories that match no current prefix stay, as the prefixes are those of the computer that opened last and change between the computers of a shared engine (before, the replaced images of the others were forgotten and stayed on the disk). Review round 1 of PR #39 (R1): a record whose `at` is more than 5 minutes in the future counts as written when the monitor first saw it (`futureSeen` in the state), and ages from then; it is not dropped (without a record the monitor would not act on the environment). The volume `devenv-session-monitor` is guarded by name like `devenv-helper-cache` (`foreignVolumeName`, class `protected`), so no configuration (mounts, `-v`, Compose volumes) can mount it to forge or remove records. Stop order: the dev container (no `nimblescape.devenv.compose-service`) first, the stop of the engine (plan step 11I, U1: over the Engine API, as `docker stop` without a time; a container that is gone, 404, counts as stopped; time limit 60 s); a failed stop is tried at the next tick. Plan step 8, PR B (user decision D2 of 2026-09-30): each automatic stop runs under the environment lock, the same `/state/locks/<id>.lock` that the workers hold for their operations (section 17). `stopLocker` (`src/remoteMonitor/stopLock.ts`) opens it with the code of the worker, shared in `src/core/helperChannel/lockFile.ts` (`openLockFile`: a strict ID, `isStorageId`; the folder 0700, never a symbolic link; the file with O_NOFOLLOW, 0600, a plain file only; O_CLOEXEC; never deleted), and runs `flock -n -E 75 3` on it (`flockNoWaitArgs`; no wait, unlike the 10 s of D3; time limit 10 s for the start). Busy (exit 75: a Start, Stop, Delete or other operation of a window holds it) → the environment is skipped in this tick and checked again at the next one; the log says so once per busy streak. A lock file that cannot be opened (a symbolic link, a folder that is not one, an invalid ID) or a `flock` that fails → skipped and logged once per streak: never a stop without the lock. Held → the containers of the environment (the list of the engine by the label `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id=<id>`, plan step 11I, U1; `docker ps -a --filter label=…=<id>` before) and its records are read again and `decide` runs again for it (the state of the tick, without a new gap): a heartbeat, a keep, or a Start that came before the lock was taken means no stop (logged); otherwise the stop as above, then the release (the file is closed), also after an error. The lock lives with the open file of the monitor process, so a monitor that is killed or replaced mid-stop leaves no lock held. No Git summary (the next open records it). The log on stdout (`docker logs devenv-session-monitor`) names each event once. `DEVENV_MONITOR_TICK_MS` (100..60000, read only by `main.ts`, set only by the Docker test) shortens the tick; the gap and the grace are then 4 and 8 ticks.
  - *Exit when idle* (plan step 8, PR B; user decision Q5 of 2026-10-02). After a tick, when no container with the label `nimblescape.devenv.environment-id` was seen running (`running`, `restarting`, `paused`; a kept environment that runs counts, so the monitor stays for it) for 5 minutes (`REMOTE_IDLE_EXIT_MS`, on the monotonic clock `performance.now()`; measured from the end of the stops of the tick, and a list that fails counts as not idle) and the container maintains no images (no `DEVENV_IMAGE_PREFIXES`), `run` waits for a running pass of record removals and ends with 0 (log: "No environment container ran and no heartbeat was fresh for 300 s, and image updates are off; the Session Monitor exits. The next open starts it again."). Review round 1 of PR #86 (A-R1-1): a tick whose `decide` says `active` counts as activity too (after the records were read): a heartbeat record within its time limit (`now - at <= limitSeconds * 1000`, with the clamped `at` of a record from the future, as for the stop). Review round 2 of PR #86 (A-R2-1): only that; a labelled container in the state `created` does not count (a failed start after `up`, or a Compose service whose `depends_on` never becomes healthy, leaves it `created` for ever, and the open keeps it), nor does a keepRunning record without a running container (a kept container that runs counts as running; Keep Running When Closed stays in the records after a Stop). The window of an open sends heartbeats until its container started, and the open ensures the monitor again right after `up`. So an open whose clone and build take longer than the idle time keeps the monitor: the window sends heartbeats for the environment of its busy mark also before its container exists (`WindowHeartbeats.collect`: the existence check of A-R2-1 is only for the connected environment), every 30 s, with the limit of the setting. Records age out, so the monitor exits the idle time after the last record passed its limit; a running container whose record passed its limit (an open that failed after the start, no window) is stopped first, and the idle time counts from the end of that stop. Only between two ticks: never while it holds a lock or stops a container. The restart policy `on-failure` leaves it exited; the records stay in the volume. The next open ensures it (`docker start` of the exited container, *Replace table* in section 18), and the heartbeats of a window repair a missing or exited monitor (Q4). After its start the gap rule holds every stop for 2 minutes, as after any start. `DEVENV_MONITOR_IDLE_MS` (100..86400000, set only by the Docker test) changes the idle time.
  - *Heartbeats* (plan step 8, PR A; user decisions Q1 and Q4 of 2026-10-02). `computer.id` (section 4) is the source. Each window sends them (`WindowHeartbeats`, `src/core/session/windowHeartbeats.ts`), driven by the 15 s tick of its `SessionCoordinator` (not awaited; review round 1 of PR #85, A-R1-2: the engines of a tick are served at the same time, each with its own in-flight guard, so a tick skips only an engine whose previous call still runs): for the environment it is connected to (`SessionCoordinator.environmentId`) and the environments it holds a live busy mark for (its window ID and process, younger than `BUSY_MARK_MAX_AGE_MS`; so a long open or rebuild of this window is covered), on every engine. Each goes to the engine the window uses the environment on (`engineFor` = `resolveHeartbeatEngine`, asked once per environment and role and kept; review round 1 of PR #85, A-R1-3: for the connected environment the engine of this window's own Docker context, the one in its authority that its status file records as `dockerContext`, read with `DockerTargets.ofContext`, also for a local environment and whatever context is current globally; without one, the current target when the environment is on it, else the existing context of the host (`HeartbeatEngineSources.remoteContext`, `findRemoteContext`; none when there is none) for an SSH host and the context `default` for the local Docker; for an environment it is only busy with, the current target on its host or the context of the host (`remoteContext`). An engine that cannot be found is logged once; for the connected environment each tick then counts as a failed heartbeat towards the warning below. Review round 2 of PR #85, A-R2-1: the engine found for the connected environment is trusted only when the environment's container is on it (`containerExists`: the operation `windowState` of the worker of that engine for its `containerName`, plan step 11D1, within the deadline below); when it is not, or the check fails, that tick counts the same way, nothing is sent or repaired for it there, and the engine is resolved again at the next tick. A verified engine is kept and checked again after a failed heartbeat. A repair happens only on an engine where an environment of the heartbeat has its container: the connected one checked that tick, or a busy one checked at the repair), through this window's worker of that engine: its operation `heartbeat` (plan step 11D1), which runs the entry `monitorHeartbeat` of the registry in `devenv-session-monitor` over the Engine API (plan step 11I, U2: `node /opt/devenv/monitor.js heartbeat <json>` under the lock of the records, `flock -w 5 -E 75 /state/.heartbeats.lock timeout -s KILL 10`; no input, no variable; the JSON holds ids and flags only; time limit 20 s); the worker is made ready by the call (D1: the helper image, then the open). One heartbeat per engine carries every environment of the window there with its flag (`keptWhenClosed`), the limit `stopAfterSeconds(stopAfterMinutes)` (5..1440 minutes, default 10, the long limit of Q1) and `seq` = the time before the registry was read. It is sent when an environment or its flag is new to the series of the engine or the limit changed, when the last one failed, or 30 s (`WINDOW_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_MS`) after the last successful one (the series and flag logic of the former `MonitorLoop.sendHeartbeat`, without its clear-only full sync). A result that says the monitor container is missing starts it again (`repair`: the operation `monitorEnsure` of the worker of that engine, plan step 11D2, made ready like every operation) and sends once more; after a failed repair the next one on that engine waits `REPAIR_BACKOFF_MS` (1, 2, then 5 minutes; the plain heartbeat is still sent at each due tick; a success resets it; review round 1 of PR #85, A-R1-4). Each attempt (send, repair, send) is bounded by `HEARTBEAT_ATTEMPT_DEADLINE_MS` (2 minutes, below the smallest limit of 5 minutes): its `AbortSignal` goes to the operations `heartbeat` and `monitorEnsure`, and an attempt past it counts as a failure (`no answer in time`). Review round 2 of PR #85, A-R2-2: it ends only the heartbeat's wait for the helper image, never the build: the preparation that a heartbeat or a repair starts (`HelperChannels` `prepare` → `ensureImagePresent`, for the worker of each of their operations) runs with the long signal of `HeartbeatPreparation` (`src/core/session/heartbeatPreparation.ts`: `HELPER_PREBUILD_TIMEOUT_MS`, aborted when the window closes; an `AsyncLocalStorage` scope marks the heartbeat's calls, other operations keep their own signal), so the next attempt joins the build that runs instead of starting it again. Review round 3 of PR #85, A-R3-1, and review round 4, A-R4-1: after a build of the helper image for a heartbeat or a repair that started and failed (`onBuild` of `ensureImagePresent`; an engine that does not answer or a failed presence query is no failed build), the next build on that engine waits 1, 2, then 5 minutes (per engine; preparations that fail together count once); within the wait a heartbeat only checks the tag (`HelperImages.presentImage`, bounded, never builds) and goes on when it is present (which ends the wait), else it is refused at once and counts as a failed heartbeat; any successful preparation on the engine ends the wait, also one of an operation of the user (which never waits) and a build that succeeded (`onImageBuilt`); the wiring is `heartbeatHelperImage` (`src/core/session/heartbeatHelperImage.ts`). A lookup of an engine (`engineFor`, bounded by its sources) past its deadline is joined until `LOOKUP_ABANDON_MS` (twice the deadline) after its start, then given up for a new one; while such a call still hangs, each due heartbeat of that engine counts as one more failure without a new call (A-R1-2). After `HEARTBEAT_WARN_AFTER_FAILURES` (2) failures in a row on the engine of the connected environment the window shows `Messages.heartbeatsFailing` with the minutes left (from the last success, or the start of the series, plus the limit), once per failure streak; a success ends the streak (logged). `WindowHeartbeats.sendFor(id)` sends one heartbeat for one environment at once (Close and Keep Running, Keep Running When Closed, Stop When Closed) by the same path. The worker stays open while a window sends heartbeats (each is an operation). Plan step 8, PR C: `WindowHeartbeats.release(id, limitSeconds, signal)` sends the short release of an environment the window leaves by the same path (section 12), and the first heartbeat of a series on an engine (and the first after a change of the keep settings) carries `clearOnly` entries for the environments of the computer there that are not kept (section 12, *Stale keep records*).
  - *Shared engine* (reviewer note of PR 2). The container decides by the newest record of all sources, but a release only while no record of another source is within its limit (review round 1 of PR #87, A-R1-2), so the release of one computer never stops an environment that a window of another computer still sends heartbeats for, and a newer keep of another computer stays. Plan step 8, PR C: the question `monitor.js records <id>` that the removed Session Monitor process asked before its stops is gone (no window-side check, Q6); `inUseByOtherComputer` stays in the protocol; the subcommand is removed in plan step 11I (U10, decision of 2026-10-08). Delete runs `monitor.js forget <source> <id>` on every engine (best effort).
  - *Limits.* The remote monitor never touches an environment without any record: environments of a build without heartbeats. Since plan step 8, PR A every open sends the first heartbeat on every engine, local and remote. An environment whose first heartbeat and all later heartbeats failed has no record either (the window warns, Q4); plan step 8, PR C: no process of the computer stops it any more.
- **Images on the remote host** (user requests 2026-09-28: "the monitor tasks shall look for all devcontainer-dev* and devcontainer-classroom* images and shall regularly pull these images … always the latest major version image"; "only keep the two latest versions … and clean the images from docker regularly"; "only in the remote scenario"; "all images"; "1 minute after the monitor starts then in the morning again, at 6:07 CEST"; "a configuration for the packages patterns that shall be checked" and "a setting that tells the monitor to fetch in a guided cron style manner"). The Session Monitor of each engine does it (plan step 8, PR A: also on the local Docker; before, only on a remote host).
  - *Settings.* `devEnvLauncher.imageUpdates` (plan step 8, PR A: formerly `remoteImageUpdates`, now for the monitor of every engine, no migration; the patterns; scope `application`; default empty (user decision 2026-09-29: the extension knows no image repositories of its own; the maintenance is off until the user names some); each a repository prefix with a registry, a trailing `*` dropped, invalid entries left out: `imagePrefixesOf`; not Docker Hub (`docker.io`, `index.docker.io`, `registry-1.docker.io`), whose images `docker image ls` lists without the registry, so they would never match (review round 8, S4); an empty list turns it off; at most 50, review round 5 of PR #57, P1: `maxItems` in the settings, and the first 50 are used, as the monitor takes no more; review round 6, F2: each at most 128 characters, and together at most 4096 characters as JSON, as they go on the command line of `docker run` of the monitor: without the limit, very long patterns made that line too long and no monitor started at all; review round 9: `DEVENV_IMAGE_PREFIXES` carries only as many as that line still takes, as `settings -` brings all of them at each open; the pattern of the setting takes exactly what the code takes, checked by a unit test, T2) and `devEnvLauncher.imageUpdateSchedule` (formerly `remoteImageUpdateSchedule`; scope `application`; a cron expression of five fields, minute hour day-of-month month day-of-week, in the time zone of this computer; default `7 6 * * *`, every day at 06:07). The schedule is "guided": the settings UI shows a table of the fields and examples (`markdownDescription`), `settings.json` offers the examples (`7 6 * * *`, `7 6 * * 1-5`, `7 6,18 * * *`, `7 */6 * * *`, `7 6 * * 1`, `7 6 1 * *`), and a pattern checks the five fields; an invalid expression means the default (`normalizeSettings`). The cron syntax (src/core/remoteMonitor/cron.ts, `parseCronSchedule`): each field `*`, a number, a range `a-b`, a step `*/n`, `a-b/n` or `a/n`, or a list of these; months and weekdays also by their three-letter English names; weekday 0 and 7 are Sunday; when both the day of the month and the day of the week are restricted (neither starts with `*`), a day that matches either one counts, otherwise it has to match both, as Vixie cron (review round 3 of PR #57, N2: before, a step such as `*/2` in a day field that starts with `*` was ignored). `@daily` and the like are not supported.
  - *Container.* The monitor gets the settings at its creation as `DEVENV_IMAGE_PREFIXES` (JSON; all prefixes, at most 4096 characters as JSON by `imagePrefixesOf`: plan step 3 removed the loop that cut them to what the Windows command line took next to the script), `DEVENV_IMAGE_SCHEDULE` and `DEVENV_IMAGE_TZ` (the time zone of this computer, `Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone`; review round 5, P2: UTC when Node.js does not know it, `Etc/Unknown` for an empty TZ, which the monitor refused at every open). Review round 1 of PR #57 (C): only whether the image maintenance is on is part of its label (`IMAGE_MAINTENANCE_LABEL_PART`), because the container has the default network then (outbound only: it reads the tags of the registry; still no published port, no capability, no new privileges) and `--network none` without; the settings themselves are not, so computers with other patterns, another schedule, or another time zone on the same engine do not replace the monitor at each open (a replacement ended a running pull, started a pass a minute later, and held every stop for 2 minutes). Instead each open gives the monitor the settings of this computer (plan step 11E6, decision D1 of 2026-10-05: they travel with the operation `open`, and the worker gives them to the monitor of its engine right after its ensure, at every open whose settings name prefixes; one bounded command, cancellable, review round 1 of PR #111, A-L4; before, the extension sent them in the background, on the Docker target of the open, when they changed or an hour after the last send per host and window, review rounds 2 and 4 of PR #57, R6 and L2): the entry `monitorSettings` of the script registry, `node /opt/devenv/monitor.js settings -` with `{ "prefixes": [...], "schedule": "…", "timeZone": "…" }` on its standard input (strict: `parseImageSettingsInput`; at most 50 prefixes), stored in `/state/image-settings.json` (atomic). The monitor reads that file before each check and each pass (`CurrentImageSettings`), so the newest settings sent by any computer apply from the next minute on; without the file, those of its container. A failed send is tried again at the next open.
  - *All images.* ghcr.io lists no repositories without a token, and the monitor never holds one. So the extension reads the container packages of each owner of a ghcr.io prefix in the background, at most once an hour per engine and window (plan step 11E6, decision D1 of 2026-10-05: `imageLists`, `src/vscode/imageLists.ts`; an open carries the list that a read before it gave, and never waits for one), with the GitHub session (scope `read:packages`, `getPackagesCredentials({ interactive: false })`): `GET /orgs/<owner>/packages?package_type=container` (or `/users/<owner>/…` for a user), all pages (`ghcrRepositories` in src/core/remoteMonitor/imageRepositories.ts), and the open gives the names of the public packages that start with a prefix to the monitor: right after the ensure, the worker runs the entry `monitorImages` of the script registry, `node /opt/devenv/monitor.js images -` with `{ "repositories": [...] }` on its standard input (at most 500, each `registry/path` in lower case, strict: `parseImageListInput`); the monitor stores it in its volume (`/state/images.json`, atomic). Only the names travel, never the token. Review round 1 of PR #57: the listing runs in the background with a time limit of 60 s for all its requests (`PACKAGES_TIMEOUT_MS`; B: before, a GitHub that did not answer held Start); more than 500 names are cut to the first 500, logged (I: before, the monitor refused the whole list); private and internal packages are left out, because the monitor reads the tags without a token and could never update them (J); a list that the monitor did not take goes with the next open again, not after an hour (D). Without a session with that scope, the log says so and a question offers **Sign in** once per window; the monitor then updates only the repositories that it has images of.
  - *The passes* (src/remoteMonitor/images.ts, `ImageMaintenance`; the schedule: `ImageSchedule` in src/remoteMonitor/main.ts). One minute after the monitor starts, then at each time of the schedule: every minute (`IMAGE_CHECK_MS`) it looks whether a time of the schedule lies after its previous check (`nextCronTime` in its time zone, daylight saving time included: `7 6 * * *` in Europe/Vienna is 04:07 UTC in summer and 05:07 UTC in winter; review round 2 of PR #57 (R1, R2, R7), as cron: a wall time that the change to summer time skips runs later by the change on the wall clock (02:30 → 03:30 for a change of one hour), east and west of UTC alike, also more than 12 hours ahead of UTC (review round 3, N3: Pacific/Auckland) and for a change of two hours (N4: Antarctica/Troll); in the hour that the change to winter time repeats, a schedule of every hour (`*` in the hour field) runs at both times and one of fixed hours once, at the first); at most one pass runs at a time, and a time that comes during a pass is left out (logged; review round 10 of PR #57, U1: also for a pass that a check started, whose checks of the following minutes do not run; before, a second pass followed at once). A failed write of a file of the volume leaves no temporary file behind (review round 10, U2). A clock of the host that steps back a little does not run a time again that was handled already (R3); one that steps back by more than an hour (a clock that was far ahead, then corrected) starts the schedule again from its time, logged (review round 4, L1: before, no pass came until the clock caught up). Each request to a registry ends within 30 s in any case, and one that cannot start (an invalid URL of the realm of a registry's challenge, or an invalid token in the header) fails at once without a timer left behind (review round 3, N1: that timer ended the monitor with an uncaught error, and it restarted in a loop) (R5: before, a registry that sent a byte now and then, or a connection cut in the middle of an answer, kept the pass, and with it every later one, open for ever). A schedule that never matches (`0 3 31 2 *`) runs only the first pass. A pass: (1) the repositories with a prefix: those of the list of the images of the engine (plan step 11I, U1: over the Engine API, as the rows of `docker image ls -a`, `localImagesOf`; an answer of up to 16 MiB, review round 1 of PR #126, F2) and those of the stored list; (2) for each, the tags of its registry (anonymous: the token of the Bearer challenge of `/v2/<path>/tags/list`, all pages, only https realms), the highest plain major tag (`2` of `latest`, `2`, `2.0`, `2.0.14`, `2.0.14-amd64`), and a pull of `<repository>:<major>` (plan step 11I, U1: the pull of the Engine API without a login, as `docker pull --quiet` of the monitor's CLI before; an error in the stream of the pull fails it; the engine downloads only what changed; a public image needs no sign-in); a repository whose tags cannot be read is not updated (logged); (3) for each repository on the engine, the versions, newest first: an image ID is one version, ranked by its highest version tag, where a shorter tag names the newest image of its line and so ranks above the longer ones (`3.0.0` > `2` > `2.0` > `2.0.14` > `2.0.13`; review round 1 of PR #57, A: before, a missing part counted as -1, so the image just pulled as `:2`, which has no longer version tag on the engine, ranked below older `2.0.x` images and was removed), an ID without any tag of the repository below those with one, and between those by creation time; review round 2 of PR #57 (R4): an ID whose tags are no versions (only `latest`, or `2.0.14-amd64`) is no version of the line that the monitor pulls (it never pulls `latest`, so such an image can be of any age): it takes no kept place and is never removed. The two newest stay; each older one is removed by its references in the repository (plan step 11I, U1: one removal of the Engine API per reference, each tried, as `docker image rm <repository>:<tag>…` did; or its ID when it has none; an image in use answers 409 and stays), never with force, when no container uses it (the containers of the engine with the filter `ancestor=<id>`, as `docker ps -a --filter ancestor=<id>` before) and no other image is built on it. Review round 1 of PR #57 (G): Docker, asked to remove the tag of an image that another image (an environment image) is built on, removes only the tag and keeps the image, so the monitor checks that itself: it reads the layers of all images once per pass (the IDs of the list of the images, then the inspect of each, its `RootFS.Layers`; plan step 11I, U1: over the Engine API, before `docker image ls -a -q --no-trunc` and `docker image inspect --format '{{.Id}} {{json .RootFS.Layers}}'`, 100 at a time) and leaves an image alone whose layers are the start of the layers of another image (logged: "stays: another image is built on it"); when the layers cannot be read, it removes nothing in that pass. Review round 6 of PR #57 (F1) and round 7: the image that a pull replaces loses its tag; with the containerd image store (the default of new installations of Docker Engine 29) it is kept as a dangling image without its repository (`<none> <none>`), and the Docker CLI 29 lists untagged images only with `-a` on the classic store too (there as `<repository> <none>`; the monitor listed with `-a`; plan step 11I, U1: the list of the Engine API has them). So it was never seen and never removed. Now the monitor keeps, per repository in `/state/replaced-images.json` (at most 200 per repository; beyond that the IDs that are tagged now go first, then the oldest, review round 8, S2: before, 20, and the newest untagged ones could go; written at once, so a monitor that ends in the middle of a pass loses nothing), every ID that it sees with a tag of the repository, at a pass and at each minute check while no pass runs (review round 8, S3: one list of the images; so an image that the extension pulls at an open and replaces at the next one before a pass is known; review round 9, T1: a check that takes longer than a minute is not joined by the next, and each write of a file of the volume has a temporary file of its own), and the ID of `<repository>:<major>` before its own pull when the pull changed it (the inspect of the image; `docker image inspect --format '{{.Id}}'` before plan step 11I, U1). Round 7: before, only the IDs that its own pull replaced, but the update of the extension at each open (or a user) usually pulls first. Limit: an image that was replaced before the monitor ever saw it with a tag (for example before this feature, with the containerd image store) is not known; `docker image prune` removes such dangling images. Review round 8 (S1): an ID can be a version of two repositories (a user tagged it in both); one that one of them keeps as one of its two newest is removed by none of them. Each pass adds those IDs that still exist without any tag (the inspect of the image: empty `RepoTags`, and its `Created`; `docker image inspect --format '{{json .RepoTags}}\t{{.Created}}'` before plan step 11I, U1) to the untagged images of the repository, so they go through the same rules (the two newest stay, no container, no image built on it, never force) and are removed by ID; an ID that is gone, or tagged again, is forgotten. Creation times are compared as times (RFC 3339 of the list and of the inspect; `docker image ls` and `docker image inspect` wrote them differently). Each removal and each problem is one line of `docker logs devenv-session-monitor`.
- **Live checks** (not covered by the tests, to do on real machines):
  - The Dev Containers extension attaches to a container on the remote host through the context of the host (for example `htldvm`; a new window and a window restored from **Open Recent**), installs the VS Code server there, and applies the label `devcontainer.metadata` (`remoteEnv`, `postAttachCommand`, `shutdownAction: none`).
  - Port forwarding of the attached window reaches a server in the container; a port published on `127.0.0.1` is not reachable from the local computer.
  - Stop on close (plan step 8, PR C): closing a window sends the release within about 2 s of `deactivate()`, also over SSH; the Session Monitor container stops the container after the waiting time (at least 60 s) plus 150 s (`RELEASE_MARGIN_SECONDS`, review round 1 of PR #87), and after `stopAfterMinutes` when the release was lost (VS Code killed); a window reload keeps it.
  - The image maintenance on an engine with the containerd image store and on one with the classic store: after two updates of `:<major>`, the oldest replaced image is removed (review round 6 of PR #57, F1).
  - The remote Session Monitor with the real helper image on a remote engine (rootful and rootless): the container starts, exits when idle and is started again at the next open (plan step 8, PR B), stops an environment after the computer went to sleep or offline for the limit, keeps a kept one, and `docker logs devenv-session-monitor` shows each stop. Close and Keep Running in a remote window (the heartbeat, then the window closes).
  - Whether Docker Desktop sets its own context again when it starts or updates, and the mismatch question of a window from **Open Recent** of the other host (continue after the switch back; close without it).
  - The messages with the network off, an unknown host key, and a key not in the agent: no hang, no prompt (also on Windows with OpenSSH `ssh.exe`, which has no new session: the SSH check with `BatchMode` answers first, and no `ssh.exe` is left running after a time limit).
  - A rootless engine on the remote computer: the socket found, the helper runs, published ports ≥ 1024.
  - Delete on the remote host (volumes, images, Docker Compose project) and the token in the tmpfs there (`gh` and Git in the container).
  - The image maintenance on a real remote host: the first pass one minute after the monitor starts, the daily pass at 06:07 local time, the pull of `ghcr.io/majikmate/devcontainer-*:<major>`, a repository that the host did not have yet (from the list of the packages), and that an image of an environment and one that a stopped container uses stay.


## 17. Helper channel

The agreed plan is in [plan-remote-worker.md](plan-remote-worker.md). The helper channel is the **worker** of the plan (the names stay): one container per window and Docker engine, local or remote, that runs every flow of the extension next to that engine (decisions of 2026-10-03, "the worker is the complete deputy"). User requests 2026-09-28 started it: on a remote host every Docker call opened its own SSH connection (about 1.6 s each); an operation now costs one message. The extension sets the worker up (the helper image, its start, the check of its engine) and sends it operations; the worker runs every Docker action of a flow over the Engine API, the steps on the volume of an environment in a batch helper, and the commands in containers from the script registry (section 6). Plan step 11I1 removed the relay of the extension's Docker calls through the worker, its locks and batch helpers of the extension, and the routing of the CLI adapter; plan step 11I2 removed the CLI adapter itself.

- **The channels of a window.** `HelperChannels` (`src/core/helperChannel/helperChannels.ts`) keeps one channel per Docker engine (`keyOf`: its context and its endpoint, review round 5, F1; only the local Docker and SSH hosts, never another endpoint; a known limitation: an SSH alias of `~/.ssh/config` that was pointed to another machine keeps its key, so a channel that is open still reaches the old engine until it closes), opened at the first operation, closed after 10 minutes without one (`CHANNEL_IDLE_CLOSE_MS`), and opened again after it was lost. Before it opens one, it makes the state consistent (rule D1 of 2026-09-30): the helper image on that engine (`HelperChannelsOptions.prepare`, extension.ts: `HelperImages.ensureImagePresent`, which builds only a missing tag), then the open in full. The refresh of the sidebar and the other reads in the background, which no user starts, only check the tag (`checkPresent` → `HelperImages.checkImagePresent`: never a build, PR #76 review round 1, A-R1-1 and A-R1-2), keep the wait after a failed open, and wait at most 30 s for an open that still runs (`CHANNEL_PASSIVE_OPEN_WAIT_MS`, PR #76 review rounds 2 and 3). When either fails, the operation is refused with the cause (`HelperChannelError('unavailable')`) and runs nowhere else. An operation that was not sent because its channel had closed (`closed`: it did not run) is sent once more through a channel made ready again; one that was sent is never sent again (a lost channel rejects it as `lost`). After a failed open, the next attempt for that engine waits 5 minutes (`CHANNEL_RETRY_AFTER_FAILURE_MS`); an operation of the user ends that wait for its engine, and `clearFailures` for every engine when `docker info` reports the engine running after it did not, or when a build of the helper image succeeded (`HelperImagesDeps.onImageBuilt`). The extension runs no Docker action of a flow itself: its Docker calls are those of the bootstrap (`BootstrapDocker`, plan step 11F2), and one that does not only read is logged by its command, never with its arguments (`directCommandName`).
- **Container.** `channelRunArgs`: `docker run --rm -i --pull never --name devenv-channel-<12 hex digits> --label nimblescape.devenv.helper-run=true --label nimblescape.devenv.helper-channel=<protocol>-<12 hex digits of sha256(script, pipe loader)> --network bridge --log-driver none --cap-drop ALL --security-opt no-new-privileges --mount type=bind,source=<socket>,target=/var/run/docker.sock --mount type=volume,source=devenv-session-monitor,target=/state <helper tag> node -e <pipe loader> /opt/devenv/channel.js <sha256 of the script> startChannel`, started by the Docker CLI of the extension with the Docker context of the target (`BootstrapDocker.start` within `runWithDockerTarget`). The socket is the one of that engine (section 6: for a remote host the recorded rootless socket, else `/var/run/docker.sock`); the volume of the Session Monitor holds the lock files (*Locks* below). Outbound network only, on the default bridge, never a published port (plan step 11E3a; its HTTPS goes through the proxy of the daemon, decision C1 of 2026-10-05; a daemon without its default bridge gets `--network none`, and the log says that the image checks and downloads cannot reach the network, review round 1 of PR #109, A-L3). `--log-driver none` (review round 2, B3): the engine keeps no log of the commands and the output of the worker, whatever its log driver. Never a pull: without the helper image on the host the start fails. No restart policy and no `-d`: it lives only as long as its connection.
- **Start and engine check.** The pipe loader (section 18) checks the first line of its input, the script as a JSON string, against the hash of its command, stores it at `/opt/devenv/channel.js`, and calls its `startChannel` (`src/helperChannel/main.ts`) with the rest of the input. The extension checks the length of that line as the loader reads it (8 MiB, `MAX_BUNDLE_LINE_LENGTH`; review round 1, P6). Then `hello` (the protocol version, 3; the answer names the Node.js version and the operations; another version closes the channel), then the operation `probe`: the version of the engine and its identity, its ID and root folder (`GET /version`, `GET /info`: `DockerEngine.version`, `identity`; on both sides the ID and the root folder are at most 1024 characters each, `MAX_ENGINE_IDENTITY_LENGTH`). The extension compares that identity as values (`sameEngine`; Go's `{{json}}` escapes `<`, `>` and `&`, the Engine API does not) with the identity that its own Docker CLI reads in the context of the target (`ENGINE_IDENTITY_ARGS`, `engineIdentity`). Missing on either side, or different: the worker is closed and the open fails (plan step 5, PR A), so a worker whose socket mount reaches another engine than the context, for example with `DOCKER_HOST` set to a TCP endpoint of this computer, is never used. Then, in the background, `sweep` (*The sweep* below).
- **Messages** (`src/core/helperChannel/protocol.ts`, one JSON object per line on the standard input and output of `docker run`; both sides check each strictly). From the extension: `hello`, `ping` (every 15 s), `op` (`id`, `op`, `params`, optional `secrets` and `timeoutMs`), `cancel`, `answer`. From the worker: `hello`, `pong`, `progress` (a step began), `log` (a line of the log, info or warn), `out` (output of a tool, stdout or stderr, in pieces of at most 16 K characters), `ask`, `result` (a value, or a failure with a code and whether it was cancelled or timed out), `cancelled`. A line is at most 4 M characters, a longer one ends the channel; the extension sends at most 256 KiB per request (`MAX_CHANNEL_REQUEST_BYTES`, review round 5, F3: the pings wait behind a request, so a request must reach the worker well within the pong time limit also on a slow link), and the worker counts any text that arrives as a sign of life. A request that the channel cannot carry (longer than that, a secret shorter than 4 characters, which could not be masked, a time limit that is no whole number of milliseconds from 1 to 24 hours) is not sent (`HelperChannelError('unsendable')`, review round 1, P2, S6), and refused. At most 8 operations of a channel run at the same time (`MAX_CONCURRENT_OPERATIONS`); a place that ends goes straight to the next waiting operation, which waits at most 5 s or its own time limit (`CHANNEL_SLOT_WAIT_MS`, review round 5, F2), and a waiting operation whose signal aborted is never sent (review round 1, L1, L5); one whose wait runs out is not sent either (`unsendable`) and is refused.
- **Operations** (`OPERATIONS` in `src/helperChannel/operations.ts`): one per flow, each with typed parameters that both sides check with the same function of `protocol.ts` (`parse…Params`; the extension checks each value too, `parse…Value`); no Docker command crosses the channel (section 0 of the plan). `probe` and `sweep` at the start of each worker; `refresh` and `windowState` (the reads of the sidebar and of an attached window; a refresh carries at most 200 environments, `MAX_REFRESH_ENVIRONMENTS`, and a longer list is refused as `unsendable`, never read without the worker); `tokenRemove`; `stop`; `listConfigurations` (the listing of Select configuration); `deleteCheck` and `delete`; `reconcile` (the registry rebuilt from the volumes); `heartbeat`, `recordGitState` and `monitorEnsure` (the Session Monitor, below); and `open`. The flows run the pipeline of the worker (`EnvironmentService`, built by `workerServices` in `src/core/worker/workerServices.ts`, over `EngineDocker`), `readEnvironmentStates` (the refresh), or a flow of `src/core/worker/` (`stopFlow`, `tokenRemoveFlow`, `windowStateFlow`, `monitorFlow`); a refusal of the pipeline is the value of the operation (`{ refused }`), so the extension shows it as before the move.
- **Requests of an operation** (plan step 11A; decision of 2026-10-04, one operations interface in both directions). An operation asks the extension through `ask` (`question`, `local`, `record`, `secret`; at most 16 open per operation) and waits for its `answer`; a cancel or the end of the operation ends the wait, and a late answer is ignored. Each operation may send only its own requests (`FLOW_REQUESTS` in `src/core/worker/hostSide.ts`), those that change an environment only for the environment of the operation (`SCOPED_REQUESTS`; the rebuild of a lost registry, `record restore`, only adds the missing entries of the volumes of the operation's Docker host), and the extension decides each `record` request with its own clock, owner and view of the windows under its registry lock (`hostSideHandler`, `src/core/worker/hostSideHandler.ts`; `src/vscode/hostSide.ts`); there is no generic write of the registry (plan step 11E4c). The extension is the trusted side: the worker does not check its answers again (decision U6 of 2026-10-08).
- **Secrets** (plan step 11A). An operation holds only the named secrets that the answers of its requests gave it (`token`, the GitHub token; `registry`, the login of a registry; the extension sends none with the operation itself), at most 8, each of 4 to 4096 characters. The worker masks every value that the operation ever held, longest first (each operation with its own secrets and redactors), in every progress, log, output, failure and value that it sends back (`StreamRedactor` also when a piece splits one; a log or progress text is masked before it is cut, review round 1, S2), and the output of each exec as it comes (plan step 11E1). A secret is only ever the standard input of a process (an exec, a step of a batch helper) or a header of a request: `X-Registry-Auth` of a pull to the engine, and the Basic `Authorization` of the HTTPS requests of the image check to that registry and to the token service that it names (HTTPS only; through the proxy of the daemon when it has one for that host, section 9); never an argument, a variable or a log line; a registry login is forgotten when the check or pull that needed it ended (decision B1 of 2026-10-05). The extension never logs the parameters or the secrets of an operation.
- **Progress and the log** (user requests 2026-09-28: "the output of these operations piped to our console, as detailed as the helper writes the logs and records the results"). `progress` and `log` are lines of the Dev Environments output (an open shows its steps as its progress); the output of a tool goes to the log as it comes. While more than 1 M characters of answers wait to be written (`process.stdout.write` to a pipe does not wait), the worker pauses the reading of the output of its batch helpers (`OperationContext.pausable`, review round 2, A2). A log or progress text is cut to 16 K characters, and a result longer than the extension reads fails as `tooLarge` instead of ending the channel (review round 2, C1).
- **Cancel and time limits.** A cancel (the signal of the caller), the time limit of the operation, or the end of the worker aborts the signal of the operation: its requests to the engine end, its batch helper is stopped and removed by its session label, and its result is sent once its handler ended. The extension gives each flow its time limit (the longest, the open: 4 hours, `OPEN_FLOW_TIMEOUT_MS`). On its signal the extension sends the cancel and rejects with an AbortError when the worker confirmed it (review round 4, M2): by the result of the operation, or by `cancelled` for one that had ended there already; when the channel is lost first, the operation rejects as `lost` (its outcome is not known). It gives up on a result that does not come 50 s after the time limit (`CHANNEL_RESULT_GRACE_MS`, review round 1, P4: longer than the kill grace of 5 s and the removal of a batch helper within 30 s).
- **It ends by itself** (user request 2026-09-28: nothing on the host can clean up after it). Four independent ways, each enough alone: (1) the end of its input (the connection closed or `docker run` ended); (2) no message for 60 s (`CHANNEL_SILENCE_EXIT_MS`) while the connection hangs without an end, for example after the computer went to sleep; the extension sends a ping every 15 s; (3) no operation for 15 minutes and none running (`CHANNEL_SERVER_IDLE_EXIT_MS`, a backstop behind the idle close of the extension); (4) `--rm` removes the container when the script ended, and tini passes signals on. Before it exits it cancels every operation; a hard deadline (40 s) ends it even when one does not end. An answer that cannot be written (EPIPE), a line that is too long, SIGTERM, SIGINT and SIGHUP end it the same way, and so does an uncaught error (review round 1, P5), after which a timer ends the process 45 s later in any case. The kernel frees the locks of the worker when it ends, whatever the way. The extension takes the channel as lost when no answer came for 30 s (`CHANNEL_PONG_TIMEOUT_MS`, checked at each ping: at most 45 s after the last answer, before the worker's 60 s; review round 1, P3), stops `docker run` (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after 5 s; before the SIGKILL, `pkill -KILL -P <pid>` ends the programs that it started, above all the `ssh` that the Docker CLI starts in a session of its own; review round 1, L2; review round 2, A3), and rejects what ran with `HelperChannelError('lost')`. When the window closes (`HelperChannels.dispose`), each channel is closed at once (`closeNow`: `docker run` and the programs that it started get SIGKILL now, synchronously, because the extension host may end right after and none of its timers runs then; one `exit` handler of the extension host does the same for every program of `NodeProcessRunner.start` that still runs; review round 4, M3); the container then ends by the end of its input, at the latest by its silence. A crash of the extension host (SIGKILL) leaves those programs on the computer until TCP gives up; the container still ends by its silence.
- **The sweep** (review round 4, M1; plan step 11I, PR A and decision U5 of 2026-10-08). At each start of a worker, the extension sends `sweep` in the background: the prune of the stopped containers with the label `nimblescape.devenv.helper-run` (any value: every helper container, the workers and their batch helpers) and without the label `nimblescape.devenv.session-monitor` (`label!`), created more than 10 minutes ago (`POST /containers/prune` with `SWEEP_FILTERS`). A worker or a batch helper whose create succeeded and whose start never came (a connection or a worker that broke between the two; `--rm` and AutoRemove act only after an exit) would stay `created` for ever (a batch helper with the volume of its environment, which a later Delete then could not remove). A prune removes only stopped containers, the age keeps it away from a start that runs now, and the Session Monitor carries no `nimblescape.devenv.helper-run`, which the second filter guards too. Its value is the number of removed containers (`SweepValue`, checked by `parseSweepValue` and logged when it is not 0).
- **The engine of the flows** (plan steps 10A, 11B1 and 11B3; section 0 of the plan). Every Docker action of the worker goes through the port `DockerEngine` (`src/core/worker/dockerEngine.ts`), implemented over the Docker Engine API on the socket (`src/helperChannel/engineApi.ts`, `engineClient.ts`): no `docker` process of its own (plan step 11I, PR A removed the last ones, `probe` and `sweep`). An exec runs over a hijacked connection, so the standard input of a process (a secret) never becomes an argument. The Docker of the pipeline (`EnvironmentDocker`) is `EngineDocker` over that port (`src/core/worker/engineDocker.ts`; plan step 11I2: its one implementation). A pull always names a tag or a digest (`:latest` when the reference has neither; never every tag of a repository) and sends the login of its registry only in the header `X-Registry-Auth`; an image gets its labels by a commit of a created, never started container, and the worker reads a file of an image through the archive endpoint of one (decision of 2026-10-03: no extra containers where the API suffices). The Session Monitor talks to its engine with the same client (decision U1 of 2026-10-08).
- **The dev container of an environment** (plan step 11I, decision U4 of 2026-10-08). One rule for every flow (`devContainerOf` in `src/core/worker/environmentContainers.ts`): among the containers with the label of the environment, its dev containers (without the label of the other services of Docker Compose, or with the recorded name), the one with the recorded name whatever its state, else the newest running one, else the newest one (by the time of the create, as a number). The open (`EngineDocker.findContainer`), the refresh, and the Git state of Delete's check and of a release use it; Stop stops every running container of the environment (its running dev containers in the order of the rule, the Git state read from the first, then the running services), and the token removal empties every running dev container (`runningDevContainers`). The window state reads only the container that the window is attached to, so it sees that one as missing once it is gone, and the window reconnects through the open.
- **Batch helper** (plan step 6, PR B; decisions of 2026-09-29, "steps run inside the worker; volume steps in one helper per operation, never one container per step", and Q1, Q2, Q4 of 2026-10-01). The worker starts once per window and engine, before any environment is chosen, and a running container cannot get a volume mounted later; so the steps that need the volume of an environment run in one helper container per operation, which the worker starts with that volume (`workerBatchSession`, `src/helperChannel/batch.ts`). It first checks that the volume exists (a missing volume is refused with the code `missingVolume`, so the mount never creates an empty volume without our labels), then runs the helper over the Engine API as `docker run --rm -i` did (plan step 11G3, `DockerEngine.runAttached`, `batchRunSpec`): the pinned helper image of the operation by its ID (the worker's own image, plan step 11I, U7), never pulled; the label `nimblescape.devenv.helper-run` and the session label `nimblescape.devenv.channel-step=<session>` (a new random value per session, 96 bits, never used again: `newCleanupLabel`); the volume at `/workspaces`, the cache volume, the socket in `/run/devenv-docker` (a folder that only root can enter), the secrets tmpfs (`/run/devenv-secrets`, 0700, in memory), no new privileges, no log of the engine, no variable, no part of the script or of a secret. Its command is the pipe loader with `/opt/devenv/batch.js` and the entry `startBatchHelper`: the helper runs the worker's own script (one bundle, two entries), a second ChannelServer whose operations are the step kinds, and the worker talks to it as the extension talks to the worker (`HelperChannel` over the attached input and output). It ends with its session or its operation (its input ends; AutoRemove), and the worker removes its container by the session label in every case, never by a name; it also ends by itself as the worker does (the end of its input, also when the worker is killed, 60 s of silence, 15 minutes without a step). The worker takes it as lost only after 6 hours of silence (`BATCH_HOLD_LIMIT_MS`), because it reads none of its answers while the connection of the extension is congested; a helper that ends is seen by its exit.
  - *Its steps* (`src/core/helper/batchSteps.ts`; plan step 7; plan step 11I, decision U2 (b) of 2026-10-08). The fixed table of the step kinds: `clone`, `readFiles`, `listConfigs`, `readConfiguration`, `build`, `composeModel`, `composeHash`, `createFolders`, `up`, `runUserCommands`, `gitFiles`, `ownershipFix`, and `repositoryOwnershipFix` (plan step 11G1). The worker sends a kind and its parameters (at most 3 Mi characters of JSON per step, `MAX_BATCH_INPUT_CHARACTERS`; a larger input is not sent, `unsendable`); the helper checks them strictly (no unknown key, a variable only by a name that `isPassableEnvName` accepts: never `PATH`, `HOSTNAME`, `PWD`, `OLDPWD`, `SHLVL`, `IFS`, `ENV`, `TMPDIR`, `TMP`, `TEMP`, `NODE_OPTIONS`, `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` or `BROWSER`, nor one that starts with `DOCKER_`, `BUILDX_`, `BUILDKIT_`, `LD_`, `REMOTE_CONTAINERS` or `VSCODE_`, in any case) and builds the command itself with the builders of `scripts.ts`, `devcontainerCli.ts`, `stepInputs.ts` and `gitSummary.ts`, the arguments as positional parameters; it never runs a command line that it was sent, and an unknown kind is refused. On purpose these steps are no entries of the script registry: they are processes of the helper in its own container, not execs of the engine (U2 (b)). One step runs at a time, each in a process group of its own; its time limit or its cancel ends that group (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after 5 s), what is left of the group is killed when the step ends, and the session stays usable. `docker compose config` of `composeModel` and `composeHash` is the one use of Docker Compose besides the Dev Container CLI's, and it needs no engine (decision U3).
  - *Isolation inside the one container* (Q2 of 2026-10-01; user decision of 2026-10-01, "we shall run as the repo owner user. that is what a real user would do as well."). The helper runs as root and checks before its first step that it is root, that the folder of the socket and the secrets tmpfs are root's and 0700, and links `/var/run/docker.sock` to the socket for the root steps (the default of the Docker CLI and the Dev Container CLI; DOCKER_HOST is never set); otherwise every step is refused (`unsafe`). Every step has the network of the helper (the default network of the engine) and its cache volume: the `--network none` and the tmpfs over `CONFIG_FOLDER` of the former per-step runs are gone (accepted with Q2 of 2026-10-01). The clone runs as the user `devenv-git` (uid 52741, `BATCH_GIT_UID`; `resources/helper/Dockerfile`, no home, no login) through `setpriv --reuid --regid --clear-groups --inh-caps=-all --bounding-set=-all --no-new-privs`, with HOME=/nonexistent: it cannot reach the socket; for its step `CONFIG_FOLDER` is closed to it, `/workspaces` is writable like `/tmp`, and the secrets tmpfs is its own (its token); after the step every process of that user is killed, its files in the volume get root, its files elsewhere in the container are removed, and the modes and owners are restored. Before its first Git step, each helper repairs what a killed helper may have left (`repairCutOffGitStep`, as root: the files of the Git user in the volume get root, and temporary folders of clones older than 60 minutes are removed). `composeModel`, `composeHash`, `readFiles`, `listConfigs` and `createFolders` run as the user that owns the repository folder (its uid:gid read with `lstat` at step time; as root when root owns it; as nobody for a missing folder, a link or no folder), with HOME=/nonexistent; for the Compose steps `CONFIG_FOLDER` (the Git configuration, the Docker configuration of the dev container and gh's `config.yml`, not the token) is root's and 0700 during the step, because Compose follows `env_file` and `include` of the repository (review round 1 of PR #84, A-R1-1: only for those steps, so that Git in a running dev container keeps reading its configuration there during the other ones). For each of these steps `OVERRIDE_FOLDER` is new, empty and that user's (0700); after the step every process of that user is killed (not for a root owner, whose step ends with its process group), and `OVERRIDE_FOLDER` is removed as root. The steps of the Dev Container CLI, `gitFiles`, `ownershipFix` and `repositoryOwnershipFix` run as root with the socket. `gitFiles` (`GIT_FILES_SCRIPT`) runs no Git in the repository: its Git only edits a copy of the `gitconfig` of `CONFIG_FOLDER` in a folder of root in the helper's `/tmp` (`git config --file`, from `/`), so no program that a repository configuration names runs as root with the socket. Docker Compose fetches no remote `include`: COMPOSE_EXPERIMENTAL_GIT_REMOTE and COMPOSE_EXPERIMENTAL_OCI_REMOTE are `false` on the process of every step, after the variables of the request. The variables of a step are set on its process only, never on the container (the helper's own, then those of the request, then the Compose switches; DOCKER_HOST is removed). In the batch helper, the token travels only in the `secrets` of a step: extension → worker → helper → the standard input of the clone, which writes it to the tmpfs (TOKEN_PRELUDE) and removes it, and the helper empties the tmpfs after the step; `up` and `runUserCommands` take it only to mask their output. Into the dev container it goes on the standard input of an exec of the registry entry `tokenWrite` (section 6; section 7, *Token in the memory of the container*). **Known limitation:** a repository owned by root (the first open before the ownership fix, after the clone, whose files get root; or a dev container with `remoteUser` root) gets no `CONFIG_FOLDER` isolation for the owner steps, and its Compose read runs as root with the socket in reach, with no `kill -9 -1` after it (that would end the helper; the process group of the step still ends with it).
  - *Batch scope of the opens* (plan step 6, PR C; plan step 7; D1). The flows that need the volume (the opens: Start, Rebuild, Select configuration, Clone again, a first open; and the listing of Select configuration) run every step in ONE batch helper of the operation: `withEnvironmentLock(…, { batchVolume })` runs the flow in the batch scope of the volume (`src/core/helper/batchScope.ts`), and `WorkspaceHelper.runStreams` sends each step to the session of the scope (plan step 11I, U7: on the worker's own image). The session opens at the first volume step (after the lock, and after a first open or Clone again created the volume) and closes when the scope ends (success, failure, cancel), before the lock is released, and before a volume that it mounts is removed. A step outside a batch scope is an internal error and starts nothing. A step for another volume than the one of the scope, a session that cannot be opened, a lost lock, or a step that failed because its session was lost refuse the operation (`BatchHelperUnavailableError`, code `helperFailed`; user decision of 2026-10-01, D1: a running, current container is then not opened as it is), and every later step of the scope is refused too. A session that ended between two steps (lost, closed, or idle while a question to the user was open) is replaced by a new one under the same lock before the next step, each time (when that fails, the operation is refused); a step is never repeated or moved.
- **Locks** (plan step 5, PR B; plan step 6, PR A; user decisions D1, D2, D3 of 2026-09-30, Q3 of 2026-10-01). The flows that take the lock of an environment (`open`, `listConfigurations`, `stop`, `delete`) take it on the Docker host themselves (`takeEnvironmentLock` in `src/helperChannel/lock.ts`, through `workerEnvironmentLock` for the pipeline), the same for the local and a remote Docker: `/state/locks/<environmentId>.lock` in the volume `devenv-session-monitor`, which every worker mounts at `/state` (the folder 0700, the file 0600, opened with O_NOFOLLOW and O_NONBLOCK, anything but a plain file refused; the lock files are never deleted), locked by `flock -w <wait> -E 75` on the open file of the worker (`src/core/helperChannel/lockFile.ts`). The lock stays with that file until the flow lets it go or the worker ends: the kernel frees it then, whatever the way. A lock held by another window or computer is waited for 10 s (`ENVIRONMENT_LOCK_WAIT_SECONDS`), then the operation is refused (D3: "…is busy with an operation from another window or computer; try again in a moment"); a lock that cannot be taken for another reason refuses it too (D1); either way nothing is changed. The lock stays held while a question to the user is open (Q3), and the pipeline lets it go in `finally`, after its batch helper was closed (`withEnvironmentLock`, re-entrant: the Delete of the question about missing files takes no second lock). The automatic stops of the Session Monitor take the same lock without a wait (`flock -n`, D2 of plan step 8, PR B) and leave an environment whose lock is held to a later tick. (Before plan step 11I1, PR B1, the lock was an operation of its own, `lock`, that the extension held, with a backstop of 6 hours.)
- **The Session Monitor's commands** (plan steps 11D1 and 11D2; decision U2 of 2026-10-08). The worker runs the commands in the Session Monitor container of its engine as entries of the script registry over the Engine API (`src/core/worker/monitorFlow.ts`): a heartbeat (`monitorHeartbeat`, under the lock of the records), Delete's `forget` (`monitorForget`), and the image settings and the image list of an open (`monitorSettings`, `monitorImages`, their JSON on standard input); a monitor container that does not exist or does not run answers `missing`, and the window starts it again (`monitorEnsure`). The worker creates the container itself (`monitorEnsure`, and the open: `RemoteSessionMonitor.ensureOrThrow` over `engineMonitor`, from the worker's own image by its monitor tag, with the socket that the worker mounts and the script of its bundle; section 16, and the replace table of section 18).
- **Unreadable session folders** (plan step 5, PR D, rule D1 of 2026-09-30). `listJsonFiles` (`src/core/storage/atomicJson.ts`) gives an empty list only for a missing folder (ENOENT, or ENOTDIR); any other error (permissions, a disk error) is thrown, so `SessionFiles.readWindowStatuses`, `readPendings` and `readOperations` reject instead of reading as "nothing there", and so does one file in them that cannot be read (PR #76 review round 3, A-R3-2; a folder named `*.json` is skipped, and invalid JSON stays tolerant, as the files are written atomically). The check whether a kept connection cancels its hand-off reads only the operation file of its environment (`SessionFiles.readOperation`, PR #76 review round 4, A-R4-1; when that file cannot be read, the hand-off and its busy mark are kept), and the empty window reads the operation files one by one (`SessionFiles.readOperationsKnown`, PR #76 review round 5, A-R5-1: a file that cannot be read is not run and not dropped, and the user is warned; an operations folder that cannot be read runs nothing; in both cases the window is not opened again, since it is not known whether an operation is pending). The other-window checks count such a failure as not known: the pipeline (`otherWindowOf`) as before, and the controller (`markBlocker`, `otherActiveWindowsKnown`) refuses Stop, Rebuild, Delete and the other-window step of Start with `ControllerTexts.otherWindowsUnknown`.
- **Tests.** Unit tests of the protocol, the server, the client, the manager and the operations (`src/core/helperChannel/`, `src/helperChannel/`); `src/helperChannel/channel.e2e.test.ts` runs the real pipe loader and the bundled script in a Node.js process against a fake engine on a Unix socket (the probe over the Engine API, the end of the input, the silence, a script that does not match its hash); the flows of `src/core/worker/` with a fake engine and a fake extension side; the batch helper (`batchSteps.test.ts`, `batchScope.test.ts`, `batchHelper.test.ts`, `src/helperChannel/batch.test.ts`, and `batch.e2e.test.ts` with the bundled worker and helper); `lock.test.ts` (with real `flock` processes on Linux: a second holder gets 75, a SIGKILL of the holder frees the lock, a symbolic link is refused); `src/extensionBundle.test.ts` and `src/workerBundle.test.ts` (on esbuild's metafile: the extension's bundle holds no module of the pipeline, plan steps 11F1 and 11F2; the worker's bundle holds no module of the extension's helper image, plan step 11I, U7). The Docker tests (`test/docker/`, section 2) drive the flows end to end through real workers on the real engine: `helperChannel.test.ts` (the options of the container, an operation and its value, its end after a hard kill of `docker run` and after the silence), `workerBatch.test.ts` (a missing volume refused and not created; the Git user cannot reach the socket, the secrets or `CONFIG_FOLDER`; the token in no `docker inspect` field and no log; a step time limit keeps the session; Compose remote includes refused; the owner steps), `workerLock.test.ts` (two sets of workers: a busy second lock and the lock after a hard kill of the first worker; two Deletes with exactly one winner; a Start and a Delete, and a Delete during the question of a Start, refused as busy after 10 s), `workerFlows.test.ts`, `workerOpen.test.ts` and `workerRefresh.test.ts`, and the pipeline, Docker Compose and remote host tests, which check one batch helper per open and no worker or batch helper left over.
- **Live checks** (not covered by the tests): the worker on a real remote host (rootful and rootless), its time for an operation compared with a Docker call over SSH, and that its container is gone on the host after the computer went to sleep, after the network was switched off, and after VS Code was killed.

## 18. Pipe loading

Plan step 3 ([plan-remote-worker.md](plan-remote-worker.md), user decisions 2026-09-29): one mechanism fills our own containers with their code: the worker (the helper channel of section 17), its batch helpers, and the Session Monitor of each engine (section 16). No code goes into an argument or a variable of a container.

- **Loader** (`src/core/loader/pipeLoader.ts`, pure, no `vscode`). `PIPE_LOADER` is a one-line `node -e` program of Node.js built-ins; `loaderCommand({ path, hash, entry })` gives `node -e <loader> <path> <sha256> <entry>` as the command of the container. The arguments are checked (an absolute path, 64 lower-case hex digits, an entry name of letters); anything else exits 3.
- **Protocol.** The writer sends the bundle as the first line of the standard input (the extension the worker's bundle, `dist/helperChannel.js` of the `.vsix`; the worker its own stored script to each batch helper, and the script of the Session Monitor from its bundle to the monitor container): `encodeBundle` = the bundle as a JSON string and a line feed. It checks the length of that line before it writes (`MAX_BUNDLE_LINE_LENGTH`, 8 MiB, user decision 2026-09-29: a guard of the memory of the loader); the loader refuses a longer line too. The loader reads the line within 60 s (`LOADER_BUNDLE_TIMEOUT_MS`), parses it to a string, and checks its hash (`bundleHash`: sha256 over UTF-8). It stores the bundle atomically (`<path>.tmp`, then a rename; the folder is created), loads it, and calls its exported entry with the rest of the input that it read already; the standard input stays paused, with UTF-8 encoding, and belongs to the bundle from then on (the channel reads its messages from it). The bundle line and the input are never logged.
- **Hash.** The hash on the command line binds the container to one bundle: a broken or mixed-up transfer, or another bundle, is refused and nothing is stored. It is no defence against someone who already has the Docker socket of the engine: such a user can run anything there anyway.
- **Resume.** When the file at the path exists and has the hash of the command, the loader starts it without reading its input. So a container with a restart policy (the remote Session Monitor) goes on after `docker start`, `docker restart`, or a restart by its policy, with no extension involved. A stored file with another hash (changed in the container) is replaced by the bundle of the input only on the first start. Review round 1 of PR #69 (A-R1-1): before the loader reads its input, it creates the marker `<path>.started` (and the folder); a loader that finds the marker without a stored file of its hash was started before and never got its bundle, and exits 3 at once (`started before without its bundle`). Without the marker such a restart waited: the first, attached run (`docker run -i` sets StdinOnce) reads the end of its input when its client goes, but at every restart the daemon gives the container a new standard input without a writer that never ends, so the loader waited 60 s as `running`, exited 3, and was restarted (a run of 10 s or more resets Docker's back-off): almost always `running`, never its script. With the marker the container keeps exiting 3 at once (`restarting`, with a growing back-off) until ensure replaces it. The worker and its batch helpers run with `--rm` (AutoRemove) and are never restarted.
- **Exit 3** (`LOADER_EXIT_CODE`). Every failure of the loader writes one line `devenv loader: …` to stderr and exits with 3: invalid arguments, started before without its bundle (the marker, see *Resume*), the input ended before the bundle, no bundle within 60 s, a line that is too long, not a JSON string or another hash, the marker or the bundle cannot be stored, the bundle cannot be loaded, it has no such function, or its entry throws (review round 1 of PR #69, A-R1-3: `the entry failed: <message>`; before, Node.js exited 1 and printed the source line of the error, the whole one-line bundle). The writers log only the lines of 1 to 1000 characters of the end of stderr (`readableStderr`; at the cap of the tail its cut first line is dropped). Review round 2 of PR #69 (A-R2-3): a bundle has short lines too (template literals keep their line feeds), and an error thrown later, outside the entry (a timer, an event), is still uncaught: Node.js exits 1 and prints its source excerpt (a header `<file>:<line>`, the source line, and a caret line when the column is known). So `readableStderr` also drops that excerpt by its shape: a header (a line that starts with `/`, a drive letter and `\` or `/`, or `[eval`, and ends with `:<line>`, but is no stack position `:<line>:<column>`) is kept and the line after it dropped; a caret line (only `^`) is dropped, and when no header came right before its source line (the header was cut off at the cap), the kept line right above it is dropped too. The error name and message and the stack stay.
- **Replace table of the remote Session Monitor** (`RemoteSessionMonitor.ensure`; since plan step 11D2 it runs in the worker over the Engine API, `MonitorEngine` of `src/core/worker/engineMonitor.ts`, and `docker container inspect`, `docker rm -f`, `docker start`, `docker exec` and `docker info` in this table name the requests of the API that do the same; by the inspect of status, exit code, label, `RestartCount`, ID and `.Created`; review round 4 of PR #69 added `.Created`): first, a container that is `created` (of any label) may be the attached create of another window between its create and its start (one to several seconds over SSH, when the extension created it, before plan step 11D2), so ensure looks again while the same ID stays `created`, 12.75 s at most (`REMOTE_MONITOR_CREATED_WAITS_MS`; each look has the time limit of 60 s and the signal, and a cancellation ends a wait), and whatever it finds then (gone → create; `running` or `paused`, another status, or another ID) goes through the table below (review round 4 of PR #69, A-R4-1). Review round 5 of PR #69 (A-R5-2): when the name moved to another ID that is still `created` (another window removed an abandoned one and is between its own create and start), the waits start once more for that ID, once only (25.5 s at most in all, one budget of looks, the signal honoured); another ID after that goes through the table, and a `created` one is judged by its own age. Still `created` with the same ID → `docker rm -f <ID>`, then create, only when the daemon created it more than `REMOTE_MONITOR_STALE_CREATED_MS` (the Docker time limit of 60 s plus 30 s) ago on the clock of the daemon (`.Created` against the `SystemTime` of `docker info`, `MonitorEngine.daemonTime`, with the time limit and the signal; the clock of the computer does not count): every live create starts its container or gives up and removes it by its nonce within 60 s of its start, so only an abandoned one is older (its client died between the create and the start; a restart policy never applies to a container that never started). Younger, or an age that cannot be read (no `.Created`, Docker's zero time, a failed or unparsable `docker info`) → kept, a warning, `failed`: never replaced on uncertain evidence. Then: the matching label and `running` or `paused` with `RestartCount` 0, or `paused` with any `RestartCount` (Docker refuses `docker exec` in a paused container; review round 2 of PR #69, A-R2-1) → nothing; `running` with `RestartCount` > 0 (Docker restarted it by its policy) → `docker exec <name> sha256sum /opt/devenv/monitor.js` (plan step 11I, PR B: the entry `monitorScriptHash` of the script registry, section 6; review round 1 of PR #69, A-R1-1; `RestartCount` 0, the normal open and the create of another window that is still loading, costs no extra call): the hash of the script → nothing; definite evidence of another or no stored script (another hash of 64 lower-case hex digits; a failure with a line that starts with `sha256sum: …No such file or directory`, with `Error response from daemon: ` or `Error: ` and `No such container: ` or `container <id> is not running` / `is restarting`, or with `OCI runtime exec failed: exec failed: cannot exec in a stopped container` or the older `… cannot exec a container that has stopped`, also on the stdout of the exec, where the daemon writes it after the stream was hijacked (exit 126): a first load that was cut off, or a container that stopped between two restarts) → `docker rm -f`, then create; a check that fails otherwise (no answer in time, a failure of the engine or of the connection to it, a stored script that cannot be read such as `Permission denied`, an unexpected answer) → nothing, logged (review round 2 of PR #69, A-R2-2; review round 3, B-R3-1, A-R3-4, A-R3-5: each pattern is tied to the start of a line of its source, so a "No such file or directory" of the transport, for example an SSH warning about an identity file, is no evidence; known gap of plan step 8, fixed in fix/remote-monitor-restart-check: such a check, often cut off when the loader exits 3 at once between two restarts by the policy, is followed by an inspect by the same ID (`checkAgain`): `restarting`, `exited` with 3, or a RestartCount that grew → `docker rm -f <ID>`, then create; still `running` with the same RestartCount → one more `sha256sum` check, whose answer counts (`unknown` again → kept, logged); an inspect that fails, a missing container, another ID, or any other state → kept, logged, nothing removed); `exited` with another exit code than 3 (plan step 8, PR B: also the exit 0 of a monitor that was idle, Q5) → `docker start <ID>` (resume), then the same `sha256sum` check as for a restarted one (review round 4 of PR #69, A-R4-4: a first run that was killed after its marker but before its script was stored exits 3 again at once, and the restart policy loops it): the hash of the script → `started`; definite evidence of another or no stored script (the same patterns, for example `is restarting` or `is not running` of a container that exited again at once) → `docker rm -f <ID>`, then create; a check that fails otherwise → the same `checkAgain`, with 0 as the base RestartCount because `docker start` resets it (review round 1 of PR #83, B-R1-1), then kept, `started`, logged when it finds no evidence; a `docker start` that fails (also "No such container") fails; `restarting`, `dead`, `removing`, or `exited` with 3 → `docker rm -f`, then create; another label or none (not `created`) → `docker rm -f`, then create; missing → create. Every `docker rm -f` and `docker start` of this table takes the ID that inspect read (review round 2 of PR #69, A-R2-2); review round 4 of PR #69 (A-R4-5): never the name: when the ID cannot be read, nothing is removed or started and ensure fails with a warning (the container of the name may be that of another window by then). The `docker exec … sha256sum` check stays by the name (a read). A window never removes a container that another window created meanwhile; its `rm` gets "No such container" or, when another window removes the same container right then, "removal of container … is already in progress" (both tolerated; review round 3 of PR #69, A-R3-1), and its create may meet a name conflict. On a name conflict (review round 3 of PR #69, A-R3-1, A-R3-2) ensure inspects the name again: while the container there is `created` (the attached `docker run` of another window between its create and its start) or `removing`, it waits and looks again: review round 5 of PR #69 (A-R5-1): while it is `created` by the waits of the first look (`REMOTE_MONITOR_CREATED_WAITS_MS`, 12.75 s at most), while it is `removing` by the short ones (`REMOTE_MONITOR_CONFLICT_WAITS_MS`); one counter for both lists, never reset when the status changes, so at most as many waits as the longer list has; review round 6 of PR #69 (A-R6-1): only that budget ends the looks, a shorter list repeats its last wait (a container that stays `removing`: 7.75 s); a cancellation ends the wait; there is no age check and no removal here (inspect only); a container of the matching label that is `paused`, or `running` with `RestartCount` 0, is accepted; one that is `running` with `RestartCount` > 0 is accepted only when the `sha256sum` check finds the script of this version, otherwise the conflict fails (review round 4 of PR #69, A-R4-3; the next open's first look decides on evidence); when the name is free, the create is tried once more (once only); anything else (still `created` after the waits, another label, another status) fails with a warning. That loop never removes anything: the container is not ours. Review round 4 of PR #69 (A-R4-2): a name conflict is only the daemon's refusal of the name (plan step 11D2: the answer 409 to the create with the message `Conflict. The container name … is already in use`; before, that message of the daemon at the start of a line of the stderr of the CLI, with its exit code 125); and before the loop, the list of the containers with the label `nimblescape.devenv.monitor-create=<nonce>` (`MonitorEngine.idsWithLabel`, time limit and signal) must find no container of this create: one found means this create did make a container (no conflict), and a list that fails is no evidence; both remove by the nonce (best effort) and fail. A conflict that fails also removes by its nonce before the warning (nothing after a true conflict). Only a create of its own that fails is removed, by its nonce. Plan step 11D2: the wait of up to 5 s for the end of the killed CLI client before that list (`REMOTE_MONITOR_CLIENT_EXIT_WAIT_MS`, review round 4 of PR #69, A-R4-1) is gone; a create request that the time limit or the cancel cut off may still make its container after the removal (review round 1 of PR #100, A-L1), and such a `created` container is replaced as abandoned after `REMOTE_MONITOR_STALE_CREATED_MS`. The loader is part of both labels (`remoteMonitorLabelValue`, `channelLabelValue`), so a new loader replaces every monitor once; the container upgrade mechanisms stay as they were (the monitor label, `CHANNEL_PROTOCOL_VERSION`, the container version label; the local monitor protocol version went with the local Session Monitor, plan step 8, PR C).
- **Where.** The bundle lives in the container's own file system (`/opt/devenv/channel.js`, `/opt/devenv/monitor.js`, and since plan step 6, PR B, `/opt/devenv/batch.js` of a batch helper), so it always matches its container and goes with it. The per-step workspace helper scripts were not moved to the loader in this step (user decision 2026-09-29); plan step 7 removed the per-step helper runs.
